# Devin autonomous task list — Arora Qt 4.5 → Qt 6.11.3 migration

This file is the task queue for the Devin CLI task loop
(`.devin/taskloop.sh`). Each run of the loop picks the FIRST row whose
Status is `pending` or `in_progress` (crash-restart), works it to
completion, updates the row, commits, and stops.

Status values: `pending` | `in_progress` | `done` | `blocked`

Rules for the agent working this file:
- Mark the row `in_progress` before starting work, `done` only after
  building and verifying per the task's own verification hint.
- `blocked:<reason>` in Notes when genuinely stuck — never fake done.
- One task per run: after committing, STOP.
- Never revert unrelated working-tree changes.
- Append a one-line summary to `.devin/WORKLOG.md` when a task completes.
- Format: the table holds a one-line summary per row; the FULL spec
  lives in the `### <id>` section under `## Task details`. Keep table
  cells short — long text belongs in the details section.

Context: repo is Arora, a Qt WebKit-era browser (upstream dead ~2010),
being ported to Qt 6.12.0 + QtWebEngine. User-local Qt lives at
~/Qt/6.12.0/gcc_64 (qmake6 on PATH via ~/.local/bin). Env helper:
`source .devin/qt-env.sh`. ~21.5k LOC under src/, 28 files use
QtWebKit APIs, 62 use legacy patterns (QRegExp/foreach/SIGNAL macros).
QtWebKit DOES NOT EXIST in Qt6 — port to QtWebEngine. Qt5Compat module
IS installed for staged migration (QRegExp/QTextCodec bridges).
NO SUDO — everything user-local (~/Qt, ~/venvs, ~/.local).
Headless: QT_QPA_PLATFORM=offscreen (default in qt-env.sh) or xvfb-run.
Build: `cd src && qmake && make -j2` after sourcing qt-env.sh.
WebEngine runtime note: QtWebEngineProcess + resources ship inside
~/Qt/6.11.3/gcc_64/{libexec,resources} — the app must find them
(QtDeploy/qmake handles this on PATH-based installs).

| ADB02 | Optional engine swap: Brave's adblock-rust via C FFI — details below | done | 1 | rustup toolchain at ~/.cargo (rustc 1.99.0). New src/adblock/rust crate (adblock 0.13.3 + base64 + serde_json, staticlib): panic-safe C FFI engine_new/add_resource/check/cosmetic/free; use_resources replaces storage wholesale so registered stubs accumulate+replay (bug found via unit test). `qmake CONFIG+=adblock_rust` → -DARORA_ADBLOCK_RUST + links libarora_adblock_ffi.a; default build untouched. AdBlockRustEngine wrapper: ResourceType→webRequest type strings, IO-thread calls mutex-serialized; registers all stub names+aliases (new AdBlockResourceHandler::registrationNames, aliasTable hoisted) so $redirect resolves to data: URLs; rewritten_url → Redirect+new AdBlockDecision::redirectUrl (interceptor redirects literal URLs), unresolved names fall back through canonicalResourceName else plain block. AdBlockNetwork under flag: rebuildRules serializes enabled+supported rules via filter() ($badfilter passes through, rust resolves it), builds engine OUTSIDE the lock, swaps under write lock; match() delegates when engine loaded, matchNative() kept public for comparison. Cosmetics: rustCosmetic() → AdBlockPage::applyRustCosmetic (hide selectors → same :is() injection w/ :-abp-* translation, injected_script verbatim); empty engine or hostless document (about:blank — rust cannot evaluate, uBO injects nothing there either) → native fallback. Verified: cargo test 2/2; --adblock-rust-smoke PASS (13/13 rust==expected incl block/@@/type/party/domain/$redirect→data:/$removeparam rewrite + cosmetic hide; 13/13 native agreement); --adblock-smoke 4/4 under BOTH engines (rust engine blocked+excepted real navigations); quit/nam/history/cookie/bookmarks/search smokes pass both configs; default build unchanged (flag-free binary, rust-smoke SKIP). Gaps: procedural cosmetic actions (procedural_actions not exported by FFI — native engine still covers when active), scriptlet injection empty (only stub resources registered — no real uBO scriptlet library bundled), engine rebuild per rulesChanged (acceptable behind opt-in flag). Stays OFF by default — flag only. NOTE for C++ users of match(): under flag, native path still compiled (matchNative). |
| MIG10 | Autofill: autofillmanager/autofilldialog — details below | done | 1 | Kept Arora's own engine (Qt surfaces no autofill API on Linux) — storage/prompt/dialog/settings unchanged, capture+fill reimplemented in-process: new src/data/autofill.js (replaces parseForms.js) is injected by AutoFillManager::attachToPage from WebView::loadFinished — fills stored forms (JSON payload built C++-side, skips disabled/readOnly/hidden/reset/submit like the old per-element JS) and installs a document submit listener + HTMLFormElement.prototype.submit wrap (form.submit() bypasses the event). Submits report through a per-page AutoFillBridge registered as "aroraAutofill" on the existing QWebChannel; bridge tags the page's url at inject time and the manager drops reports whose JS-claimed location.href mismatches (blocks cross-origin credential overwrite via forged channel calls). Same policy kept: only password-bearing forms saved, dedup by stripped url, autocomplete=off fields dropped under ignoreAutoCompleteOff, Never/Not-now/Yes QMessageBox prompt, autofill.dat QDataStream format + AutoSaver unchanged (now BrowserPaths::dataFilePath). OTR profile pages are filled (old private-mode parity) but never captured (bridge disabled + hook not installed). AutoFillManager::instance() lazy qApp singleton (HistoryManager pattern); BrowserApplication::autoFillManager delegates, dead static + TODO(MIG15) noted like MIG06/07. Dialog: mechanical port (instance(), fn-ptr connects, reset→begin/endResetModel, fm.width→horizontalAdvance, model parented). Deltas vs WebKit: no POST visibility so JS-observed submits only — iframe forms uncaptured, submits before channel handshake (~ms after loadFinished) or racing immediate navigation can be lost; documented in ChangeLog. | SEC06 | Certificate-error UX gap: no QWebEngineCertificateError handling anywhere — details below | done | 3 | needs:task:SEC02 — WebPage::certificateError rejects the failed request and loads an interstitial (new src/htmls/certerror.html) served by a CertErrorSchemeHandler on the registered arora-cert-error: scheme — setHtml()/data: proved sandboxed (custom-scheme action links landed on about:blank#blocked and never reached acceptNavigationRequest). Per-error markup is published into a mutex-guarded nonce-keyed registry (64-entry FIFO bound) read on the IO thread; the page shows the symbolic error type + description and cert subject/issuer/validity/serial/SHA-256, all attacker-influenced fields escaped. Action links are nonce-checked in acceptNavigationRequest (forged/stale dropped silently): "Back to safety" (default/prominent) → history back or qrc startpage; "Proceed anyway" renders only when error.isOverridable() (HSTS-pinned etc. get no proceed) → inserts into a per-profile session-only whitelist keyed host / leaf-cert-sha256 → queued reload re-fires certificateError which auto-accepts. Nothing is persisted — a new session or a different profile prompts again. Subframe/subresource and concurrent main-frame errors are rejected silently; CertificateErrorDomain load failures skip the generic notfound page; the interstitial stays out of app-side history; a real navigation supersedes a pending decision. Verified: clean qmake6+make 0 warnings; new autotests/certerror 9/9 PASS offscreen against an in-process QSslServer with a self-signed cert (details shown + request parked, proceed loads real page, back returns to prior page/falls back to startpage, injected forged-proceed click ignored then real link works, same-profile second load auto-allowed without interstitial, different profile re-prompts, subframe denied without touching host page); top-level make check exit 0 |

| PERF02 | Adblock matcher scaling — details below | done | 0 | Token dispatch index in AdBlockNetwork, structure simpler than sketched and still exact: AdBlockRule::matchToken = longest ASCII literal run between *^ / metachars (every run char is a literal atom in the compiled regexp -> any match must contain it verbatim, casefold aside; /regex/, sub-4-char and non-ASCII-interrupted runs are unindexable). rebuildRules snapshots a per-subscription RuleIndex per list (exceptions/blocks/removeparam/exceptions): unique lowercase tokens hashed by packed first-4-chars -> token -> rule indices; unindexable rules land in the generic bucket; $document/$elemhide/$generichide/$genericblock split into a pageRules list scanned once per request against the DOCUMENT url (old loop mixed them into per-list scans). match() gathers generic + gram-hit candidates, verifies token containment via QString::contains, sorts ascending so original first-match-wins + exception-beats-block + $important + cross-subscription order are byte-identical; each candidate still runs full networkMatch (type/party/domain/denyallow re-verified). matchLinear() = the old O(n) scan kept as ground truth (public, documented test-only). Gotchas encoded: match-case rules stay indexed (lowered-token superset still holds); non-ASCII splits runs (percent-encoded URLs never carry them verbatim anyway); mid-pattern ' / ' treated as a run separator — safe since literal atoms still required. Measured on a 60k-rule EasyList-shaped file-corpus (mostly untyped /  / domain^, ~2% untokenizable) with a 120-request realistic mix at script type: linear 493-504ms/req -> indexed 0.9ms/req = ~550x (target was 10x; residual is the honest ~2% generic bucket). Verified: tst_adblocknetwork 16/16 PASS offscreen incl. new differential() (corpusRules x corpusUrls x 7 resourceTypes, every decision action/redirect/removeParams identical to matchLinear) + indexScales() benchmark; adblock subtree tests all pass; make check 47 programs 0 failed; --adblock-smoke/--quit-after-load exit 0 offscreen |

| FRZ01 | Event-loop blocking audit — details below | done | 0 | needs:task:MIG15 — Audit found zero QEventLoop/sync-runJavaScript sites (all runJavaScript calls are fire-and-forget or callback-driven) and all .exec() uses are intended modal UX (consent prompts already queued off WebEngine callbacks per SEC02/05/08). Three real blocking calls fixed: (1) SingleApplication::newConnection waited unbounded waitForReadyRead() on the GUI thread — a same-uid client connecting and never writing froze the whole browser for the full socket timeout, repeatably; the handoff is now driven off readyRead (readyRead→emit messageReceived→deleteLater, disconnected→deleteLater, 10s single-shot reaper for never-writing clients). (2) sendMessage's waitForBytesWritten + the getwinid reply write in browserapplication.cpp were unbounded — both bounded to 2s. (3) BookmarksManager::importBookmarks spawned htmlToXBel and blocked in waitForFinished(-1); HTML import now calls the shared bounded converter (tools/htmlToXBel/converter.cpp, pulled into src build via bookmarks.pri) in-process — also fixes a real packaging hole: the tool's .pro installs only its man page, never the binary, so the feature was dead in installed builds. Input file capped at 64 MiB. Vestigial qApp->processEvents() removed from AdBlockDialog::addCustomRule — addRule's rulesChanged resets the model synchronously already; the call only added a re-entrancy window. Verified: clean qmake6+make 0 warnings; make check end-to-end green (check-warnings PASS, all test programs 0 failed incl. new tst_singleapplication::stalledClientDoesNotBlockServer — connects, never writes, real client still serviced within 500ms); e2e: second `arora file:///tmp` forwards to running instance and exits rc=0, first instance stays responsive; --quit-after-load + --bookmarks-smoke PASS offscreen |

| PERF01 | Performance sanity — details below | done | 2 | Measured via new headless --perf-smoke flag (test-mode data paths): cold start 6.8s -> ~0.6s, first-window ~20ms, tab-open ~0.3ms avg, 40k-entry history load ~110ms, 5k-file perm sweep ~20ms. Root cause of the stall: AdBlockRule eagerly compiled a QRegularExpression per filter (~80k rules across cached lists = ~6.7s inside installOnProfile); regexp compilation is now deferred to first match through a shared lock-guarded pattern cache — per-object lazy state would race because snapshot rules are read on the WebEngine IO thread and GUI thread under read locks — and convertPatternToRegExp's five per-call regexes are static. HistoryParser readEntries appends then reverses instead of per-entry prepend (contiguous Qt6 QList made it quadratic). SEC12 profile-permission walk now runs once per profile per launch and only chmods entries whose permissions differ. | SECLVL | Mullvad-style security tiers — Standard / Safer / Safest selector in Settings (Standard = current behavior, the default): SAFER = JavaScript blocked on non-HTTPS pages via the request interceptor (drop script-type subresource requests on http: origins) + QWebEngineSettings::PlaybackRequiresUserGesture ON (click-to-play media); SAFEST = QWebEngineSettings::JavascriptEnabled off globally on the profile + auto-play denied + tighter Settings surface (the SEC05 broker already defaults deny — confirm tiers compose with it rather than fighting). Tier is a profile-wide persisted QSettings choice applied at profile setup; per-site JS allowlist via the existing SEC05 permission broker if it exposes one — else global only (honest scope). Verify: Safer loads https page scripts but drops http page scripts; Safest renders scriptless everywhere; toggling back restores; make check PASS. | done | 0 | privacy/securityLevel persisted tier (0/1/2), combo atop the Privacy settings page with per-tier hint; Safer+ drop script-execution subresource requests (Script/Worker/SharedWorker/ServiceWorker) on http first-party origins inside PrivacyRequestInterceptor (IO-thread snapshot via loadSettings; TorRequestInterceptor mirrors it for http .onion), exempting loopback as a Chromium secure context; Safer+ force PlaybackRequiresUserGesture, Safest forces JavascriptEnabled off profile-wide in BrowserProfile::applySettings after the websettings block so the tier wins and the stored enableJavascript value survives a downgrade; Enable Javascript checkbox greys under Safest. SEC05 composition: hard-deny pins + AskEveryTime unchanged, broker unaffected. Honest bounds: interceptor cannot reach inline <script>/handlers on http pages (documented in UI hint/README/ChangeLog); no per-site JS allowlist — SEC05 broker covers permission features only, JS is global-per-tier (JSCTL owns per-site). | CHAN01 | WebChannel execCallbacks JS errors — details below | done | 1 | done: root cause was transport contention, not message shape — qt.webChannelTransport exposes ONE onmessage slot per frame and every new QWebChannel() overwrites it, so the injected autofill client stole the start page's handshake responses (and vice versa) → execCallbacks[message.id] TypeErrors on every launch. Fix: new src/data/arora-channel.js bootstrap armed once per page at DocumentCreation (MainWorld, "arora:channel") installs a demultiplexer on the transport's onmessage via defineProperty — every client's handler stays in a rotation and each response is routed to the client whose execCallbacks accepts its id (init responses and orphan ids dropped quietly too) — plus one shared lazy client exposed as window.__aroraChannel used by autofill.js (connects only on a pending report) and startpage.html. Probed en route: setWebChannel(ch,worldId) REPLACES the page channel (one channel per page — multi-world channel split is impossible) and QWebEngineScript defaults to ApplicationWorld not MainWorld — the SEC16-armed autofill bundle ran in an isolated world where its form.submit() prototype wrap was invisible; replacePageScript now pins MainWorld. Verified: ./arora launch (startpage) + ./arora https://example.com produce ZERO execCallbacks/js errors offscreen; tst_webpage 41/41 incl new webChannelAutofillIsolation (page-side client + autofill report + live page invoke all coexist, page client keeps round-tripping, 0 console errors); tst_webview 11/11; make check all binaries build PASS |

| LEAK01 | WebRTC/DNS leak hardening verification — details below | done | 1 | Ordering premise stale — applyChromiumFlags already runs BEFORE the BrowserApplication ctor (main.cpp ~970); verified empirically anyway via new --webrtc-smoke/--webrtc-off-smoke differential: armed run (privacy/webrtcIpProtection pinned on pre-flag) emitted ZERO ICE candidates total under disable_non_proxied_udp — no IP literal, no mDNS, UDP transport fully disabled; unprotected control produced 1 mDNS-obfuscated candidate proving real gathering. Flag is process-global → normal/OTR/Tor profiles all covered; per-profile WebRTCPublicInterfacesOnly stays as second layer (all profiles get applySettings). Harness gotchas encoded: probe view must be SHOWN (hidden WebContents background-throttles timers — COV03 gotcha) and the server must consume the request before replying (close() on unread bytes = RST/ERR_CONNECTION_RESET). Both flags added to check-coverage SMOKE_FLAGS. DNS story: honest bound now in README — off/automatic DoH modes still resolve via the system resolver (lookups visible to local network); strict custom DoH (DOH01), the Tor window (SOCKS5 tunnels DNS), or a system VPN/DoH resolver are the answers. | SAFE07 | HTTPS-first false-downgrade bug — details below | done | 1 | Fixed after resuming a killed run: downgrade marks now require ConnectionErrorDomain (net -100..-199) minus ~30 excluded codes (resolver faults, proxy/SOCKS/tunnel plumbing, local-machine socket state, client-cert/pinning/CT enforcement — all prove nothing about origin TLS; aborts/vetoes land in InternalErrorDomain and never reach the gate, redirect-hop protection via m_requestedUrl match kept). | POL03 | Page-level polish batch — details below | done | 0 | (a) WebViewSearch::find now reports result.activeMatch()/numberOfMatches() as n/m — Qt 6.12's activeMatch is 1-based (verified empirically: fresh find returns 1, next returns 2 of 2 — the docs' 0-based claim is wrong for this build); zero matches keep 'Not Found'; emptying the field or unchecking highlight-all calls findText(QString()) to clear renderer highlights + the counter. No FindMatches/highlight flag exists in 6.12 — a normal find already highlights every match, so the toggle re-runs find (on) vs clears it (off). (b) Settings>Appearance 'Render pages in dark mode' → websettings/forceDarkMode → QWebEngineSettings::ForceDarkMode in BrowserProfile::applySettings — verified it auto-inverts a white page to #121212 under offscreen; it does NOT flip prefers-color-scheme (media query still follows OS theme — the Chromium switch for that is a different mechanism; auto-invert is what Qt exposes, so that is what ships). UI/palette untouched. (c) websettings/middleClickAutoscroll (default true, Q_OS_MACOS false) → applyChromiumFlags merges 'MiddleClickAutoscroll' into --enable-blink-features via new addListEntry() helper — comma-list flags (--enable-features/--enable-blink-features) merge entries rather than append a second switch occurrence that Chromium would silently shadow; dedupes + preserves user QTWEBENGINE_CHROMIUM_FLAGS. Native autoscroll chosen over a custom event-filter wheel hack because engine events are swallowed by the render focusProxy, synthetic wheels don't scroll, and JS scrollBy dies on JS-blocked pages; Blink's implementation knows link-vs-page targeting. Takes effect next launch (process-lifetime flag). | SRCH07 | Omnibox search broken — details below | done | 0 | user-tested — 'hup' went to https://hup; conf toggle got written back to false by the app — done: fallbackUrl() now degrades to the bundled DuckDuckGo engine then a hardcoded https://duckduckgo.com/?q= endpoint when engineForContext() yields null or an invalid template, so search-enabled input can never emit a bogus http://<term>. Diagnosis (a): urlloading/searchEngineFallback is only written by SettingsDialog::saveToSettings (settings.cpp:820, unconditional) and removed by resetSearchSettings (1103); the dialog is constructed per-open with loadFromSettings so a saved false is faithfully round-tripped on every OK — the observed write-back was the running app's dialog/cache holding the pre-patch false, not a second writer; live conf re-set to true while no app ran. | START01 | Start page (qrc:/startpage.html) loads forever — details below | done | 1 | Root cause already fixed by STALL01's isWebRequestScheme interceptor gate — the reported binary (post-REF01, pre-PING01) predated it: the start page's qrc subresources (qrc:///qtwebchannel/qwebchannel.js, qrc:/startpage.css, qrc:/arora.svg) crossed the adblock interceptor and widened uBO rules (a global $script redirect-rule whose arora-resource: stub re-entered the matcher) could stall them; the degeneration-across-relaunches shape matches rules persisting in the custom subscription. | ENG12 | Servo backend: crash reloading a tab (repro: reload empty Untitled tab) — details below | done | 1 | Resumed an interrupted run — impl was landed, verified + wrapped here. Root cause: libservo v0.7.0 Opts is a process-global OnceCell — a SECOND se_init (any later servo tab / swap-back-and-forward) panics "Already initialized" in components/config/opts.rs and the panic can't unwind across extern "C" → whole-process abort. Reload of an empty tab was only the reporter's trigger; the swap itself burned the one slot. Fix: process-wide ServoSlot Free→InUse→Exhausted under a QMutex in servobackend.cpp — ServoBackend::createPage refuses once claimed (addEngineTab/reloadTabInEngine/newTab then fail the whole engine tab and surface a status-bar message instead of manufacturing the abort); ServoView ctor double-checks at se_init, dtor spends the slot only if it held the claim, and a spent slot is never handed back (a failed se_init may already have consumed Opts). cbWake is mutex-gated on the live-view pointer so servo threads waking during se_destroy can't UAF the raw this. tabwidget.cpp surfaces "<engine> is not available for this tab". spikes/servo/servo-embed/src/lib.rs gains the upstream-side gate (INITIALIZED swap + catch_unwind over se_init_inner) for the next artifact build — the pinned .so predates it; Qt-side guard is the shipped fix. Verified: --servo-smoke PASS (empty-tab swap→servo, 10x reload + stop/back/forward/zoom/js on it, real load + real reload, swap back to Chromium, re-swap refused not aborted, direct second createPage refused), cargo check --offline green on servo-embed, --quit-after-load exit 0, make check rc=0 — check-warnings (no-rust; needed enginetab.h include lifted out of ARORA_RUSTCORE), supplychain, engine-audit, rustcoverage all PASS + 78 test programs 0 failed offscreen --disable-gpu. Merge fallout fixed en route: bookmarks ctx menu "Delete"→"Remove Entry" (SIDE04 label the merged test asserts) |

## Task details

Full task specifications, keyed by row id.

### BOOT01

Toolchain gate + smoke build: verify qmake6 = Qt 6.11.3 at ~/Qt, `.devin/qt-env.sh` works, then compile a minimal QtWebEngineWidgets hello-world offscreen (`QT_QPA_PLATFORM=offscreen`, load about:blank, exit 0) under /tmp — proves WebEngine actually runs headless on this box (sandbox helper, nss, gl). Write the smoke test to autotests/smoke/.

### MIG01

Build system: rewrite arora.pro + src/src.pro + src/src.pri for Qt6. `QT += core gui widgets network printsupport webenginewidgets webchannel core5compat` (add uitools if used). Drop webkittrunk.pri, Qt4 conditionals, QT_VERSION<0x050000 blocks. Get `main.cpp` + a stub window compiling+linking under Qt6 — the goal is a compiling skeleton, not a working browser; stub out webkit-dependent calls behind TODO markers listed in Notes so later tasks know the blast radius.

### MIG02

Core web layer: port webview.{h,cpp} + webpage.{h,cpp} + webactionmapper.{h,cpp} + utils/webpageproxy + utils/networkaccessmanagerproxy to QWebEngineView/QWebEnginePage + QAction mapping. Map QWebPage::WebAction→QWebEnginePage::WebAction, QWebSettings→QWebEngineSettings, signals (loadProgress/loadFinished/linkHovered/statusBarMessage). QWebFrame DOM APIs have no WebEngine equivalent — route through runJavaScript async; mark irreconcilable sync callers with TODO + list them in Notes.

### MIG03

Cookie jar + profile: port cookiejar/ + the app-wide profile wiring in browserapplication to QWebEngineProfile/QWebEngineCookieStore. Private browsing → offTheRecord() profile. Persistent cookies → profile setPersistentCookiesPolicy + storageName. Cookie exceptions/allow-lists → QWebEngineCookieStore filter or keep app-side list.

### MIG04

Network layer: networkaccessmanager + network/fileaccesshandler + network/* → Qt6 model. WebEngine does its own networking — replace fileaccesshandler with a registered QWebEngineUrlSchemeHandler for the app's custom scheme(s); strip QNAM interception of web loads (keep QNAM only where the app itself fetches: opensearch, favicon fetch if app-side, adblock lists).

### MIG05

Downloads: downloadmanager.{h,cpp} + downloaditem.ui + downloads.ui → QWebEngineDownloadRequest API (state machine: downloadRequested → save dialog → downloadProgress/downloadFinished). Keep the download list UI; sanitize download filenames (SEC01 audits deeper).

### MIG06

History: history/ models+managers → port to Qt6; QWebHistory integration becomes QWebEngineHistory / profile-side historyUrlSchemeHandler if needed. Keep the existing history model UI (tree/filter model) — only the webkit-facing edges change.

### MIG07

Bookmarks + model-driven menus: bookmarks/ + modelmenu + modeltoolbar → Qt6. Mostly QtGui→QtWidgets mechanical port; check QItemModel API drift (createIndex, sibling, QModelIndex lifetime) and Qt6 action/menu ownership.

### MIG08

Location bar + search: locationbar/, searchlineedit, searchbar, searchbutton, toolbarsearch, opensearch/ → Qt6 + WebEngine. Location-bar completion model, open-search engine parsing (XML — harden: SEC04 audits it), toolbar web-search integration via the webengine profile.

### MIG09

Adblock: adblock/ (adblockpage etc.) → QWebEngineUrlRequestInterceptor on the profile + keep the rule parser/network classes. WebEngine has no per-request page hook like WebKit had — all blocking goes through the interceptor; verify matching semantics preserved (substring/domain/regex rules).

### ADB01

Pump up the adblocker toward uBlock-Origin-level coverage, keeping the existing engine + UI: (a) upgrade adblockrule to modern ABP+uBO syntax — extended cosmetic selectors (:has, :style, procedural), !important redirects, $redirect=/redirect-rule= to bundled stub resources, denyallow, removeparam subset; (b) point defaults at LIVE lists (easylist.to mirrors + EasyPrivacy + uBlock-filters text endpoints) since the mozdev.org URL is dead; (c) multi-subscription verify: download a real EasyList under offscreen Qt and confirm rules load+match. STRETCH (same task, try it): once EXT01's extension manager works, load uBO MV2 unpacked and record which chrome.* APIs it demands — document gaps in Notes, don't chase full compat.

### ADB02

Optional engine swap: Brave's adblock-rust via C FFI — user-local Rust toolchain (rustup, no sudo), thin Qt wrapper, keeps Arora's manager/UI/subscriptions but delegates matching+scriptlet application to the Rust engine. Feature-flagged (ARORA_ADBLOCK_RUST) so the pure-C++ engine stays the default; compare coverage vs ADB01 on a test URL set before enabling by default.

### MIG10

Autofill: autofillmanager/autofilldialog — WebKit's autofill hooks don't exist in WebEngine. Options: use QWebEngine's own autofill (limited on Linux) or degrade to profile-persisted form data + keep the dialog for stored entries only. Decide, implement, document the feature delta in Notes + ChangeLog.

### MIG11

Settings/Preferences/ClearPrivateData: settings.{h,cpp,ui} + clearprivatedata + preferences dialogs → map each QWebSettings attribute to QWebEngineSettings::WebAttribute / QWebEngineProfile clear* APIs (clearHttpCache, clearAllVisitedLinks, cookie store). Settings without a WebEngine equivalent get removed from UI with a ChangeLog note, not silently dead.

### MIG12

View source + page search: sourceviewer/sourcehighlighter/plaintexteditsearch/webviewsearch → Qt6. Page find → QWebEnginePage::findText (async, no flags like HighlightAllOccurrences → wrap with settings.webAttribute). View-source → view-source: URL scheme or saveHtml callback; keep the syntax highlighter widget for the source view.

### MIG13

Remove ALL Flash/plugin machinery — user directive: modern browsers ship none. Delete src/qwebplugins/ entirely (webpluginfactory, arorawebplugin, clicktoflash/ incl. its .ui/.pri), the "Use ClickToFlash on flash plugins" checkbox in settings.ui, the corresponding QSettings key handling in settings.cpp, the qwebplugins.pri include in src.pri, and any translation strings. Verify: `grep -ri flash src tools` returns nothing but ChangeLog history. Also port remaining utils/ stragglers (networkaccessmanagerproxy handled in MIG04).

### MIG14

Chrome assembly: browsermainwindow + tabwidget + tabbar + useragent/ → Qt6. Port QTabWidget/QTabBar subclassing (paint/drag/tab semantics changed in Qt6), action plumbing via webactionmapper, menu/toolbar wiring. After this the browser should actually open windows and tabs offscreen.

### MIG15

App entry: browserapplication + main.cpp — single-instance via QLocalServer/QLocalSocket (QtSingleApplication/qtlocalpeer pattern), session restore, CLI parsing (Qt6 QCommandLineParser), translator loading, `qApp` init order for WebEngine (QtWebEngineQuick/QtWebEngine::initialize() before QApplication for widgets path — verify correct call for Qt6 WebEngineWidgets).

### MIG16

.ui files + locale/ + data/ + install rules: uic3-era .ui files → Qt6 uic compat (check custom-widget headers, Qt3Support leftovers), lupdate6 for translations, qmake install target for linux desktop file/icons/useragent data.

### MIG17

Deprecated-API sweep: QRegExp→QRegularExpression (Qt5Compat bridge allowed where risky), foreach/Q_FOREACH→range-for, SIGNAL()/SLOT() string macros→function pointers, qsrand/qrand→QRandomGenerator, QTextCodec→QStringConverter, QDesktopWidget/QApplication::desktop→QScreen, QString::null/SplitBehavior Qt5 names→Qt6. Goal: zero deprecated warnings on a clean Qt6 build.

### EXT01

Chrome-style extension support via QWebEngineExtensionManager (Qt 6.11.3 tech preview — verified present in ~/Qt headers): per-profile manager wiring, an Extensions page in Settings (load-unpacked, install, enable/disable, uninstall — the manager API covers all of these), extension state persisted in the profile dir, permission/user-consent handling where Qt surfaces it, and graceful messaging for unsupported chrome.* APIs. Chrome format chosen deliberately: WebEngine is Chromium — Firefox browser.* flavor would need reimplementation. Also wire QWebEngineScript/QWebEngineScriptCollection as the user-script path for what extensions can't cover.

### TOOL01

tools/ modernization — 3 Qt4-era utilities: (a) htmlToXBel uses QtWebKit+injected extract.js just to parse Netscape bookmark HTML — rewrite as a plain QXmlStreamReader parser, no browser engine needed; keep its tests/ fixtures (firefox3/opera9/ie7 html) passing; (b) cacheinfo wraps QNetworkDiskCache which does not exist in Qt6 (WebEngine cache is internal) — repurpose to report the profile's on-disk cache stats or drop it with a ChangeLog note; (c) placesimport (Firefox places.sqlite via QtSql) is portable — retarget it at the MIG06 history store. Port all .pro files to Qt6, keep tools/ in the top-level SUBDIRS, man pages in data/ stay.

### UA01

User-Agent modernization — user hit Google's /sorry/ bot-block page on a plain google.com search: the default profile UA is QtWebEngine's own (contains 'QtWebEngine/x.y' → flagged as automation). Fix: (a) default the profile UA to a current vanilla Chrome-on-Linux string matching the bundled Chromium version (QWebEngineProfile default minus the QtWebEngine token, or query Chromium version at runtime and compose); (b) refresh src/useragent/useragents.xml presets — it's a 2009 time capsule (Safari 4, IE 6-8, Opera 10 Presto, Googlebot) → current Chrome/Edge/Firefox/Safari/Android-iOS entries; (c) keep the useragentmenu/Settings UA override working. Verify: offscreen run loads google.com and a search completes without the /sorry/ redirect (or document if IP-reputation still trips it — then the UA is no longer the cause).

### TST01

Port autotests/ to Qt6: the qtest suite (adblock, cookiejar, autosaver, bookmarks, history, downloadmanager, opensearch...) compiles+runs under QT_QPA_PLATFORM=offscreen; wire a `make check` target the loop can call. Skip/xfail tests whose target module isn't ported yet rather than deleting them — unskip as MIG tasks land.

### COV01

Coverage harness: lcov is NOT installed — use clang source-based coverage (clang++ -fprofile-instr-generate -fcoverage-mapping + llvm-profdata-18 + llvm-cov-18, both at /usr/bin) or gcov+gcovr (gcovr via the aqt venv pip). Instrumented qmake variant, `make check-coverage` producing a total-% summary, baseline committed to .devin/COVERAGE.md. UI-only code (dialogs, painting) is expected to lag — measure honestly, gate on total.

### COV04

Coverage push to >=80% total: remaining logic + dialog model-level tests where feasible; extract testable logic out of UI classes rather than testing pixels. Gate: `make check-coverage` reports >=80% and the target is enforced in the check.

### SEC01

Downloads hardening: filename sanitization vs path traversal/mkdir escapes, executable-extension warning, MIME/extension mismatch, overwrite policy, partial-download cleanup. Download paths come from a hostile web — treat as untrusted input.

### SEC02

URL scheme + external protocol handling: audit which schemes the app accepts/registers (file://, javascript:, data:, custom arora: scheme), external-protocol confirmation dialogs, no silent shell-outs.

### SEC03

Credential/autofill storage: any passwords/cookies/autofill persisted at rest — plaintext? WebEngine stores cookies in its profile dir; document what's encrypted vs plaintext, encrypt or gate what the app itself writes.

### SEC04

Untrusted-input parsers: opensearch XML, adblock filter lists, bookmarks import (xbel?), history import — XXE on XML parsers (Qt XML defaults differ from libxml2), regex DoS on adblock rules, entity expansion.

### SEC05

Web permission handling gap: nothing in the tree handles QWebEnginePage::featurePermissionRequested / QWebEnginePermission — unhandled requests auto-deny silently, so sites asking for notifications/geolocation/camera/mic/clipboard just do nothing (and any permissive default would be worse). Implement a default-deny permission broker: consent prompt identifying origin + feature, per-site per-feature remember (persistent profile store, auditable list in Settings), media devices default deny, clipboard/midi/etc deny. Audit QWebEngineSettings flags that widen the surface (JavascriptCanAccessClipboard, ScreenCapture, LocalContentCanAccessRemoteUrls/FileUrls) — set only what's needed. Tests: offscreen WebEngine page requesting notification/geolocation gets denied by default, grant persists, revoke works; make check PASS.

### SEC06

Certificate-error UX gap: no QWebEngineCertificateError handling anywhere — a bad cert is a generic load failure with no reason shown and no override path. Add a cert-error interstitial page (QWebEnginePage::certificateError or error-page path): show cert chain/error detail, "back to safety" default, explicit per-host proceed for THIS SESSION ONLY (QWebEngineCertificateError::acceptCertificate scoped non-persistent — never write trust store), no proceed for name-mismatch-critical cases per Qt semantics. Reuse the existing error-page plumbing if SEC04/earlier work created any. Tests: self-signed/untrusted-cast cert gets interstitial with details, proceed loads once, re-navigation re-prompts; make check PASS.

### SEC07

Private-browsing verification: code paths reference private browsing (browsermainwindow/webpage privateBrowsing) but nobody verified it actually runs an off-the-record profile under WebEngine. Verify with tests: private window uses QWebEngineProfile::offTheRecord or a dedicated OTR profile — zero cookie writes to the default profile dir, zero history/autofill persistence, downloads not recorded in download model, adblock still applies (read-only rules), closing the window leaves no on-disk traces beyond what Qt guarantees. Fix whatever leaks (cookiejar/historymanager writes are the likely suspects — they're app-side stores that may not check OTR). Tests assert empty stores after a private session; make check PASS.

### SEC08

QWebChannel bridge exposure — the sharpest remaining surface. webpage.cpp registers three C++ objects on EVERY page's channel: aroraAutofill.submitForm (writes arbitrary form data into the autofill DB — any site can poison it), external.AddSearchProvider (any site silently registers search engines), arora.currentEngine() returns a LIVE QObject* giving JS access to every public slot of OpenSearchEngine. The comment claims safety because "only pages that load qwebchannel.js see them" — wrong: qwebchannel.js is a public file and the transport is injected into all frames, so any remote page bundling it reaches these objects. Fix: stop registering the channel on untrusted pages entirely (attach it only to internal qrc/arora- pages that need it) or gate every exposed slot behind an origin check + user consent; never hand out QObject* handles to web content; AddSearchProvider must prompt like a modern search-engine install. Tests: offscreen page loading qwebchannel.js and calling aroraAutofill.submitForm must NOT write to the autofill store; make check PASS.

### SEC09

External-handler launch + CLI URL sanitization. (a) downloadmanager.cpp:773 does QProcess::startDetached(program, url) for open-with handlers — audit where the program name comes from (must be explicit user choice, never content/settings-derived), validate the URL arg can't smuggle a different scheme or extra args. (b) main.cpp argv URL handling — `arora file:///etc/passwd` or `arora javascript:...` on the command line: confirm argv URLs go through the same sanitization/allowlist SEC02 built for drop/paste/IPC (they should funnel into one loadUrl gate, not a parallel path). (c) Single-instance socket perms: QLocalServer socket file must be user-only (0700 dir / 0600 socket) — any local user able to connect can push URLs into the running browser. Tests: crafted argv/scheme args rejected or safely handled; open-with only launches registered handler binaries; socket perms verified; make check PASS.

### SEC10

Rich-text injection via page-controlled strings: Qt::AutoText is the DEFAULT text format — any page title, downloaded filename, or suggestion string landing in a QLabel/QToolTip/notification auto-detects HTML and renders markup in chrome (tab tooltips, history labels, download items, dialogs). Audit every site where a web-controlled string reaches a widget: force Qt::PlainText or escape, with a helper (e.g. plainText(const QString&)) so it's not re-introduced. Check history menu/tooltips, download manager items, autofill dialog, bookmarks-from-page-title, location completer delegate. Tests: a page titled '<img src=x onerror=...>' shows literal text in tab tooltip/history, no markup rendered; make check PASS.

### SEC11

Search-suggestion privacy: opensearch suggestions stream every locationbar keystroke to the engine's suggest URL — on by default means every typed character (incl. typos of passwords, internal hostnames) leaves for Google before the user hits Enter. Verify current default; make suggestions opt-in (off by default), per-engine, clearly labeled in Settings; when off, suggestions data path must issue zero network requests (assert via a test NAM/proxy). Also audit Accept-Language header sent — should follow the accept-language dialog config, not leak the full system locale list. Tests: suggestions off -> zero outbound requests while typing; make check PASS.

### SEC12

Clear-private-data completeness: clearprivatedata.cpp wipes visited links, HTTP cache, cookies, download history — but localStorage/IndexedDB/service-worker/WebSQL data inside the WebEngine profile survives (clearHttpCache does NOT touch DOM storage — exactly what trackers use). Audit what QWebEngineProfile exposes for storage wiping (Qt6 has limited API — may need clearVisitedLinks + cookieStore->deleteAllCookies + filesystem wipe of the profile storage subdirs, or an injected-script clear) and make every checkbox in the clear-private-data dialog actually clear what it claims. Also verify profile dir/file permissions are user-only. Tests: seed localStorage + cookie via offscreen page, run the clear path, assert both gone on disk and in-profile; make check PASS.

### PERF02

Adblock matcher scaling: adblocksubscription.cpp iterates m_rules LINEARLY per network request — at EasyList scale (~60k rules) every image/script/css load walks the whole list. ADB01 widened syntax coverage but nobody indexed matching. Profile match cost per request on a realistic page (100+ requests, 60k-rule list), then index: domain-keyed hash for $domain rules, first-char/anchor buckets for domain-anchor and separator rules, regex rules in a separate small list — classic ABP matcher structure. Must keep exact-match correctness vs the linear path (differential-test: random requests, indexed == linear results). Verify: match latency drops >=10x at EasyList scale; existing adblock tests still PASS.

### FUZZ01

Fuzz harness for the non-web parsers (adblock rules, opensearch XML, bookmarks/xbel, history import): libFuzzer or AFL++ on the parser entry points, seed corpus + dictionary, crash corpus committed, fix what it finds.

### STAT01

Static-analysis sweep over the whole ported tree — the Abinova-style SAST pass. Available on this box: `g++ -fanalyzer` (GCC 13.3) and `clang++ --analyze` (LLVM 18). clang-tidy/cppcheck/scan-build are NOT installed and can't be (no sudo) — note in the report that they were unavailable. Triage every finding: real bug → fix it; false positive → suppress with a one-line justification; commit the analyzer output to .devin/STAT01-report.md.

### MEM01

Memory-leak hunt: LeakSanitizer (bundled with ASan — set detect_leaks=1 on the HARD01 sanitizer variant) + /snap/bin/valgrind over the autotest suite under offscreen. Maintain a suppression file for known-Qt/WebEngine upstream leaks (they're not ours to fix); fix everything in our code. Commit suppressions + a leak summary.

### QTUP01

Qt 6.12.0 compatibility check — user-requested upgrade: ~/Qt/6.12.0/gcc_64 is installed alongside 6.11.3 and .devin/qt-env.sh now points at 6.12.0. VERIFY the whole tree against it: clean `qmake -r` + full `make` with 0 new warnings (check-warnings gate), `make check` all test programs + smokes PASS offscreen, then a manual smoke pass — real page loads (https site, search, downloads dir listing, cert-error interstitial), WebEngine process spawn, session save/restore, settings dialog tabs. ### MEM02

MEM01 leak REPORT only — no fixing. What kills it: running the whole instrumented suite under LSan/valgrind inside one 2GB run. Bound hard: (a) enable ARORA_SANITIZE_LEAKS on the existing HARD01 sanitizer variant (detect_leaks=1), (b) run test binaries in BATCHES of a few per invocation — backgrounded, teeing LSan output to files, never the whole suite in one process; valgrind only on the 2-3 smallest tests (it's ~10x memory — full suite hopeless), (c) collect every report into .devin/MEM02-report.md: each entry tagged our-code vs Qt/WebEngine-upstream with the alloc stack, (d) seed the upstream suppression file (.lsan-suppressions or equivalent) from the Qt/WebEngine entries. If a batch is too heavy, drop to per-binary runs — never re-run the suite wholesale. Deliverable = committed report + suppression file.

### MEM03

MEM01 leak FIXES — gated on MEM02's report existing (.devin/MEM02-report.md): fix every our-code leak it lists (upstream entries stay suppressed — not ours). Bounded per fix: if a leak's fix grows beyond ~30 min, checkpoint progress into the report and leave in_progress rather than thrash. Verify: the instrumented suite (same batched runs MEM02 used) reports zero our-code leaks; suppressions + updated report committed; make check PASS.

### CPP01

C++ modernization sweep: this is Qt4-era C++03/early-11 code — bring it to C++17/20 practices: NULL→nullptr, missing override/final, =default/=delete special members, [[nodiscard]] on pure getters where it pays, raw owning pointers→std::unique_ptr/QScopedPointer where ownership is unambiguous (leave genuinely-shared ownership alone), foreach/Q_FOREACH→range-for (MIG17 does the compat sweep; this does the idiom sweep), const correctness, move semantics on value types. Rule: modernize idioms, don't re-architect.

### WRN01

Zero-warning build: -Wall -Wextra -Wdeprecated-warnings + Qt's own deprecation marks; eliminate every warning in src/ (errors too if any survive migration). Don't add -Werror to shipped config (packaging fragility) but keep a warnings=0 gate in `make check`.

Event-loop blocking audit: synchronous QProcess/QNAM/modal exec() on the GUI thread, unbounded loops in paint/layout paths, runJavaScript sync-wait misuse. Qt analog of the Abinova FRZ work — same methodology, headless reproduction.

Performance sanity: cold-start time, tab-open latency, large-history model load. Profile once with QT_QPA_PLATFORM=offscreen where possible; fix only pathological (accidental O(n^2), sync I/O on hot path).

Post-migration sweep: after the MIG tasks settle, git-grep for Qt4 leftovers (webkit includes, QtCore/4 compat paths, dead #if QT_VERSION blocks, files never compiled), remove dead code, verify no file remains that isn't built or audited.

### SESS01

Session restore end-to-end (user report: closing and reopening does not restore tabs). The machinery exists — startupBehavior combo (settings.ui value 2 -> restoreLastSession), saveSession/restoreLastSession in browserapplication.cpp, TabWidget::saveState/restoreState serializing tab URLs + WebEngine history (serializePageHistory was ported to QWebEngineHistory). Find why it doesn't actually restore: is the settings option exposed/defaults wrong, is saveSession never called on quit (aboutToQuit path), does restoreState fail silently on the version-2 stream, or do tabs get created but never load? ### TABS01

New "Tab Settings" page in the settings dialog (settings.ui is a QTabWidget of generic pages — add a dedicated tab named "Tab Settings"). ### TOR01

Tor daemon integration (open-source path, NOT Brave's implementation): QtWebEngine has no per-tab/per-profile proxy API (verified in 6.11.3 headers — QNetworkProxy::setApplicationProxy is process-global), so Tor must be a separate window/process like Brave's Private-Window-with-Tor model — which is also the privacy-correct design (mixing Tor + clearnet tabs in one profile leaks/correlates). This task = the daemon layer only: manage a `tor` child process — prefer a system tor binary (PATH / /usr/bin/tor), else a bundled Tor Expert Bundle binary (BSD-3-Clause, per-platform, in resources/ or shipped alongside); spawn with a private DataDirectory under the profile dir, SOCKS5 on 127.0.0.1:0 (auto-port), control via the tor control protocol or just lifecycle signals; health-check bootstrap completion before declaring Tor ready; kill on app exit; never run as root; no Brave code — our own launcher. Verify: spawned tor reaches bootstrap, SOCKS5 handshake works locally (test via a local echo through the proxy), daemon dies with the app; make check PASS.

### TOR02

"New Tor Window" feature on top of TOR01: File-menu action + optional toolbar entry launches a separate Arora process (`arora --tor`) that (a) QNetworkProxy::setApplicationProxy to the managed tor's SOCKS5 port — this is process-global which is WHY it's a separate process, (b) dedicated OTR-style "tor" profile (never persisted — cookies/history/autofill all memory-only), (c) distinct window accent so users can tell Tor from normal at a glance (Brave-purple equivalent but our own styling), (d) hardening: no extensions, no WebChannel bridge objects, normalized User-Agent, suggestions/telemetry off, HTTPS-Only-friendly. All tabs inside the Tor window share Tor — matching Brave's UX contract. Verify: tor window's traffic provably exits via SOCKS (test endpoint), normal windows unaffected, no state persisted; make check PASS.

### UA02

Google /sorry/ block follow-up — empirical probe + hardening (user still hit the bot-block after UA01 + cleared the stale saved userAgent override from Arora.conf which claimed macOS/Chrome141 on a Linux build — the UA-vs-platform mismatch was the likely trigger, but IP reputation from the loop's automated google.com hits may independently flag). ### PRIV01

Privacy hardening pass — user goal: 'secure browser for secure pages, minimize what an observer can see without VPN'. Implement in order of impact, all honest-bounded (QtWebEngine = Chromium underneath, cannot reach Tor-Browser-level fingerprint resistance — TOR01/02 is the answer for hiding traffic endpoints): (a) WebRTC IP leak — set QWebEngineSettings::WebRTCPublicInterfacesOnly on all profiles (and consider --force-webrtc-ip-handling-policy=disable_non_proxied_udp via QTWEBENGINE_CHROMIUM_FLAGS) — WebRTC can otherwise leak local+real IP even behind proxy/VPN; (b) HTTPS-First mode — QWebEngineUrlRequestInterceptor upgrades http:->https: navigations with a visible downgrade warning path; (c) DNS-over-HTTPS — attempt via QTWEBENGINE_CHROMIUM_FLAGS (Chromium DnsOverHttps feature / --force-fieldtrials) to hide lookups from ISP DNS snooping; if flags don't wire through WebEngine, document 'not exposed' honestly rather than fake it; (d) third-party cookie blocking via QWebEngineCookieStore::setCookieFilter — reject third-party cookies by default (Settings toggle, default ON); (e) clear-on-exit option — auto-run SEC12's complete wipe (incl. DOM storage) at quit when enabled; (f) referrer trimming — reduce cross-site referrer via Chromium flags if exposed. Each item: Settings toggle with honest description, defaults favor privacy. Verify: WebRTC leak probe (offscreen page enumerating interfaces sees only public), third-party cookie rejected, http->https upgrade observed, make check PASS; document in README what observers CAN still see (destination IPs, timing, fingerprint).

(b) TRACKER/ADBLOCK toggle — 'Block content on this site' on/off wired to injecting a @@ / done / host^ whitelist exception into the adblock engine (needs:task:ADB01's rule engine). (c) PERMISSIONS granted to this origin — read view of SEC05's permission broker grants (notifications/geo/etc) with revoke buttons. (d) HTTPS state indicator line (secure / insecure / cert warning) reading the page's security state. (e) 'Clear site data' button scoping SEC12's wipe to this origin. Panel = small QFrame popup anchored under the indicator, not a dialog; persists choices immediately (exceptions lists are already QSettings-backed). Also make the shield icon stateful: shown/normal when protections active, greyed when site whitelisted. Verify: popup opens/closes correctly offscreen, cookie tri-state writes the right exception, adblock toggle inserts/removes whitelist rule, make check PASS.

### SEC13

Autofill/credential store hardening — user direction: BUILT-IN ONLY, no OS keychain/libsecret/DPAPI/Keychain dependencies. Honest crypto note for the task agent: AES-256-GCM (current securestore impl via OpenSSL EVP) is already post-quantum-sufficient for at-rest data — Grover only gives quadratic speedup so 256-bit symmetric retains ~128-bit PQ security; NIST considers it fine. PQC (liboqs/ML-KEM) protects key EXCHANGE — no exchange happens on a local file, don't add it. The REAL weakness is key custody: securestore.key sits as a file beside the ciphertext. Built-in-only fix: (a) OPTIONAL master-passphrase mode — user sets a passphrase in Settings; the AES key is derived in-memory via Argon2id KDF (bundle the reference implementation — public-domain/CC0, pure C, cross-platform, or libsodium's argon2 if a bundled dep is already available — no OS calls), so NO key file exists on disk in that mode; ciphertext is decryptable only with the passphrase. Wrong passphrase = clean 'unlock failed' UX, no oracle leakage (GCM tag verifies). (b) Default mode unchanged (random key file, 0600 perms — document it only protects at-rest theft of ciphertext, not the key file). (c) Prompt to unlock at first credential access, cache derived key in RAM only (zeroize on exit/lock), 'Lock now' menu action. Verify: passphrase mode encrypts/decrypts roundtrip, tampered ciphertext rejected by GCM tag, wrong passphrase clean-fails, no key material in written files, make check PASS; same code path builds identically on Win/mac/Linux (no #ifdef OS crypto).

### PRIV02

Fingerprint-surface normalization (the implementable Mullvad knobs): (a) TIMEZONE — optional 'Report UTC timezone' toggle; implemented as TZ=UTC for the browser process when enabled (document it affects JS Date/Intl reads); (b) ACCEPT-LANGUAGE — normalize profile httpAcceptLanguage to a fixed value (e.g. 'en-US,en') instead of leaking the full system locale list (pairs with SEC11's Accept-Language audit — coordinate so they don't conflict); (c) USER-AGENT consistency check — UA01 already ships a vanilla-Chrome UA; verify nothing else leaks a more specific client identity (QWebEngineProfile default hints, navigator.platform/userAgentData reads). NOT in scope (documented honestly in notes): canvas/WebGL noise, hardwareConcurrency spoofing — not exposed by QtWebEngine. Verify: with toggles on, an offscreen page reading Intl timezone + navigator.languages sees the normalized values; make check PASS.

Zero-telemetry verification + kill-list (Mullvad-style — the cheap wins): audit what outbound connections Arora can make that aren't user-requested navigations: (a) adblock subscription auto-fetch on first run (EasyList download counts as unsolicited outbound — make it prompt or defer until user enables adblock); (b) opensearch suggest calls (already covered by SEC11 opt-in); (c) any update-check or phone-home (none expected — confirm); (d) WebEngine-internal traffic — Chromium components may still fetch (variations/field-trial seeds, component updater): strip via QTWEBENGINE_CHROMIUM_FLAGS (--disable-background-networking, --disable-component-update, --disable-domain-reliability, --no-first-run) and verify zero unsolicited connections on a clean launch (watch outbound fds/tcpdump-lite or a local proxy log for N seconds after idle start). Document whatever can't be silenced. Verify: clean-profile launch issues zero non-user-initiated connections in a 30s window; make check PASS.

### PERF03

Startup + menu responsiveness — user reports very slow startup AND very slow menu opening. Instrument FIRST (QElapsedTimer checkpoints in BrowserApplication startup + ModelMenu::aboutToShow, log to a --profile-startup flag or stderr; record before/after numbers in Notes/ChangeLog): (a) STARTUP lazy-init pass — candidates visible in code: BrowserApplication calls historyManager() unconditionally at end of startup (forces full history DB before window shows — defer to first use), OpenSearchManager::load() synchronously QFile-parses every engine XML in loadDirectory (defer or cache), bookmarks XBEL full parse, autofill load — make each lazily-init-on-first-use or move to a post-show queued slot (QTimer::singleShot(0) after window shows is the cheap fix where thread-safety is unclear); (b) MENU fix — ModelMenu::aboutToShow() calls createMenu() which rebuilds the ENTIRE menu from the model synchronously on every open — for history/bookmarks menus with thousands of entries that's O(n) per click: rebuild only when the model signals changes (connect to model's rowsInserted/etc to dirty a flag) rather than every show, and/or honor m_maxRows strictly + chunk long lists ('Show more' pseudo-item for the tail like Firefox history menus). Verify: before/after startup-to-window time + menu-open latency recorded in Notes; menu opens in <100ms with a populated history; make check PASS.

### DOH01

Custom DNS-over-HTTPS exploration — bounded, builds on PRIV01's finding (Chromium only auto-upgrades to DoH for its known-provider list; no custom endpoint exposed). Explore in order, stop at first that works: (a) Chromium's managed-policy DnsOverHttpsMode+DnsOverHttpsTemplates — check whether QTWEBENGINE_CHROMIUM_FLAGS or a Qt6.12 API can set equivalent feature/fieldtrial params (e.g. --enable-features=DnsOverHttps<...> with params, --force-fieldtrials); (b) local MIT-licensed DoH proxy on 127.0.0.1 high port — viable ONLY if the browser can be pointed at it (WebEngine exposes no DNS hook, so verify path (a) or system-level redirect first — a proxy Chromium can't reach is dead code, don't ship it); (c) if neither works, document the hard limit in README/ChangeLog and close. Related honest-bound item from the adblock-rust audit: CNAME uncloaking (trackers disguised as first-party via CNAME) needs DNS-response inspection — adblock-rust supports it but WebEngine exposes no DNS hook, so document it alongside the DoH outcome rather than faking support. If a custom endpoint IS reachable: Settings toggle + endpoint field (default a reputable resolver), verify via a test page showing resolver (e.g. dnsleak-style check or a local mock DoH server test), make check PASS.

### DVT01

Web Inspector dead — user report: Tools>Web Inspector does nothing. Root cause found: browsermainwindow.cpp:878 triggers QWebEnginePage::InspectElement but NO QWebEnginePage ever gets a devToolsPage set — Qt6 WebEngine has nowhere to render DevTools so the action silently no-ops (WebKit's built-in inspector had no such requirement). Fix: create a dedicated DevTools host — lazily-created top-level window (or dock — window is the classic inspector UX) containing a QWebEngineView; on InspectElement trigger, call currentPage->setDevToolsPage(devToolsView->page()) then triggerPageAction — devtools render in that window. Per-tab: devToolsPage is a per-page property — set it on the CURRENT page each invocation (tab switches must re-point it), and clear it when the devtools window closes. Profile: devtools page must use the SAME QWebEngineProfile as the inspected page (it inspects the page's own contents). Note webpage.cpp:402 already whitelists the devtools: scheme — verify SEC02's handler doesn't strip what the devtools view needs. Verify: Tools>Web Inspector opens a devtools window showing the current page's DOM offscreen (or a programmatic check that devToolsPage is set and the view loads), context-menu Inspect Element works the same, closing devtools is clean, make check PASS.

### SEC16

browseraudit fix pass — gated on SEC15's report: fix every APP-SIDE failure/warning it lists (bounded per fix like MEM03 — ~30min ceiling then checkpoint). THEN attempt APP-LAYER MITIGATIONS for engine-side items before writing them off — user direction: 'try to have solution on app side, we cannot do anything with the engine'. The workable app-layer levers, applied where they actually reach: (a) INTERCEPTOR ENFORCEMENT — the request interceptor sees request URL+type+firstPartyUrl even without response headers, so OUR stricter defaults can be enforced there regardless of page CSP: block ws: outright (wss-only policy), enforce https-only subrequests, block suspicious scheme/nav combos the tests flag; (b) ADDITIVE CSP INJECTION — meta-CSP or a page-level injected policy is cumulative-restrictive on top of header CSP (can't loosen, only tighten) — where a warning is 'policy X wasn't enforced', injecting our own stricter baseline via QWebEngineScript/SchemeHandler headers for arora-served content can close real gaps without engine access; (c) COOKIE-STORE FILTERING — QWebEngineCookieStore::FilterRequest sees firstPartyUrl + thirdParty flag; sandboxed-iframe cookie access may be distinguishable by context — attempt attribution, block if identifiable; (d) PERMISSION BROKER / JS-SURFACE — SEC05's broker + script injection can deny APIs that engine CSP mishandles. ONLY after a mitigation is proven unreachable (the API genuinely can't observe the surface — e.g. response headers we never see, sandbox attribution Chromium doesn't expose) does an item get the documented 'engine-side' entry — with the specific missing-API named, not a generic 'can't fix'. Re-run affected categories to confirm each fix/mitigation, update the report. Stretch goal: app-side column zero + engine-side column minimized to items with a named missing API. Verify: SEC15 report updated post-fix, every item resolved/mitigated/or carries a specific API-gap citation, make check PASS.

### CHAN01

WebChannel execCallbacks JS errors — recurring console noise since SEC08: 'Uncaught TypeError: channel.execCallbacks[message.id] is not a function' fires on every launch/page-load (~5x per start). The SEC08 rewrite hardened the bridge (token-gated autofill, qrc-only arora object) — the likely cause: the rewritten channel bootstrap publishes/responds in a message format the bundled qwebchannel.js doesn't expect (a response without a matching pending callback id — e.g. an unsolicited push or a reply keyed wrong), or objects arrive before callbacks register. Diagnose: which page/object emits it (startpage? every web page? arora-resource pages?), what message ids lack registered callbacks; fix the bootstrap/protocol mismatch OR if it's upstream qwebchannel.js noise triggered by our message shape, suppress/comment the offending send path. Verify: a clean load of the start page + an https page produces zero execCallbacks errors in console, channel features still work (engine switcher, autofill prompt), make check PASS.

### UA03

UA version-token bump — user report: sites nag 'out of date Chrome 140, latest is 155'. The UA is derived live from Qt's default minus the QtWebEngine token (browserprofile.cpp defaultHttpUserAgent), so it honestly reports the bundled Chromium — but the nag is cosmetic sniffing and the fix is presentation-only: rewrite the Chrome/<ver> token in the UA to a presented version. MUST be done consistently: (a) UA string AND QWebEngineClientHints brand list must AGREE — clientHints() exposes the real Chromium version ('Chromium' -> '140.x.y.z'); a UA saying 155 with hints saying 140 is itself a fingerprinting signal, worse than the nag. Check applyClientHints() (browserprofile.cpp ~107) — the brand map needs the same presented version; (b) make the presented version a constant/setting easy to bump (e.g. kPresentedChromeMajor = 155 or track latest-stable), NOT hardcoded in two places; (c) keep platform tokens accurate (Linux x86_64 etc.) — only the Chrome version number is presented; (d) honest note in ChangeLog: the engine is still Chromium 140 underneath — this answers version-sniffers, it doesn't add engine features. If a site feature-gates on the version and breaks, the setting is the escape hatch (WebPage::setUserAgent override already exists). Verify: the UA sent on the wire shows the bumped Chrome/ version, client hints match it exactly, no QtWebEngine token, make check PASS (any UA-sensitive tests updated).

### TLS01

Weak TLS cipher suites in the client advertisement — user paste from a howsmyssl-style client probe: Arora's ClientHello still advertises TLS_RSA_WITH_AES_128_GCM_SHA256 (0x009c), TLS_RSA_WITH_AES_256_GCM_SHA384 (0x009d), TLS_RSA_WITH_AES_128_CBC_SHA (0x002f), TLS_RSA_WITH_AES_256_CBC_SHA (0x0035) — all RSA key-exchange = NO forward secrecy — plus TLS_ECDHE_RSA_WITH_AES_128_CBC_SHA (0xc013) and _256_CBC_SHA (0xc014), CBC-mode weak. App-side lever exists: Chromium's --cipher-suite-blacklist=<comma hex list> switch is honored via QTWEBENGINE_CHROMIUM_FLAGS / the existing addFlag mechanism in browserprofile.cpp (PRIV01 already uses it). Task: blacklist those 6 suites (advertise only AEAD + ECDHE forward-secret suites: the 0x13xx TLS1.3 set + 0xc02b/c02f/c02c/c030/cca9/cca8). Considerations: verify the flag still works on the bundled Chromium (feature-check — if the switch was dropped, document and try the alternative surface); the blacklist is advertised-suites only, negotiated suites unaffected server-side; potential compat hit on ancient TLS1.0/1.1-only sites is acceptable per hardening default — but if the flag supports it, keep a QSettings escape (tlsStrictCiphers on by default). Verify: a local TLS probe (openssl s_server or python) enumerating the client's advertised suites shows the 6 gone; https sites still load normally; make check PASS.

### STALL01

CRITICAL — every page load hangs indefinitely on the live build. User report: 'basically nothing opens, just loads continuously' on the running instance (binary 11:45 = post-REF01 ce64a2d, PRE-PING01). WebEngine renderers spawn fine (per-tab renderer pids observed) — so the stall is in the REQUEST path, not rendering. Evidence chain: (a) it is progressively worse across relaunches of the SAME binary — startpage rendered at first launch, hung at second, all pages hung at third → a stateful/degenerating cause (lock, leaked resource, or IO-thread block), not deterministic code; (b) log shows the adblock interceptor rewriting a devtools:// internal script to arora-resource:/noop.js — the interceptor is touching traffic it shouldn't even see, which widens suspicion it can also stall real requests; (c) STRONG PRIME SUSPECT — QSettings on the IO thread: PrivacyRequestInterceptor::interceptRequest + adblock interceptor run on Chromium's IO thread; any QSettings read there (settings value(...) or the lock-guarded snapshot refresh) can block on file I/O/lock contention and, since the profile serializes requests through the interceptor, ONE stalled interception freezes every load in the browser — matches the 'first pages worked, then nothing' degeneration (works until the first contended settings sync). Also check AdBlockRustEngine's mutex: if the deferred warmup rebuild (PERF03) races a check(), the guard can serialize-request-starve every request — same symptom. REQUIREMENTS: (1) reproduce under offscreen — scripted load of 3+ real pages asserting loadFinished < 10s each, loop it 10x to catch the degeneration; (2) get thread stacks from a STALLED live instance (eu-stack -p <pid> or /proc/*/stack — gdb absent) before fixing — identify which thread is parked and on what; (3) fix the actual stall — if it's QSettings-on-IO-thread, hoist all settings reads into the GUI-thread snapshot pattern (loadSettings() under a mutex, atomic flag copies — the pattern PING01 already uses for blockPings); if it's the rust engine mutex, fix the rebuild-vs-check serialization; (4) interceptors must early-return untouched for devtools:/chrome:/arora-file:/arora-cert-error:/arora-resource:/qrc:/abp: schemes — audit every interceptor path; (5) IMPORTANT — the working tree holds UNCOMMITTED SAFE01 work-in-progress (~212 lines across interceptor/sitepanel/settings): do NOT revert or lose it — either commit it as explicit WIP first or work alongside it; it is not the cause (binary predates it) but it may contain related code paths. Verify: reproduction harness passes 10/10 consecutive full page loads; the stall mechanism is named in Notes (not 'works now'); make check PASS; commit+push — this gates every other task.

### SAFE01

HTTPS-Only strict mode — today httpsFirst silently upgrades http→https but a downgrade/redirect can still land on http, and http-first sites load insecurely without any warning. Add a strict mode (privacy/httpsOnly, default ON alongside httpsFirst): any main-frame navigation resolving to plain http gets intercepted and shown an in-app warning page (reuse the arora-cert-error/SEC06 interstitial mechanism — an internal arora: scheme page) with 'this site doesn't support HTTPS' + proceed-once / always-allow-this-host exception list (QSettings-backed, surfaced in SitePanel like other per-site rows). HSTS-style: exceptions remembered per host, subresource http on https pages already covered by Chromium mixed-content + SECLVL. Careful interplay: httpsFirst upgrade happens BEFORE this check (upgrade first, warn only if still http); localhost/LAN/.onion inside tor window exempt; redirects landing on http must also warn (interceptRequest covers each hop). Verify: http://neverssl.com → warning page; proceed works + remembers; https sites unaffected; redirects http→http warn; make check PASS.

### SAFE02

Insecure form-submission warning — Firefox/Chrome warn before credentials/data POST over plaintext http; Arora doesn't. In WebPage::acceptNavigationRequest on NavigationTypeFormSubmitted (and check NavigationTypeReload/Other where data posts): if the request URL is http (and not localhost/LAN/tor-window), show a confirm dialog ('You are sending this form over an insecure connection — the data is visible to anyone on the network' + Submit anyway / Cancel). One-shot-per-page confirmation, not per-POST nagging; pair naturally with SAFE01 — if httpsOnly already warns on the nav, the form warning shouldn't double-fire (form warning is for http pages the user excepted or pre-SAFE01 flows). Verify: http page with a form → POST triggers warning, cancel aborts, submit-once proceeds; https forms silent; make check PASS.

Verify: fake subdomain (paypal.com.evil.tld) shows evil.tld emphasized; a xn-- homograph domain renders as punycode; normal ascii domains unchanged; make check PASS.

### UIP06

Preferences overflows the screen with no way to scroll — user report (ESCALATED): the modal SettingsDialog opens taller than the screen and the bottom is unreachable — including the `buttonBox` (Ok/Cancel, settings.ui:18) which sits BELOW `pagesLayout` in the dialog's vertical layout, so the user cannot apply ANY setting (icon theme change confirmed blocked by this — they found the combo but can't reach OK). Structure: `pagesLayout` QHBoxLayout holds `pagesList` sidebar + `tabWidget` QStackedWidget, NO QScrollArea anywhere; a QStackedWidget's sizeHint is the MAX over all pages, so the tallest page (Privacy/Advanced — grown by PING01/POPUP01/SAFE01/REF01 rows) dictates dialog height; on small screens, scaled displays, or large fonts the modal exec() dialog opens taller than the screen and Qt won't shrink it below sizeHint. Fix — do BOTH: (a) wrap each stacked page in a QScrollArea (`setWidgetResizable(true)`, ScrollBarAsNeeded, `setFrameShape(QFrame::NoFrame)` so it doesn't add a border) — implement in settings.ui by wrapping the page widgets OR in code by iterating `tabWidget` children and re-parenting each page into a scroll area (code path is uniform and avoids a giant .ui diff — pick whichever is cleaner; the sidebar does NOT get a scroll area, it must stay fixed height); (b) CRITICAL for the reported bug: the `buttonBox` must ALWAYS be reachable — it stays pinned at the dialog bottom OUTSIDE any scroll area (it's in the dialog's outer VBox already — keep it there) AND cap the dialog height to `screen()->availableGeometry()` (~90% margin) in showEvent/resizeEvent or via maximumHeight so the dialog can never spawn taller than the screen; with (a) in place the pages scroll instead of pushing the button box off. Interactions: scroll areas must live on the page CONTENT, not the dialog shell — PREFS01 (pending) later embeds this widget in a tab and must inherit the scrolling; do not break the UIP03 pagesList <-> stacked index sync or the persisted 'currentTab' index; do not shrink page minimum sizes — scrolling is the answer, not squishing. Verify: force a small viewport (resize below content height or offscreen QScreen override) — every page scrolls to its bottom, OK/Cancel always visible+clickable, all controls reachable; normal-size screens show no scrollbar where content fits; all 9 pages intact, index sync works; make check PASS.

### SAFE04

Cheap interceptor privacy blocks — two resource-type toggles riding the existing privacy group: (a) privacy/blockRemoteFonts (default OFF — compat impact, opt-in hardening) → block ResourceTypeFontResource (remote fonts are a fingerprinting + tracking vector; local/system fonts still work); (b) privacy/blockPrefetch (default ON — prefetch loads leak requests to sites you never visit) → block ResourceTypePrefetch. Both are one-line blocks in PrivacyRequestInterceptor behind the lock-guarded snapshot pattern PING01/SECLVL use — implement after PING01 lands so the pattern is proven (or merge into it if it hasn't started). Verify: a test page requesting a Google font + link rel=prefetch shows both blocked when toggles on, font loads when its toggle off; make check PASS.

External-protocol launch confirmation — audit + gate what happens when web content triggers non-http(s) schemes (mailto:, tel:, sms:, intent:, vnc:, magnet:, custom app handlers). Today schemeaccesshandler only gates FETCHES; navigation to external schemes may hand straight to QDesktopServices/OS. Requirement: any navigation request whose scheme isn't http/https/arora:/data:/about:/qrc: must prompt before OS handoff ('Site wants to open <app> — Allow/Deny'), no silent launches. Check acceptNavigationRequest + QDesktopServices call sites. Verify: a test link for mailto/tel/custom-scheme prompts; deny does nothing; internal schemes unaffected; make check PASS.

### CONT01

Container profile core — Firefox-style isolated containers. Foundation task: extend BrowserProfile with a ContainerManager: N named persistent QWebEngineProfiles (one per container id), each with its own storage path (~/.local/share/arora/containers/<id>/ — gives isolated cookieStore/cache/localStorage/serviceWorkers automatically, same mechanism the private profile uses minus off-the-record). Container registry in QSettings: id/name/color(+icon?) list. API: createContainer(name,color), containers(), deleteContainer(id), profileFor(id) (lazy-creates on first use), default container = existing defaultProfile(). CRITICAL plumbing: every container profile must get the FULL default-profile treatment — PrivacyRequestInterceptor + adblock installed, profile settings applied, disk cache/web-engine settings wired (look at how BrowserApplication/the default profile is set up and factor it so containers reuse the same path). Verify: unit test — set a cookie via container A's profile, assert absent in container B and default; profiles persist across restart; make check PASS.

### CONT02

Per-tab container assignment + tab UI — each WebView/tab binds to a container profile. UI: tab context-menu 'Reopen in container ▸' + File/'New Container Tab' menu (Firefox pattern — submenu listing containers + 'Manage…'); tab strip shows a colored top-border/side-bar indicator in the container's color + container name chip when not default. New tabs default to the current tab's container (child-tab inheritance like Firefox). The tab's WebView must be constructed against the container's QWebEngineProfile — check how WebPage/WebView get their profile today (BrowserApplication profile() plumbing) and add a profile param through that path; reopen-in-container = open new tab on the target profile + close old (carry scroll/history where cheap). Session restore must record each tab's container id (extend the session format backward-compatibly). Verify: two tabs different containers → different cookies live (test: login-state isolation on a test page); reopen-in-container works; colored indicators show; session restore keeps container binding; make check PASS.

### CONT03

Container management settings UI — a 'Containers' page/section in Settings (the vertical-tabs settings once that lands, else a Privacy-adjacent group): list existing containers (name+color chip), add (name + color picker — small fixed palette is fine, Firefox has ~8), rename, delete (warn: 'deletes all site data in this container — cookies, logins, storage'), and 'no containers' empty state hiding the feature cleanly. Deletion must remove the profile's storage dir. Verify: create/rename/delete round-trips, deleted container's storage gone, UI consistent with settings conventions, make check PASS.

### CONT04

Site→container rules — 'Always open this site in <container>' (Firefox's marquee container feature): (a) QSettings-backed host→container rule list, settable from the tab context menu ('Always Open in This Container') and removable from the containers settings page (rule list per container); (b) enforcement at navigation: WebPage::acceptNavigationRequest (main-frame only) — if target host has a rule for a DIFFERENT container than the current tab's, don't load here — open the URL in a tab on the right container and abort; (c) the reverse prompt is optional (Firefox asks 'open in assigned?' — the always-reopen behavior is the MVP). Edge loops to handle: subdomain matching (rule on example.com covers *.example.com), redirects, about/data URLs exempt. Verify: assign host to container → typing its URL in a default tab reopens it containerized; unassign restores; redirect chains land in the right container; make check PASS.

### CONT05

Container isolation audit + edge hardening — the 'prove it' pass after CONT01-04: (a) verify NO cross-container leaks — cookies, localStorage/IndexedDB, cache, service workers, HTTP auth, TLS session resumption (document which QtWebEngine shares per-process vs per-profile; any shared-by-engine item documented honestly, e.g. if DNS/socket pools are process-global note it), (b) shared-surface decisions: downloads/history are app-level (sqlite) — tag history entries with container id? (decide: simplest honest = history stays global, document it); autofill/password store shared by design (document); (c) containers inside private windows = forbidden (private profile stays singular — assert); tor window keeps its own profile, containers don't apply there; (d) verify interceptor/adblock/settings propagation onto every container profile actually works (the CONT01 wiring — test a blocked domain in a container tab).
