### EXT02

Extensions management UI — ExtensionManager (EXT01) has the full API (load/install/remove/setEnabled, manifest inspection with unsupported/unverified permission lists) but ZERO user-facing surface. Build it: (a) extensions page in Settings or a dedicated dialog — list installed extensions (icon/name/version/state), per-extension enable/disable switch → setExtensionEnabled, Remove → removeExtension w/ confirm; (b) INSTALL FLOW — 'Install extension…' picking a dir or .zip, then a PERMISSION-REVIEW screen before install completes: manifest name/version + requested permissions humanized ('Read and change all your data on all websites' style), unsupported permissions flagged as 'won't work', unverified as 'partially supported'; Require explicit consent — no silent installs. (c) show install source path + last-loaded state; errors surface via errorOccurred. Keep the toolbar-action popup question honest: if QtWebEngine can't host browser-action popups, extensions relying on them get a note in the review screen rather than a broken button. Verify: install→consent→listed; toggle persists across restart; remove deletes; a hostile-permission manifest shows the warnings; make check PASS incl extension-smoke.

### EXT03

Extension update mechanism — Chrome-Web-Store auto-update does NOT exist in QtWebEngine (no CRX3 signing verification, no store client) — document that honestly. What IS buildable: manifest update_url polling — a manual 'Check for updates' button on the extensions page + opt-in periodic auto-check (default OFF — silent code updates are a supply-chain risk; user opt-in only): fetch the update manifest XML from the extension's update_url, compare versions, and either download+install the package through installExtension or at minimum notify 'update available, install manually' with the fetched .crx/.zip saved for inspection. Version compare must handle the dotted-quad scheme; network failures non-fatal. Scope honest bound: works only for extensions declaring update_url with Qt-acceptable packages; Web-Store-only extensions can't self-update — label them 'no update source'. Verify: a local HTTP server serving an update manifest + .zip → update detected and installed/flagged; no update_url → labeled; make check PASS.

Verify: OTR smoke result recorded; if supported, private window with an allowed extension shows it active + a non-allowed one absent; tor window extension-free regardless; make check PASS.

Sleeping tabs — suspend inactive tabs to reclaim memory. QWebEnginePage::setLifecycleState supports Discarded/Frozen (Qt6) — cross-platform, no platform code. Design: settings option 'Suspend tabs after N minutes idle' (default off — opt-in) + per-tab context 'Sleep tab'/'Wake'; a sleeping tab unloads its WebEnginePage content but keeps its tab slot + title + favicon (dimmed/zZ badge like Edge); click activates → reload/restore (scroll pos + form state if cheap via history state; document what's preserved). ### SAFE07

HTTPS-first false-downgrade bug — user repro on the live build: typing 'github.com' shows the 'This site does not support HTTPS' interstitial for http://github.com instead of silently upgrading. Root cause identified (verify before fixing): webpage.cpp ~981 noteNavigationFailure(errorUrl) records a host into s_downgradedHosts on ANY main-frame load failure — ERR_ABORTED (redirect chains, user Stop), ERR_BLOCKED_BY_CLIENT (adblock/privacy vetoes), transient network errors ALL poison the host for the session; and s_downgradedHosts is a process-global static shared across normal/private/Tor profiles, so a failure inside a Tor or private window marks the host downgraded in normal windows too. Fixes: (a) scope the downgrade marking by error type — only TLS/connectivity failures prove 'no https' (ERR_SSL_*, ERR_CONNECTION_REFUSED/TIMED_OUT/RESET, handshake-family errors); NEVER mark on ERR_ABORTED, ERR_BLOCKED_*, or interceptor vetoes; (b) scope the set per-profile (normal/private/Tor each get their own — or skip marking entirely under Tor/proxied contexts since failures there say nothing about the site's TLS support); (c) while in there: audit whether redirect-chain aborts reach this path (a host mid-redirect must not be marked), and give downgrades a re-probe path (clearDowngradedHosts exists — consider a bounded TTL or clear-on- manual-retry so a transient blip isn't a session sentence). Verify: typed 'github.com' -> https://github.com/ silently (no interstitial); force one transient https failure then retry http -> still upgrades (host not poisoned); a failure in a Tor window does NOT mark the host for normal windows; a genuinely http-only host still warns; make check PASS.

Verify: find shows n/m + highlights; dark toggle visibly inverts a test page; autoscroll works on X11/Wayland offscreen-checkable paths; make check PASS.

### PING02

Ping-spotter residual verification + Reporting-API coverage — user directive: if ping-spotter still leaks after PING01, fix it. PING01 blocks ResourceTypePing + ResourceTypeCspReport and disables HyperlinkAuditing — but there's a real residual vector: modern CSP reporting uses the Reporting API ('report-to'/'Report-To' directives → reports_to endpoints), which may arrive at the interceptor as a DIFFERENT resource type than ResourceTypeCspReport (or via a Chromium-internal path the interceptor never sees — ping-spotter's CSP test may use either). Task: (a) re-run https://armin.dev/apps/ping-spotter/ against the PING01 build — record all three outcomes; (b) if the CSP vector still leaks: determine the actual request path (interceptor log vs a local reporting endpoint — set up a local report collector and fire a violating page with both report-uri and report-to headers to see what arrives and as what resource type), then extend the block to cover Reporting-API deliveries (whatever resource type/path they use); if the interceptor can't see them at all, document the named-API-gap and evaluate the next lever (CSP header rewrite for arora-injected policies only, or a Chromium flag if one exists for reporting); (c) <a ping> + beacon must stay dead — regression-check. Verify: ping-spotter shows all three blocked; local collector receives zero reports through both directives; make check PASS.

### SRCH07

Omnibox search broken — user repro on the live build: typing 'hup' in the URL bar navigated to https://hup/ (ERR_NAME_NOT_RESOLVED error page) instead of searching. Expected path per tabwidget.cpp guessUrlFromString: 'hup' has no space but looksLikeAddress() correctly rejects it (no dot, not localhost, no :port) -> fallbackUrl() -> search through engineForContext. Forensics already done: (a) ~/.config/Arora/Arora.conf had urlloading/searchEngineFallback=false — patched to true, but the RUNNING app wrote it back to false on its next QSettings sync, meaning the in-memory value stayed false OR something re-set it — only writer found is settings.cpp:664 (dialog saveAll); find out why the value did not stick and whether the Settings dialog is the only path that can flip it (is there another setValue, or does QSettings write a stale cached image?). (b) Separately: even when the toggle is ON, if engineForContext() returns null (engine list failed to load) fallbackUrl silently emits http://<words> which HTTPS-first upgrades to https://<words> -> DNS error — the silent-bogus-navigation path should degrade visibly (e.g. error page saying no search engine configured, or hard fallback to https://duckduckgo.com/?q=). (c) Also verify keyword-search and multi-word input still route correctly post-fix. Reproduce first: add a --search-guess-smoke harness asserting guessUrlFromString('hup') -> duckduckgo search URL, 'docs.qt.io' -> https://docs.qt.io/, 'localhost:8080' -> address, 'two words' -> search. Verify: smoke passes; manual 'hup' searches; the conf key persists across restart; make check PASS.

### START01

Start page (qrc:/startpage.html) loads forever — user report: progress spinner spins, page never completes on the 11:45 build (post-REF01, pre-PING01). Same binary rendered it fine at first launch, then hung on relaunch -> flaky/racy, not deterministic. Suspects in order: (a) CHANNEL RACE — webpage.cpp registers the 'arora' object on urlChanged-to-qrc, and startpage.html's init() connects via window.__aroraChannel (CHAN01 bootstrap at DocumentCreation); if the channel handshake resolves before urlChanged's registerObject lands, channel.objects.arora is undefined -> update() no-ops -> blank page; if the handshake itself stalls, init never returns. Instrument to find which. (b) STALLED SUBRESOURCE — startpage.html pulls qrc:/startpage.css, qrc:///qtwebchannel/qwebchannel.js, qrc:/arora.svg; if the interceptor or the qrc scheme path stalls a request instead of failing it, loadFinished never fires -> infinite spinner. Check whether PrivacyRequestInterceptor (REF01 policy injection + PING01 landing) touches qrc requests and whether all paths call block()/redirect()/return. Reproduce under xvfb/offscreen in a loop (load qrc:/startpage.html N times, assert loadFinished<5s each — a flake harness). Fix the actual stall/race; add the N-times regression check. Verify: 50/50 consecutive start-page loads complete; search box works (channel object resolves) on every load; make check PASS.

### HIST01

History site icons never show — user report: history entries show only the generic icon. The wiring LOOKS done (webpage.cpp:355 iconChanged -> HistoryManager::setIcon for non-OTR pages, HistoryManager::icon falls back to :graphics/defaulticon.png), so find the real break: suspects (a) m_icons is an in-memory QHash with no persistence — after restart EVERY entry is generic; (b) QWebEnginePage::iconChanged may not deliver favicons in Qt 6.12 without the page actually settling / may deliver empty icon; (c) icon lookup keyed by full URL while entries are keyed differently. Fix: persist favicons keyed by host (a small icon store like the old WebKit IconDatabase — files under the data dir, e.g. icons/<host>.png, loaded lazily), backfill history model icons for existing entries, verify iconChanged actually fires on Qt 6.12 (if not, fetch iconUrl via the profile's cookie-aware path or the favicon manager). Verify: visit a site -> history shows its favicon; RESTART -> icon persists; history menu + history dialog + completer all show real icons; make check PASS.

### PTAB01

New Private Tab — user request: REPLACE the app-global 'Private Browsing' checkbox action with a 'New Private Tab' menu item placed directly under 'New Tab' in the File menu. Design: private browsing today is window-scoped (BrowserApplication::isPrivate -> OTR profile per window). A private TAB means a WebView on the off-the-record profile inside a normal window: newTab() currently hardcodes BrowserApplication::webEngineProfile() — add a private-tab path constructing WebView(QWebEngineProfile::offTheRecord or the app's OTR profile). Requirements: (a) OTR page in the shared window (history/cookie writes already gated on profile()->isOffTheRecord — verify each site that checks it: history add, icon store, autofill, cookie jar, disk cache — must all exclude the OTR page); (b) visual marker on the tab (e.g. 'Private' badge/mask icon + the existing private.png indicator in the location bar must reflect per-TAB state, not window-global); (c) the 'Private Browsing' checkbox in File menu is removed/replaced (window-global toggle stays internally for full-private windows but the user-facing entry becomes New Private Tab); (d) keyboard shortcut (Ctrl+Shift+P or similar) consistent with other browsers. Verify: private tab leaves no history entry/cookie after close while sibling normal tabs still record; marker visible; menu order New Tab -> New Private Tab; make check PASS.

### PREFS01

### TOR03

Tor window: remove the dedicated search box — user report: in a Tor window the nav-bar search widget shows as a small collapsed sliver (button mode, the showSearchBox default-off path) and they want it gone entirely. `BrowserMainWindow::setupNavigationBar` always constructs `m_toolbarSearch` into `m_navigationSplitter` (browsermainwindow.cpp ~1128) next to `locationBarStack()`. Gate it on `!BrowserApplication::isTorMode()`: in tor mode the widget must be fully hidden and the location bar take the whole splitter width — Tor windows keep omnibox search via the location bar (SRCH01 covers the same job; a separate engine box in a Tor window is also a privacy wart — divergent-engine leakage + wasted width). Mind the dereference sites before choosing hide-vs-skip-construction: `applySearchBoxVisibility()` (~1160), the Ctrl+K/focus path (~1556), `toolbarSearch()` accessor (~1592), and browserapplication.cpp:737 which qobject_casts toolbar widgets into ToolbarSearch for search-history sync — prefer hide-only (`setVisible(false)` + splitter sizing) unless audit shows all uses null-safe; skipping construction is also acceptable if every site is guarded. Ctrl+K in a tor window should focus the location bar or no-op, never crash on a hidden/absent widget. Non-tor windows: byte-identical behavior (button mode + opt-in field both still work). Verify: `--tor-window-smoke` PASS; tor window has no search widget (widget-tree dump or offscreen check); normal window unchanged; make check PASS.

### TOR04

Tor circuit/hops on the status bar — user request: a Tor window's bottom status bar should show how the traffic is connected — the circuit hops (fallback: surface it in the site shield panel instead). Feasible via existing plumbing: `TorManager` owns a `TorControl` client on the daemon's ControlPort (tor/tormanager.h, tor/torcontrol.h) — `getInfo("circuit-status")` returns `250+circuit-status=` lines `<CircID> <Status> <$FP~Nick,$FP~Nick,...> ...` where the Path column is the hop chain (guard -> middle -> exit); `getInfo("stream-status")` maps `StreamID -> CircID + Target` so the display can prefer the circuit carrying the current tab's streams (fallback: newest BUILT PURPOSE=GENERAL circuit); `setEvents({"CIRC"})` or a poll-on-tab-change drives live updates. Spec: (a) TorManager grows a circuit surface — e.g. `requestCircuitInfo()` + `circuitsChanged` signal emitting a parsed list of {circId, status, hops:[{fingerprint, nickname}]}; TorControl is already single-connection serialized so queries queue fine; do NOT open a second control connection. Optional per-hop country suffix via `ns/id/<fp>` -> `ip-to-country/<ip>` ONLY if cheap — no consensus/ns/all download. (b) BrowserMainWindow: tor-mode-only permanent status-bar widget via `statusBar()->addPermanentWidget` (same pattern as m_loadingIndicator/m_zoomControl ~line 237) — compact chain text like `Tor: <guard> -> <middle> -> <exit>`; states: `Tor: connecting...` while Bootstrapping, `Tor: no circuit` pre-stream, error text on Failed; updates on circuitsChanged + tab change. Keep it a plain QLabel — status-bar real estate is limited; tooltip or SitePanel row can carry fingerprints/countries. (c) FALLBACK per user: if the status-bar surface proves infeasible (e.g. control connection can't serve circuit queries), put a read-only 'Tor circuit' row inside SitePanel's tor section instead (src/locationbar/sitepanel.cpp) — status bar is the target, shield only if blocked. (d) no widget/row in non-tor windows. Verify: `--tor-window-smoke` PASS; circuit chain rendered from a real daemon or a stubbed circuit-status fixture (unit-test the parser on captured 250+ reply text); updates on CIRC events/tab switch; nothing added in normal windows; make check PASS.

### ICONS02

Preferences dialog icons uneven — user report: the settings sidebar icons are not aligned, some render smaller some bigger. Cause is identifiable: the UIP03 Vivaldi-style `pagesList` QListWidget (settings.ui ~line 30, icons set in settings.cpp ~128-144 via `AroraIcon::get` -> `QIcon::fromTheme`) never sets `iconSize`, so every item paints at whatever size the active theme yields for that name. The pageIcons names (go-home, edit-find, zoom-original, security-high, tab-new, go-next, user-bookmarks, system-run, list-add) resolve at different nominal sizes across themes — worst under the "Native" theme where the system set has mixed 16/22/24/32 assets, and when a name is missing and falls back to arora-legacy (Size=16) it lands at yet another size -> uneven icon column + label baseline drift. Fix: (a) set a fixed `pagesList->setIconSize(...)` — size should follow the style's PM_SmallIconSize or a deliberate constant (16 or 20/22 — pick what the bundled themes look right at, keeping the sidebar compact), plus `setUniformItemSizes(true)`; mind HiDPI (setIconSize is in device-independent px — correct) and re-apply if theme switching at runtime (ICONS01 combo calls AroraIcon::setTheme — verify icons re-render at the fixed size, not re-resolve bigger). (b) Sweep the rest of the dialog for the same miss: the iconThemeCombo preview rows (settings.cpp ~152-158 — each theme's own view-refresh glyph will differ in canvas size too) and any other icon-bearing widget in settings.ui — normalize there too if visible. (c) Do NOT patch individual icons in the icon themes themselves — normalize at the widget/iconSize level so every current and future theme behaves. Verify: open Preferences offscreen under each bundled theme (adwaita, breeze, tabler, native) — all sidebar icons share one pixel height and the text baselines align; combo previews uniform; make check PASS.

### DOWN01

Rich download cards (Opera/Vivaldi-style) — user request with reference screenshot: download items should expand into a detail card showing filename, source URL, destination path, Started/Finished timestamps, total size, current speed, a speed-over-time area graph, and 'Restart' + 'Show in File Manager' buttons. Today DownloadItem (downloaditem.ui) is a compact row: fileIcon + squeezed filename + QProgressBar + info label ('X of Y — Z/s, ~Ns remaining') + stop/tryAgain/open buttons; DownloadManager (downloads.ui) is a dialog listing them via DownloadModel. What exists: m_url captured (downloadmanager.cpp ~149), m_downloadTime QElapsedTimer, currentSpeed() (~589 — CUMULATIVE average bytesReceived/elapsed, unsuitable for the graph), 200ms update throttle (m_lastProgressTime), DownloadManager::dataString/timeString formatters. Spec: (a) SPEED SERIES — add a bounded ring buffer of instantaneous samples: on each receivedBytesChanged, push (elapsed ms, deltaBytes/deltaSeconds); ~2 samples/sec, keep last N (~120 = 60s window); on stateChanged->Completed/Interrupted freeze the series and store the finish QDateTime + final average. (b) CARD UI — expandable per item (collapsed = today's compact row; expand on click or a chevron like Vivaldi — an always-expanded variant is acceptable if markedly simpler, expansion preferred): SqueezeLabels for source URL + destination path (file:// elided middle), 'Started: <time>' + 'Finished: <time>' lines, total size, live speed text. (c) GRAPH — small custom QWidget (paintEvent, ~48px tall, follows the UIP04 statusbar-widget convention): filled area sparkline of the speed series, palette Text/Highlight colors (dark-scheme safe per UIP01), auto-scaled Y to peak sample, flat-line at zero when no samples; repaint on new samples only. (d) BUTTONS — 'Show in File Manager' reveals the finished file's folder (QDesktopServices::openUrl(QUrl::fromLocalFile(dir)) — Linux path, xdg-open; selecting the file itself is bonus); 'Restart' generalizes the existing tryAgain path to also appear on completed items (re-issue m_url through the normal download flow); keep stop/open as-is. (e) persisted/cleared downloads handled sanely: items restored after restart show what data is known (URL, filename, size, timestamps) and simply omit the graph. Verify: download a file — live card shows rising/falling graph + speed text updating ~2x/s, Finished timestamp on completion; Restart re-downloads; Show in File Manager opens the folder; collapsed row unchanged; make check PASS.

### DLACC01

Downloads settings page + engine selector — foundation for the accelerated-download work (user-approved plan: selectable engines). ### DLACC02

'Built-in' turbo: Chromium ParallelDownloading flag — the cheap acceleration (user-approved as the default option). Chromium's `ParallelDownloading` base::Feature splits a file into ~3 parallel range requests when the server supports Accept-Ranges — engine-native, no new code path. Spec: (a) settings checkbox on the new DLACC01 Downloads page — 'Parallel segments (built-in engine, applies at restart)' — persisted downloadmanager/parallelSegments default ON; (b) wire into `BrowserProfile::applyChromiumFlags()` (main.cpp ~1355 reads settings BEFORE engine construction — perfect fit): when enabled, append `--enable-features=ParallelDownloading` to QTWEBENGINE_CHROMIUM_FLAGS; label must honestly say 'takes effect on restart' since flags latch at engine init; (c) Tor windows: same flag is fine (engine path stays proxied) — no special-casing needed, but note it in the row. Verify: launch with flag applied — a range-capable download uses multiple connections (check via server access log or chunk interleave), flag absent => single stream; setting persists; make check PASS.

### DLACC03

Rust accelerated downloader (reqwest via C FFI) — the real 'turbo' engine behind the DLACC01 selector. Architecture per codebase precedent (ADB02's adblock-rust plan): a Rust `staticlib`/`cdylib` crate (e.g. src/rustdl/ or tools/rustdl/) exposing a thin C API — `dl_start(url, dest_dir, suggested_name, connections, cookie_file_or_null) -> handle`, `dl_poll(handle) -> {state, bytes_done, bytes_total, speed_bps, error}`, `dl_cancel(handle)`, `dl_free(handle)` — implemented on `reqwest` blocking feature + std::thread (simpler FFI than a tokio runtime bridge): HEAD/GET-probe for Accept-Ranges + Content-Length, then N ranged GETs (user-configurable 4-16, default 8) into part files, merge by atomic rename on completion; servers without ranges => fall back to single-stream, NOT the engine (decision documented). Rust toolchain: user-local rustup only (same constraint as ADB02 — needs:tool:rustup acceptable; do NOT fail the whole task if rustup absent — write the crate + build script + gate it behind a qmake CONFIG flag so the tree builds without Rust; when rust is absent the selector shows only Built-in). Qt side: thin QObjects wrapper (RustDownloadEngine) mapping handle->DownloadItem, polling on a QTimer (or callback thread posting to the GUI thread), feeding progress into the existing card UI; cancel->dl_cancel->cleanup. File hygiene INSIDE scope: part files in a 0700 temp dir under BrowserPaths data dir, cleanup on cancel/fail, final file appears only via atomic rename after merge+size check; suggested filename sanitized against `../`, absolute paths, NUL, Windows device names (Chromium does this for free today — we must replicate). Build: qmake subdirs or a Makefile target building the crate; document `cargo vendor`/offline in a comment. Verify: fixture server (python http.server lacks ranges — use a small ranged fixture or the Rust dev-dep http crate in tests) serves a 10MB file in 8 segments -> merged checksum matches; progress polls ~2x/s; cancel cleans part files; engine fallback on non-range server; make check PASS (builds with AND without rust present).

### DLACC04

Custom-engine LEAK/privacy parity — the Rust download path must not weaken any guarantee engine downloads get for free. Engine downloads today pass through QWebEngineUrlRequestInterceptor (adblock rules, HTTPS-first upgrade, SAFE01 httpsOnly veto) AND the application proxy — a custom engine bypasses ALL of it. Required parity BEFORE the accelerated engine handles real URLs: (a) POLICY GATE — run the same pre-flight the interceptor would: httpsFirst upgrade http->https, httpsOnly veto/warn on plain http (SAFE01 semantics incl. localhost/LAN/.onion exemptions), adblock/tracking-rule match on the download URL (a download to a tracker domain gets blocked same as a resource); (b) REDIRECTS — every redirect hop re-runs the whole gate; refuse redirects to non-http(s) schemes; do NOT forward cookies/auth headers cross-origin on redirect (strip Authorization/Cookie when the hop changes registrable domain); cap redirect count; (c) HEADER PARITY — send the same User-Agent as the profile (a distinct UA on downloads is a fingerprint diff) + the same Referer rules the page would produce (REF01's levels — carry the document URL's referrer per policy, never leak the full URL of a sensitive page as referer to a third-party host); (d) PROXY/TOR HARD RULE — if an application proxy is set, the Rust engine MUST use it (reqwest Proxy) — and in a TOR WINDOW downloads must NEVER go direct: either route via the managed tor's SOCKS5 (preferred — keeps acceleration in tor mode) or fall back to the engine path entirely (acceptable v1) — but a direct clearnet fetch from a tor window is a DE-ANONYMIZATION BUG, treat as release-blocker; (e) PRIVATE-IP/SSRF — apply the same private/LAN host rules the interceptors use (a page can't use the download engine to probe intranet hosts). Verify: fixture matrix — http URL upgrades under httpsFirst, http blocked under httpsOnly, tracker-domain URL refused, redirect chain re-gated + cross-host cookie strip asserted, tor-mode download provably exits via SOCKS or engine (no direct socket), UA matches profile UA; make check PASS.

### DLACC05

Custom-engine secrets + filesystem hygiene — information-leak hardening for the Rust download path (user directive: 'make sure it won't leak any information'). Spec: (a) COOKIES — authenticated downloads need session cookies; export ONLY the rows matching the target host (QWebEngineCookieStore cookieAdded/cookieRemoved maintain a live per-host Netscape-format file OR query-and-dump on demand), file mode 0600 in the 0700 temp dir, fsync+closed before the engine starts, deleted (secure-ish unlink) when the download finishes/fails — never a whole-jar export, never world-readable; (b) LOG HYGIENE — signed URLs carry tokens in query strings: nothing logs a full URL; any debug/progress output redacts query+fragment (host+path only); the download item's stored/displayed source URL may keep the URL (it's user-visible anyway) but it must NOT land in logLine/debug streams or the WORKLOG; (c) TEMP/PART FILES — private dir under BrowserPaths (0700), randomized names (don't leak the destination filename in tmp), unlink on every exit path incl. crash-adjacent drop guards where feasible; merged output lands via atomic rename; (d) FILENAME SANITIZATION — Content-Disposition/suggested names: strip path separators + drive letters + device names + control chars, cap length, decode %-escapes safely — a malicious server must never write outside the download dir (this duplicates DLACC03's in-scope item deliberately — it's the security gate that verifies it, incl. a fixture with `filename=../../evil`); (e) NO EXTRA SURFACE — the FFI boundary passes no other profile data (no history, no other cookies, no page DOM); the Rust engine must not phone home (no telemetry, no update checks in the crate — audit its dep tree for anything that networks). Verify: adversarial fixture server — traversal filename lands sanitized inside download dir, cookie file perms are 0600 + only target-host rows + deleted after, grep of a debug run shows no query strings/tokens, tor+cookie combinations leak nothing cross-host; make check PASS.

### TELEM02

Telemetry residual sweep — during-browsing + DNS prefetch (user directive: remove all telemetry, block what can't be removed). TELEM01 already zeroed the idle-launch surface (kill-flags: background-networking/component-update/domain-reliability/metrics/sync/first-run; adblock lists consent-gated; --telemetry-smoke proves 0 hits/30s). What it did NOT cover: (a) DURING-BROWSING traffic — extend the telemetry-smoke capture window through real page loads (drive a few real sites through the capture proxy, not just idle start) and classify every non-content connection: connectivity checks (clients3.google.com/generate_204), OCSP/CRL cert validation (CA endpoints — engine-managed; document as unavoidable-by-design rather than disable validation), HSTS/CRLSets, anything surviving the kill-flags — remove via flag if removable, else block the endpoint in PrivacyRequestInterceptor with a named-exception comment, else document with justification; (b) DNS PREFETCH — browserprofile.cpp:338 sets DnsPrefetchEnabled=true: Chromium resolves every hostname on every rendered page, an unsolicited stream of the user's browsing-domain hints to the resolver — make it a privacy/ toggle (default OFF — prefetch saves ~ms per click, not worth the leak; Tor mode: verify it cannot be on at all since Chromium DNS resolves via the system resolver outside SOCKS — that would be a proxy-bypass class bug, escalate if observed); (c) regression guard — add the during-browsing capture to the smoke harness so a future feature can't silently re-introduce chatter. Verify: extended smoke shows zero non-content/non-essential connections during a real browsing session incl. search/navigation/download; DnsPrefetchEnabled off by default; make check PASS.

### TABS02

Vertical tab bar: horizontal labels + resizable width — user report with screenshots: tab bar position Left/Right (TABS01) renders tab TITLES rotated 90° (Qt's style paints RoundedWest/East text vertically) — unreadable; user wants horizontal labels and a resizable strip, Vivaldi-style. TABS01 already flagged this caveat. Spec: (a) HORIZONTAL LABELS — TabBar (src/tabbar.{h,cpp}, already a QTabBar subclass with verticalTabShape() + tabSizeHint override ~366/460) needs a custom paint path for West/East shapes: draw each tab as a horizontal ROW — favicon left, title text horizontal+elided (ElideRight), close affordance + selection/hover states via palette (reuse the horizontal-shape painting approach but laid out as a row — do NOT let the style rotate; either initStyleOption + manual draw or full custom paintEvent for the vertical shapes only, horizontal shapes keep style painting). Row height ~fm+padding (e.g. 26-30px), tabSizeHint for vertical returns (persisted width x row height); unreadable rotated text is the acceptance criterion — labels must be horizontal. (b) RESIZABLE STRIP — QTabWidget's bar can't go in a splitter, so implement drag-resize on the bar's INNER edge: track a ~4-6px grip zone along the content-facing edge of the vertical TabBar (left edge when bar is Right, right edge when bar is Left) -> SizeHorCursor -> drag adjusts a persisted width (e.g. tabs/verticalTabWidth, default ~180, clamp ~120-400) -> tabSizeHint returns it -> force relayout (tabLayoutChange/updateGeometry); width persists + applies live to all windows (same live-apply path TABS01 used for position); must not break tear-off drag (grip zone excluded from tab drag initiation — check hit test vs tabAt). (c) keep everything else working in vertical: movable tabs, context menu, the '+' affordance, scroll buttons if overflowing, drop indicator position. (d) TOP/BOTTOM positions unchanged. Verify: Left and Right positions show horizontal readable titles; edge-drag resizes live and persists across restart + windows; tear-off/context/move still work in all 4 positions; make check PASS.

### TABS03

Tab Bar Position: Vivaldi-style visual tile picker — user request with reference screenshot: replace the plain 'Tab bar position:' QComboBox on the Tab Settings page (settings.ui `tab_6` ~line 1815, items Top/Bottom/Left/Right -> persisted tabBarPosition int, read at settings.cpp ~647/898, mapped to shapes at ~999) with a row of 4 CLICKABLE PREVIEW TILES: each tile is a miniature browser-window sketch showing where the tab strip sits (a dark rounded rect with a lighter tab-strip bar painted on the top/left/right/bottom edge + a couple of tab cells inside it), a text label under each ('Top','Left','Right','Bottom'), and the selected tile shows a highlighted border + check badge (palette Highlight — dark-scheme safe). (b) expose index semantics compatible with existing code — same 0..3 mapping (Top/Bottom/Left/Right — the VISUAL order in the screenshot is Top/Left/Right/Bottom but the persisted indices must NOT change or existing settings remap wrongly; decouple display order from stored index), a currentIndex()-like getter/setter + a positionChanged signal so settings.cpp load/save rewiring is minimal (~3 lines); combo can be deleted from the .ui or kept hidden — prefer delete if wiring is clean. (c) live apply unchanged — the same saveToSettings -> all-TabWidgets path TABS01 wired keeps working. (d) tile layout inside the group: label the group 'Tab Bar Position' (matching the screenshot's heading style — a group box or a bold label above the tile row replaces the old label+combo row). Verify: all 4 tiles render correct previews in light+dark, click/keyboard select applies live + persists across restart, old saved values (0-3 + out-of-range) load onto the right tile, tst_settingsdialog updated + PASS, make check PASS.

### SBAR01

Status-bar: per-tab memory + live bandwidth indicators — user request: show the current tab's memory usage in the status bar, and show bandwidth while network activity happens. Both ride the UIP04 widget pattern (src/statusbarwidgets.h — LoadingIndicator/ZoomControl added as permanent widgets at browsermainwindow.cpp ~237). Feasibility already established: QWebEnginePage::renderProcessPid exists (Qt 6.12 header verified) giving the real renderer PID per tab; the codebase already parses /proc (main.cpp ~412+ reads /proc/net + /proc/<pid>/stat + fds). Spec — (a) MEM WIDGET: 'MemIndicator' — on a ~2s QTimer read /proc/<renderProcessPid>/status VmRSS for the CURRENT tab's page and show e.g. 'MEM 238 MB' (KB->MB format, one decimal >=100MB); follow tab changes (setWebView pattern like LoadingIndicator) AND renderProcessPidChanged (renderer swap/crash -> resubscribe); pid 0/-1 or dead process -> show '—'. (b) NET WIDGET: 'NetIndicator' — poll every ~1s the io counters (/proc/<pid>/io read_bytes/write_bytes) of the app's QtWebEngineProcess children — primarily the network-service utility process (identify via /proc/<pid>/cmdline — it carries --type=utility with a network service subtype marker; if not identifiable, sum all QtWebEngineProcess children and accept the cache-I/O noise — document the approximation honestly in a comment + tooltip) -> delta/time = current up/down rates -> '↓ 1.2 MB/s ↑ 84 kB/s'; DISPLAY RULE per user: visible only while nonzero/recently-active — hide (or collapse to zero-width) after ~3s idle so the bar stays clean; ALSO fold in real download throughput when DownloadManager has active items (their receivedBytes deltas are exact — prefer real numbers over the io approximation while a file downloads). (c) both widgets: palette-only colors, accessibleName, tooltip explaining what they show + the approximation caveat; Linux-only /proc reads behind Q_OS_LINUX — hide the widgets on other platforms (no needs: tag — code-gated). (d) do NOT disturb the status-bar message area or the existing permanent widgets' order contract (new widgets go right of zoom — or left of loading; pick consistent with Vivaldi's left-anchored stats). Verify: a large page load shows nonzero down-rate then auto-hides; a heavy tab reads plausible MB that changes on allocation churn (e.g. opening a big site moves it); tab switch updates the number; renderer kill -> '—'; make check PASS.

### SAND01

Cross-platform sandbox layer — user directive: multi-OS release planned (Linux dev now; macOS, BSD, Windows 10/11 at release), so build this as an abstraction NOT a Linux script. New src/sandbox/ module: a declarative SandboxPolicy (one rule list: profile dir rw, ~/Downloads rw, rest of $HOME rw MINUS denylist ~/.ssh ~/.gnupg ~/.config-of-other-apps + browser cookie stores of other browsers, /etc + /usr read-only, no net restriction — browsing needs it) -> per-platform GENERATORS as pure functions (testable on Linux!): (a) LINUX permissive-bwrap argv generator — wraps the arora binary: --bind $HOME minus denylisted paths (each denied path bind-mounted over /dev/null or absent tmpfs), plus a launcher script installed next to the binary. THIS backend is applied + verified end-to-end here: sandboxed arora launches, browses, saves a file anywhere in $HOME except denied paths, and a denied-path read inside the sandbox fails (test: try opening ~/.ssh/id_rsa from the sandboxed process). (b) macOS Seatbelt backend — generate a .sb profile text (deny file-read* for the same denylist, allow elsewhere) + a Q_OS_MAC apply path via sandbox_init()/sandbox-exec wrapper — generator output unit-tested on Linux; apply path ifdef'd and documented UNTESTED-ON-TARGET with a runtime --sandbox-status diagnostic that reports which backend applied. (c) WINDOWS 10/11 AppContainer backend — generate the capability manifest + CreateAppContainerProfile-based launcher code behind Q_OS_WIN (works on a normal .exe, no MSIX store needed); manifest XML unit-tested on Linux, apply path ifdef'd + same untested disclaimer. (d) BSD backend — OpenBSD: unveil(2)/pledge(2) call sequence generated from the same policy (unveil per-path rules + pledge promises); FreeBSD: document the Capsicum gap honestly and provide the unveil-equivalent stub with a clear warning. One switch: --no-sandbox env/flag + QSettings escape hatch (documented as dev-only), and the app must still work 100% unsandboxed when bwrap/sandbox-exec/AppContainer is absent on the host (detect, warn once, continue — never hard-fail). Verify: Linux permissive-bwrap e2e PASS incl. save-anywhere-except-denylist; all four generators unit-tested offscreen; unsandboxed fallback PASS; make check PASS.

### SEC17

ClearURLs-style tracking-param stripping in Rust — extends the ADB02 FFI pattern: small Rust cdylib holding a compiled ruleset (utm_*, fbclid, gclid, _ga, etc. — start from the ClearURLs rules data, vendored as data file so it's updatable like filter lists) exposing strip(url)->url via C FFI; PrivacyRequestInterceptor consults it on every request and 307-redirects to the stripped URL when the rule fires (before the request leaves). Skip rules for URLs where stripping breaks auth (ruleset carries per-site exceptions — respect them). Rust-only for the matching logic (memory-safe over attacker URLs); the strip must be transparent in the UI (no interstitial). Verify: ?utm_source=x&real=1 lands as ?real=1, exception sites untouched, no-strip URLs zero-redirected, make check PASS.

### SAFE06

Anti-fingerprinting script injection — Tor-Browser-style countermeasures via the QWebEngineScript mechanism autofill already uses: inject a small JS layer per page (canvas readback noise, WebGL vendor/renderer masking to a generic value, navigator.hardwareConcurrency/plugins normalization). ### CONT06

Two-level container tab strip — user request (Vivaldi screenshot): when a window has container-bound tabs, don't show them inline with chips — show a SECOND tab row. Level 1 = one header entry per container present in the window (icon + name + tab-count badge like the screenshot's 'Example Domain [2]', plus a default entry for unbound tabs — 'Tabs' or window glyph); level 2 = the tabs of the currently-selected container only — all tab ops (reorder, close, context menu, middle-click) work on the filtered row. Clicking a level-1 header swaps level 2's contents; the active header gets selected styling; count badges update live on open/close/move. Drag a level-2 tab onto a different level-1 header -> rebind (reopenInContainer semantics — new tab in target container, old closes); drag between level-1 headers reorders them. Unbound tabs live under the default header — NO tab may be unreachable in either direction (a container with 0 tabs drops its header unless pinned-by-setting; the default header always exists). Interactions to preserve: TABGRP01 groups nest INSIDE a container level (container > group > tab hierarchy — a group's pill/chip renders within level 2 of its container), TABS01 vertical positions keep working (two-level renders stacked — level-1 row above level-2 row when the bar is vertical), the CONT02 per-tab chip may be dropped inside level-2 rows (redundant there) but kept in single-row mode. Mode choice: 'Container display: Inline chips / Two-level strip' setting on the Containers settings section (CONT03 if landed, else Tab Settings) — two-level can be the default when containers exist if it tests well; single-row stays available. Persist the active level-1 selection per window; new-window restore sane (last-used container or default). Accessibility: headers have names + counts in accessibleName, keyboard-focusable, arrow-key nav across levels. Verify: two containers' tabs never mix in one row, count badges exact, drag-across-containers rebinds, empty container header disappears, groups render inside their container's row, vertical + horizontal positions both sane, make check PASS.

### RCORE01

Shared-Rust-core foundation + credential store migration — user direction: engine-neutral app components live in a shared Rust cdylib behind C FFI (the Mozilla application-services pattern), consumed by the Qt shell identically regardless of engine and portable across Linux/macOS/Win10-11/BSD (same code; .so/.dylib/.dll artifact naming only). PART A — scaffolding: create the rustcore crate (src/rustcore/ or tools/rustcore/) establishing the conventions every later component reuses: C-ABI surface, error codes + string marshaling helpers, change-notification callback -> Qt-signal bridge pattern, cargo build wiring into qmake reusing the ADB02/DLACC03 toolchain path (user rustup, CONFIG-gated so no-rust builds skip it), RustCrypto-version pinning + cargo deny compliance per SEC21. PART B — credential store: port SecureStore's semantics into rustcore — AEAD with XChaCha20-Poly1305 (or AES-256-GCM) + Argon2id master-passphrase KDF, same securestore.key/securestore.kdf file layout (or versioned-migrate with both readers during transition), C ABI: rc_cred_get/put/remove/list + unlock/lock/change-passphrase. Document the PQ posture honestly: 256-bit symmetric AEAD + Argon2id is already post-quantum-safe at rest (Grover only halves to ~128-bit); ML-KEM-768 hybrid belongs to the FUTURE SYNC layer, not local storage — note it in the crate README so nobody 'adds PQ' where it does nothing. Qt adapter: keep SecureStore's C++ interface 1:1 so autofillmanager compiles unchanged — internals delegate over FFI. Verify: existing securestore/autofill tests pass through the Rust core, wrong-passphrase path intact, old-format files still decrypt (or migrate cleanly), build works with and without rustup present, make check PASS.

### RCORE02

Rust bookmark + history stores — migrate BookmarkModel/HistoryManager storage into the RCORE01 crate: (a) BOOKMARKS: XBEL stays the on-disk format for compat (quick-xml parse in Rust — kills the untrusted-file memory-safety class per SEC19's audit; evaluate rusqlite as the index/search layer but XBEL remains canonical unless a clean migration is written) — CRUD, folder tree, tags, dedup, atomic writes, change-event callback over the ABI. (b) HISTORY: rusqlite-backed store (visits, titles, timestamps, per-host icon refs — the HIST01 icon persistence becomes a rustcore table/file dir if this lands first; note the overlap in the task notes). (c) QT ADAPTER: QAbstractItemModel wrappers exposing lazy per-node queries — models ask for children of node X via handle, NO bulk tree marshaling across FFI (matches Qt's lazy model contract, keeps FFI calls O(touched-rows)); Rust change events -> beginInsertRows/endInsertRows correctly. (d) engine-neutrality: engines only REPORT navigations/bookmark intent — the store is shell-owned; per the ENG01 interface, the engine adapter emits visit records the core consumes. Migrate both stores' file readers with both-format tolerance during transition. Verify: bookmark tree/history views render identically, add/remove/edit/dedup round-trips through Rust, icon persistence survives restart (absorbs HIST01 if applicable), old XBEL/history files import, make check PASS.

### RCORE03

Rust session/tab serialization — move saveSession/restoreLastSession's serialization into rustcore: versioned schema, atomic write-temp-then-rename, corrupt-blob resilience (SESS01's crash-guard learning becomes a core property — a malformed session blob can never loop the crash prompt or drop the window set), multi-window + per-tab state incl. container binding and engine tag. Engine-neutrality: the core stores an OPAQUE per-tab engine-state blob — each engine adapter serializes its own history/zoom piece (QtWebEngine's serializePageHistory output is just bytes to the core; a Servo backend supplies its own) — so the session format survives the engine swap untouched. Qt adapter keeps browserapplication's save/restore call sites unchanged. Verify: close->relaunch restores windows/tabs/index/containers exactly (the --session-smoke harness from SESS01 re-run through the Rust path), corrupt/truncated blob handled gracefully, private windows still excluded, make check PASS.

Runtime adblock-engine selector in Preferences -> Security — today CONFIG+=adblock_rust is COMPILE-TIME only (#if ARORA_ADBLOCK_RUST delegates all matching to m_rustEngine). Add a settings combo on the Security page/group: "Content blocker engine: Built-in (default) / Brave adblock-rust". ### SESS02

Unclean-exit session flush — close the OS-kill gap in session persistence (user directive: session must survive 'the app crashes or is closed by the OS'). RCORE03 already made each save atomic + corruption-proof and SESS01's AutoSaver debounces saves on tab changes, but NOTHING flushes on termination signals today: SIGTERM/SIGINT/SIGHUP take the process down through the default handler — the session on disk is only as fresh as the last AutoSaver debounce fired, and a timed kill or logout can lose the in-flight change set entirely. Spec: (a) POSIX-safe signal handling — install handlers for SIGTERM/SIGINT/SIGHUP that CANNOT do Qt work in the raw handler (async-signal-safety): the standard socketpair+write-one-byte -> QSocketNotifier->event-loop bounce (or equivalent); the notifier slot calls AutoSaver::saveIfNeccessary()/saveSession() directly bypassing the debounce, then re-raises/_exits promptly — the rustcore session write is already temp+fsync+rename atomic so a final flush is always safe to issue; (b) wedged-loop cap — if the event loop can't run the flush, an alarm()/watchdog fallback exits anyway so a hung app still dies on the deadline rather than ignoring SIGTERM; (c) clean-exit distinction stays honest — normal Quit already saves; the handler only adds the termination-signal path; private windows still excluded (saveSession's isPrivate skip covers it); (d) Windows note — SetConsoleCtrlHandler/WM_CLOSE equivalent documented for the WIN01 port but POSIX-only implementation here. Verify: scripted test — launch, open 3+ tabs, change state faster than the AutoSaver debounce (so a naive run would lose it), SIGTERM, relaunch -> all tabs restored incl. the last change; SIGKILL correctly loses only the debounce window (documented, not fixable); --session-smoke still passes; make check PASS.

### PDF01

In-browser PDF viewer + Rust sanitize pass — today PDFs fall through to the download path (no reader). (a) VIEWER: QtWebEngine 6.12 ships Chromium's built-in PDFium viewer behind QWebEngineSettings::PdfViewerEnabled (browserprofile.cpp:373-448 attribute block) — enable it default-ON with a Preferences toggle (privacy/pdfViewer or content group); PDFium parses inside the sandboxed renderer — the strongest practical viewer posture. (b) INTERCEPTOR AUDIT: verify viewer-initiated traffic (remote font/resource fetches inside PDFs, outbound link clicks, file:// attempts) actually traverses PrivacyRequestInterceptor with the same verdicts — HTTPS-Only, Tor SOCKS routing, container isolation; assert no file:// access from a web-served PDF; document any bypass class found (Chromium viewer internal fetches may not hit the interceptor — if so, gate the viewer OFF for Tor windows like DLACC privacy rules do). (c) RUST SANITIZE PASS (user directive — the strengthener): a rustcore pdf-sanitize component (lopdf or equivalent rewrite crate — NOT a parser you feed pages, a policy pass that REWRITES the bytes before the viewer sees them): strip /JS and /JavaScript entries, /Launch, /AA, /OpenAction, /Names->/EmbeddedFiles, form auto-submit/XFA remnants; output a byte-identical-layout sanitized file to the 0700 temp dir with randomized name; default ON (defense-in-depth — PDF JS/actions are the policy risk the viewer sandbox doesn't address); setting privacy/pdfSanitize. (d) the download path stays intact — 'download' on a PDF link still saves raw bytes; sanitize applies to the in-browser viewing path only (document this honestly: saving gives the user's external reader raw bytes). Verify: adversarial fixture (embedded /Launch + JS) renders sanitized with both stripped in the decoded output; interceptor coverage proven for viewer fetches incl. a Tor-window check; toggle round-trips; make check PASS.

### PDF02

Engine-neutral shell-owned PDF view — the Servo-era design (libservo has NO pdf viewer; PdfViewerEnabled is WebEngine-only). A Qt document widget OWNED BY THE SHELL (like an error page/download card, not engine content) renders PDF pages rasterized by a sandboxed rustcore subprocess: pdfium-render (BSD-3, same engine Chrome uses — but now parse-risk lives in OUR minimal process per the SAND pattern, not the engine's renderer) or equivalent; page bitmaps marshaled to the Qt view; zoom/scroll/page-nav/find-in-document/print handoff. application/pdf responses route to the document view through the ENG01 engine interface — identical view on WebEngine and Servo backends; PDF01's sanitize pass always applies before the renderer regardless of engine; WebEngine's built-in viewer becomes an optional Settings fallback, not the design. Tor/private profiles get the same view (no external handoff — xdg-open is a leak surface). Bound it: sub-view first (pages render + scroll), then chrome (toolbar/nav/print); commit-per-piece. Verify: PDF opens in the shell view on the WebEngine backend through the engine interface exactly as a Servo backend would; sanitize always applied; make check PASS.

### AUDIT01

Post-landing hardening sweep — static analysis + leak/sanitizer pass over the whole tree after the task queues drain (user directive: 'there were a lot of changes'). BOUNDED triage task — fix real findings, document noise; do NOT reformat the codebase. Spec: (a) STATIC ANALYSIS — clazy (Qt-aware) + clang-tidy core checks (bugprone-*, cert-*, clang-analyzer-*) over src/: build a compile_commands.json, run, classify findings — real bugs get fixed in the same run (commit-per-area), Qt4-era idiom noise gets a triage report (.devin/AUDIT01-report.md) with per-class verdicts (fixed / suppressed-with-justification / documented-wontfix); (b) MEMORY/UB — ASan+LSan+UBSan build (qmake CONFIG+=sanitize or CXXFLAGS variant — reuse however the rustlane/dlacc builds gate toolchains, no sudo) run through make check + the key smokes (session-restore, download, tor-window): leaks in OUR code (src/) get fixed; leaks inside QtWebEngine/Chromium or known Qt internals get documented — do not chase engine-internal noise; (c) existing harnesses — re-run .devin/check-leaks.sh + check-sanitize.sh if still wired, fix regressions they flag; (d) REGRESSION — full make check green at the end incl. the no-rust build. Deliverable: report file + fixes; gate is 'zero unresolved real findings in src/', not 'zero warnings'. Verify: make check PASS on both default and sanitize builds; report committed.

### SMOKE01

Isolate smoke-test settings writes from the real user config — ROOT-CAUSE BUG, user hit it twice: url-bar search kept flipping off because --search-guess-smoke phase-4 does `QSettings().setValue(urlloading/searchEngineFallback,false)` on the REAL ~/.config/Arora/Arora.conf; the known wart is documented in main.cpp ('QSettings ignores the test-mode paths' at ~2902/2917 — smokes pin real keys and 'restore on the way out'). When a smoke run is killed mid-test (taskloop cap-kills are routine, rc=143), the restore never runs and the user's live config is left mutated — this is a correctness bug, not a test inconvenience. Fix: (a) EARLY in main() before the first QSettings is constructed — if any --*-smoke/test flag is present, redirect the config root: setenv XDG_CONFIG_HOME=<mktemp -d> (QSettings NativeFormat honors it) OR QSettings::setPath + setDefaultFormat equivalently — verify which actually moves ~/.config/Arora/Arora.conf on this Qt build (default format may be IniFormat already); ALSO redirect the cache/data roots if any smoke writes there; (b) SWEEP every existing 'pin real key + restore on the way out' block (tlsStrictCiphers, webrtcIpProtection, searchEngineFallback, the '##body cosmetic rule' leftovers at ~6425/7574, others — grep setValue inside smoke paths) and confirm they now land in the temp store; drop the restore gymnastics where they're no longer needed; (c) the test that VERIFIES persistence (searchEngineFallback persists check) must still work — it verifies the temp store round-trips, same semantics; (d) GUARD: add a test-mode assertion — a smoke that detects it wrote to the real config path fails loudly (compare resolved QSettings::fileName against the real home). Verify: run --search-guess-smoke, kill -9 a smoke mid-run, confirm real Arora.conf untouched (mtime + content diff before/after); make check PASS.

### SEC23

External-URL ingress audit — user directive 'as secure as possible': verify + lock every path an untrusted URL can enter a running app, complementing the ui-lane CONT07/CONT08 link-open work (which adds a '--tor <url>' operand — this task owns the process-boundary side). Entry-point matrix: (a) argv positional — already gated by SEC09 (main.cpp:3502-3507 refuses before load, postLaunch :567-581 re-gates via loadStringFromUntrustedSource) — add regression coverage feeding javascript:/data:/vbscript:/file-with-tricks operands; (b) single-instance socket forward — messageReceived (browserapplication.cpp:435-463) routes non-'aroramessage://' payloads through loadStringFromUntrustedSource incl. the 10s dedup guard — verify the control channel can never be reached by a URL-shaped payload and vice versa (namespace confusion), and add tests; (c) QDesktopServices 'http' handler -> openUrl() (:1009-1014) calls loadUrl DIRECTLY — no untrusted gate: route it through the same gate (defense in depth — today only http reaches it, but the slot is public, callable from in-app paths, and the registered-scheme list can grow); (d) --tor operand — torStartup (:635-649) already funnels through loadStringFromUntrustedSource: verify CONT07's new argv hand-off lands the URL in the operand slot correctly (argumentUrl :425-433 picks the last non-dash arg — a URL string must never be parsed as an option by QCommandLineParser, and nothing may go through a shell); assert a --tor process stays standalone: never startSingleServer, never accepts forwarded URLs (m_standalone gate :241-259); (e) single-instance socket hardening — confirm the QLocalServer endpoint is not guessable/writable beyond the user (runtime dir or abstract socket, not a predictable /tmp name) — a same-user unauthenticated open-URL primitive exists regardless, document that threat honestly in the report; (f) forwarded URL into a PRIVATE instance: mainWindow()->tabWidget() may be an OTR window — landing there is an upgrade not a leak, but the 'openLinksFromAppsIn' pref (:453-455) must not resurrect a cleared/closed window in an unexpected profile. Write --ingress-smoke (or extend the nearest existing smoke) driving each path with hostile scheme variants + control-message confusion, asserting no navigation/no script/no gate bypass. Verify: every entry point gated or documented-safe in a short .devin/SEC23-report.md; openUrl() hardened; --ingress-smoke PASS incl. tor-operand and standalone assertions; make check PASS.

### EXT05

Extension hardening — Manifest-V3 Chrome extensions run full-trust JS in pages; EXT01-04 built the manager (install/review/update) but the trust surface is thin. User directive: strengthen plugins. Spec: (a) CONSENT GATE — every install path (dir, .zip, dropped file) must pass the ExtensionReviewDialog's permission review BEFORE installExtension() reaches the profile — audit installExtension/loadExtension callers for any path that skips review; silent install = bug. (b) DANGEROUS-PERMISSION POLICY — the manifest review already classifies unsupported/unverified (extensionmanager.cpp ~333-399 lists debugger, nativeMessaging, browsingData, privacy, system.storage...): make a real policy — a deny-by-default set (nativeMessaging — spawns host processes if the engine honors it; debugger — full page control; webRequest blocking; cookies; browsingData; '<all_urls>' in host_permissions) requires an explicit per-permission consent checkbox in the review dialog, not just a warning label; denied permission -> refuse install or install-with-permission-stripped honestly labeled. (c) TOR/PRIVATE ISOLATION — extensions must not load on tor or private profiles: verify installOnProfile never wires the tor profile (a beaconing extension de-anonymizes) — add a hard gate + test asserting the tor profile's extension set stays empty even if the normal profile has extensions installed. (d) UPDATE CHANNEL — auto-update fetches .crx/.zip over the network: HTTPS-only (refuse http update URLs at fetch time, not just parse), verify downloaded bytes against the update manifest's declared hash if present, and require user consent before an auto-update replaces installed code (updateAvailable exists — make apply opt-in). (e) CRX3 note — real signature verification can't be done for unsigned/community extensions; document that honestly and treat 'signed-by-Chrome-Web-Store' as a v2 scope item. (f) USERSCRIPTS — the *.js dir (reloadUserScripts ~451): enforce 0700 dir / 0600 files, warn if world-readable. (g) CODE HYGIENE while in there — the by-id lookup loop is duplicated 3x (removeExtension ~218, setExtensionEnabled ~237, updateUrlFor ~655): extract a findById helper; and the QTimer::singleShot(400) 'refresh the UI after toggle' hack (~245) deserves a comment upgrade or a changed-signal poll that isn't a fixed delay if a cleaner trigger exists — small cleanup, no behavior change. Verify: install without review impossible; dangerous-permission install refuses without explicit consent; tor profile extension set provably empty; http update URL refused; tampered update payload rejected; make check PASS.

### ENG09

Tabs collapse after switching a tab to Servo — user report: 'when switching servo, the tabs are collapsed'. The swap path is reloadTabInEngine (tabwidget.cpp:1017): build replacement (WebView for webengine target via makeNewTabInContainer, EngineTab via addEngineTab) -> m_tabBar->moveTab(newIndex, index) -> closeTab(index + 1). Suspects, ranked: (a) INDEX DRIFT ON THE SWAP — moveTab can trigger normalizePinnedBlock/group/container reordering (TABS04/TABGRP01/CONT06 all re-sort the strip on insert+move), so 'index + 1' at :1079 may no longer point at the original tab -> closes an innocent tab or leaves the original behind -> strip re-renders looking collapsed/lost. Capture the original widget pointer and close THAT, not a positional guess. (b) LOST STRIP STATE — the foreign EngineTab can't carry the group tag (comment at :1048: 'a foreign tab drops it (no slot to hang the tag on)') and the pin flag is never transferred — if the source tab was pinned or inside a collapsed group, the swap drops that state and normalizePinnedBlock/group-collapse repaints the strip; a tab group losing its member or a group auto-collapsing on the remove/insert signals reads as 'tabs collapsed'. Decide honest semantics: engine tabs either keep group+pin (add the slot) or the task documents the drop — but the STRIP must never corrupt. (c) TWO-LEVEL STRIP — the CONT06 guard refuses only while a NON-default container level is active; on the default level the append+move+close still re-filters: does the new EngineTab register in the level-2 set and in m_locationBars correctly? (d) if 'collapsed' means the page AREA collapses (EngineTab content zero-height/width), check the tab widget's sizePolicy/layout on the EngineTab path instead. Repro FIRST (normal tab -> servo swap on a multi-tab strip, incl. pinned + grouped + container variants — record which variant collapses), then fix the real cause. Verify: swap to servo and back on tabs that are plain, pinned, grouped (expanded AND collapsed), container-bound: strip never loses/reorders other tabs, the swapped tab keeps its slot and strip state survives (or drops only what the task documents); tst_tabwidget legs for the swap in each strip-state variant; make check PASS.

### CRASH01

Arora keeps crashing — GPU-process CHECK in QtWebEngine — user report. Kernel journal shows the SAME crash signature 10x today (19:05 x5, 20:30-20:32 x5 — the 20:30 burst killed the run the user restarted at 20:35): 'Chrome_InProcGp[pid] trap int3 ip:...e6481 in libQt6WebEngineCore.so.6.12.0' — a FIXED offset (0x76e6481 base-relative) every time = ONE Chromium CHECK site firing in the in-process GPU thread (Chrome_InProcGp). int3 = deliberate CHECK/DCHECK abort, not a memory fault — and in-proc GPU means one GPU assertion kills the whole browser. Known adjacent: task notes already record a 'pre-existing Vulkan teardown CHECK' worked around with QTWEBENGINE_CHROMIUM_FLAGS=--disable-gpu in test runs — same family, but these traps fire mid-session while browsing (user on thinkbroadband speedtest — canvas/WebGL load), not just at exit. SECOND SUSPECT — sandbox interaction: the 20:35 launch runs under an outer bwrap (ARORA_SANDBOXED) and AppArmor logged 'unpriv_bwrap DENIED cap_sys_admin' for arora at 20:35:54 — Chromium's own process sandbox needs namespaces too; if the outer sandbox blocks them, the GPU-process init can hit exactly this class of CHECK. Also logged: 'Fontconfig error: Cannot load default config file' under the same launch — sandbox env is already leaky. Task: (a) CAPTURE THE CHECK — rerun under the same conditions with QTWEBENGINE_CHROMIUM_FLAGS=--enable-logging=stderr (plus --vmodule or --log-level=0 if needed) until it traps, and pull the CHECK text + file:line from stderr/syslog; if bwrap is implicated, compare a sandboxed vs unsandboxed run (the AppArmor denial may be fatal to GPU init only on some paths). (b) root-cause vs candidates: Vulkan init/teardown on this driver, Wayland/GL context, sandboxed-namespace denial (unpriv_bwrap), WebGL canvas teardown — pick by the captured CHECK text, not by guessing. (c) FIX if it's ours (flag ordering in applyChromiumFlags — don't blindly add --disable-gpu, that kills WebGL/canvas accel globally; prefer surgical flags: --disable-vulkan, --disable-gpu-sandbox, --in-process-gpu vs split GPU process, or the ozone/GL backend pick — document why the chosen one is right) — if it's a pure Qt 6.12.0 bug, CONTAIN it: GPU-process isolation flag so a GPU CHECK can't take the browser down, or detect-and-relaunch-path; document the upstream bug id. (d) verify: same-session stress (speedtest/WebGL pages + tab churn + suspend/resume) with zero Chrome_InProcGp int3 in the journal; the previously-trapping session shape runs clean; make check PASS.

### CRASH02

Arora exits silently around a failed print — second crash report, DIFFERENT signature than CRASH01. Evidence: launch log ends at ~21:13 with 'QPainter::begin(): Returned false' + 'Failure to print on device: Could not open printer for painting.' — user had browsed telex.hu; process then vanished with ZERO crash trace (no kernel trap since 20:32, no coredump, no OOM line). The print path (browsermainwindow.cpp:1975 printRequested) is: new QPrinter(HighResolution) → QPrintDialog::exec → view->print(printer) async — 'QPainter::begin(): Returned false' comes from the print/preview paint path when the printer can't be acquired (no CUPS printer configured on this box is plausible). Suspects for the silent exit, in order: (a) print-path abort — a qFatal/Q_ASSERT/CHECK in the Qt print path or our code after the paint-device failure (SIGABRT leaves no kernel 'traps:' line — consistent with what we see); (b) the async printFinished/lifetime dance — printer is guarded by a QObject child of the view; if the view/tab closed mid-print the guard+printer teardown order could double-free or paint on a dead device; (c) bwrap --die-with-parent — the launcher wrapper bash dying kills the sandboxed arora with NO crash trace at all (check whether the parent bash exited ~21:13); (d) clean-exit misread — verify the app didn't just quit (window-manager log shows GNOME warnings ~21:11-21:16 but nothing arora-specific). Task: (1) reproduce — Ctrl+P AND print-preview on a system with no configured printer (or CUPS stopped) while logging; confirm which suspect actually kills the process. (2) make printing NEVER fatal — guard the paint-device acquisition, degrade gracefully with an error message when no printer exists, fix any lifetime ordering in the printerGuard path. (3) crash observability — this death left nothing to diagnose; add a minimal crash reporter: install handlers for SIGSEGV/SIGABRT (and catch unhandled exceptions) that write a marker+backtrace to a file or stderr before dying, so silent exits stop being undiagnosable. Verify: print dialog on no-printer system shows an error and app stays alive; print on a real/pdf printer works; induced fault produces a trace; make check PASS.

### REFER01

Cross-site Referer rewritten to the TARGET's origin — breaks embeds — user report: embedded YouTube on hup.hu fails with error 152-4 ('video unavailable', direct youtube.com fine) because Arora rewrites the iframe's Referer to https://www.youtube.com/ instead of https://hup.hu/, failing YouTube's embed-origin check. Root cause confirmed: NavigationPolicy::rewrittenReferer (navigationpolicy.cpp:~1056) — at level 1 (RefererTrimmed, the default) the cross-site branch returns refererOrigin(target); strict-origin-when-cross-origin semantics require refererOrigin(source). Task: (a) fix — return refererOrigin(source) for the cross-site trimmed case (same-site + downgrade + level>=2 empty behavior unchanged); keep the clamp property — never send more than the incoming header/engine decided (empty incoming header stays untouched, minRefererLevel honored). (b) rust twin — the rustcore 'referer_apply' policyCall path duplicates this logic; check it for the same inversion and fix in lockstep (if rust lives in the rust lane, land the C++ fix + file the parity note). (c) regression tests — assert per level: cross-site trimmed -> 'https://hup.hu/' style SOURCE origin (the current code sends target origin — test goes red first); level>=2 -> absent; level 0 -> full source URL; https->http downgrade -> absent; same-site unchanged; empty incoming header -> untouched. Plus an end-to-end leg: a page embedding a cross-site iframe sees the iframe request carry the source-origin Referer (a local two-origin harness or the existing policy test seam — YouTube-embed-shaped case as the named scenario). (d) verify: embedded video on hup.hu plays (or at minimum the iframe request carries https://hup.hu/ Referer); make check PASS.

### SRCH10

Backspace must be able to clear the URL/search field — user report: 'when I push the backspace button it should delete the entire line, not stop the delete'. Two-part spec: (a) THE BUG — 'not stop the delete' reads like the omnibox's inline-completion fighting deletion: repeated backspaces never reach empty because the completer/suggestion path re-inserts a completion suffix (or closes the popup and eats the keypress). Trace the real mechanism — is the HistoryCompleter/OmniboxSuggestions re-completing after each deletion (suggest-on-textChanged fires even when text shrinks), is the first backspace consumed just dropping the popup, is an inline-suggestion suffix being re-selected? Fix so deletion is monotonic: while the user is actively deleting, suggestions must NOT re-extend the field (suppress completion-on-shrink for that edit burst — Chrome's behavior: backspace kills the inline suggestion AND doesn't resuggest until the user types forward again). (b) THE FEATURE — user's literal ask: a single Backspace should delete the ENTIRE line in the url/search bar. Ship it as: Backspace on a field whose text is unchanged since focus/select-all (the just-focused or just-navigated state, where Chrome/FF clear-with-one-key is expected) clears the whole field; once the user edits mid-text, backspace stays char-wise (don't make every backspace nuke the line — that breaks normal editing; document the chosen rule). Apply identically to LocationBar AND ToolbarSearch/SearchLineEdit so the two fields behave alike; keep Escape-restores-url (locationbar.cpp:360) working. (c) tests: tst leg — field with text + backspace at end -> empty (or per the documented rule); held/repeated backspace reaches empty even with live completer (negative control on the re-suggest bug); mid-edit backspace still deletes single chars. Verify: the user's scenario — click into url bar, backspace -> whole line gone; typing mid-text backspaces normally; completer can't pin text; make check PASS.

### ENG12

Servo backend crash on tab reload — user report: 'When I wanted to reload the tab with servo (it was empty), it crashed.' Repro: switch a tab to the Servo backend, reload the (empty/Untitled) tab -> app crash.

Spec: (a) reproduce + capture the crash — run under gdb/catchsegv or read the core; identify whether the fault is in the servo-embed adapter (spikes/servo/servo-embed), the EngineView reload path calling into a null/dead servo view, or a shutdown/reload race; (b) guard the reload path honestly — reloading an empty servo tab (no loaded URL, possibly no servo surface created yet) must be a no-op or load the pending URL, never a crash; (c) audit sibling servo entry points for the same unguarded assumption (stop/back/forward/reload on empty or mid-init servo tabs); (d) if the crash is inside servo's own code on an empty surface, add the Qt-side guard anyway and note the upstream bug.

Verify: reload on empty servo tab does not crash (10x), reload on a loaded servo tab still works, switching backends mid-tab stays stable, make check PASS.

DONE (2026-10-12): reproduction found the real fault — not the reload itself but the SECOND se_init: libservo v0.7.0 parks Opts in a process-global OnceCell (components/config/opts.rs:279 "Already initialized"), the panic cannot unwind across the extern "C" ABI, so any second ServoView (re-swap after switching back, second servo tab, fresh tab after closing one) hard-aborts the process; an empty-tab reload merely followed the swap that spent the single slot. Fix on the Qt side (shipped): a mutex-guarded process-wide slot (Free→InUse→Exhausted) in servobackend.cpp — createPage refuses a second page so engine-tab creation fails cleanly with a status-bar message, cbWake checks the live-view pointer against se_destroy-time wakes (UAF on the raw userdata), and a spent slot is never recycled since even a failed se_init may have consumed Opts. Upstream-side fix landed in spikes/servo/servo-embed/src/lib.rs (INITIALIZED gate + catch_unwind → nullptr) for the next artifact build; the pinned .so predates it. Verified per spec: ./arora --servo-smoke — 10x reload on empty servo tab + sibling entry points (stop/back/forward/zoom/js), real load + reload, mid-tab backend switching both directions, second-instance refusal — PASS, exit 0; make check rc=0.

### PERF01

Per-request JSON policy verdict on the IO thread — memoize within a page load (filed by AUDIT02). `PrivacyRequestInterceptor::interceptRequest` calls `NavigationPolicy::evaluate` for EVERY interceptor-visible request: it marshals a JSON manifest (url, firstParty, resourceType, method, headers, scope, tor flag, min referer level) and parses a JSON verdict via `rc_policy_evaluate` on the WebEngine IO thread — tens of calls per page load on a busy site. That's the ENG02 architecture working as designed, not a bug, but the serialize→FFI→parse cost is paid per subresource. Task: design and ship a bounded memoization layer — e.g. a per-(url, firstParty, resourceType, headers-fingerprint) verdict cache scoped to the navigation's first-party page, or hoist the decision surface that varies per request (only a handful of inputs actually change between subrequests of the same page). Constraints: the IO-thread lock rules still hold (cache must be populated/served without crossing to GUI), a verdict must never go stale across a settings/snapshot flip (key on the snapshot generation), OTR/tor scopes must never share entries with normal scope, and referer/cookie-relevant headers must be part of the key. Measure first: instrument a page load to count evaluate() calls and wall time before choosing the cache shape. Verify: fewer FFI calls per page load under --adblock-smoke/--privacy-smoke workloads, no verdict regression in tst_navigationpolicy + interceptor tests, make check PASS.

Servo reload does nothing on a loaded page — user report: with a page open on a servo tab, reload has no effect (ENG12 fixed the empty-tab reload crash; this is the live reload path). DONE (2026-10-12): the defect was not inside the servo backend at all — every user-facing reload path died before reaching it. (1) TabWidget::reloadTab/reloadAllTabs branched only on webView(index), which is nullptr on foreign-engine tabs, so the strip context menu and Reload All were silent no-ops; both now fall through to engineTab(index)->reload(). (2) The chrome path was unbound: currentChanged called mapper->updateCurrent(nullptr) for non-WebView tabs, and ServoPage::action() returned nullptr for everything, so the toolbar/menu/shortcut Reload was never wired; currentChanged now binds foreign->page(), addEngineTab feeds each page's standard actions to the shared mappers via addChild (same wiring as makeNewTabOnProfile), and ServoPage lazily exposes Back (enabled-state tracks canGoBack via urlChanged) and Reload. WebActionMapper::updateCurrent now honors the Engine::Page contract that an unmapped action reads DISABLED — previously a nullptr source left the root in whatever state the previous page left — and the destroyed-tracking connect moved ahead of the early return. Backend side: ServoView::reload() prefers the new se_reload C-ABI export (webview.reload() keeps session history intact — a same-url WebView::load pushes a duplicate entry); artifacts predating the export resolve it optionally and degrade to a same-url se_load, and an empty tab is a guarded no-op. ServoPage::reload() delegates to the view (the previous current-url -> load() round trip pushed phantom history entries). Verified: tst_engineswitch 23/23 incl new engineTabReload (strip dispatch reloads=1/2, chrome-action bind + disabled-state for unmapped Forward, cross-engine state no-leak); ./arora --servo-smoke PASS on the real backend incl new chrome-reload-loaded leg + reload-empty-10x; make check — check-warnings/supplychain/engine-audit/rustcoverage PASS, every suite program 0 failed except tst_ContainerManager::tabCookieIsolation (documented WebEngine cookie-commit flake, PASS standalone) and httponly+adblockrequestinterceptor 'test binary is not compiled' — a concurrent ENG16 run relinking binaries mid-suite, both PASS standalone.
