### ENG18

Servo engine: start page shows 'Error loading page' — user report: after switching a tab (or the default engine) to Servo, the tab that was showing qrc:/startpage.html renders an error page ('Error loading page / Could not ...'). qrc: is a Qt resource scheme — the Servo backend (libservo + the servo-embed shim) almost certainly can't fetch qrc: URLs; the engine tab hands the URL to Servo and it fails the load. Fix approach, in order of preference: (a) ROUTE-AROUND — when a tab lives on the Servo backend, rewrite internal qrc: page URLs (startpage, branded variants incl. ENG13's startpage-servo.html, tor page, error pages, any other app-internal qrc: htmls/* page) to a scheme/path Servo can actually serve — either extract the resource to a real file under a cache dir and load file://, or serve it through a scheme Servo does handle (data: for small self-contained pages is legit but check asset refs — startpage pulls css/js/images out of qrc too, so a bare data: document will lose them; extracting the whole qrc dir subtree to a temp/cache dir and using file:// keeps relative refs working). Rebuild-time watchpoint: the servo-branded start page (ENG13) already PICKS startpage-servo.html for servo tabs — same qrc: problem, same fix. (b) SHIM-LEVEL — if servo-embed's resource interceptor (ENG03: load_web_resource delegate exists) can see the request, answer qrc: loads from Qt resources inside the C ABI boundary — the shim runs in-process so it CAN read qrc via a small host callback; this keeps qrc: URLs working everywhere incl. user-typed ones. Pick whichever fits the existing backend architecture (WebEngine custom-scheme handlers already do this job for webengine tabs — find how qrc: is served there, maybe via qrc's built-in support or an interceptor, and mirror it). Also verify none of the OTHER app-internal pages fail the same way on servo: help/about, error pages, settings-in-tab if any, reader-mode shell, startpage variants for tor+servo branding. Verify: servo tab loads the start page correctly (real content, not the error page); swapping Chromium->Servo on a tab showing the start page re-renders it; servo-branded variant shows its own page; make check PASS.

DONE (2026-10-12): took approach (b), SHIM-LEVEL — the fetch pipeline consults the webview's load_web_resource delegate ahead of scheme dispatch, so interception covers qrc: documents AND subresources everywhere (typed urls included), with no extraction dir to manage. servo-embed gains se_set_resource_resolver(userdata, fn): a process-global host callback invoked synchronously inside se_spin() on the GUI thread for every resource load; a non-zero return claims the load and the shim streams the reply through WebResourceLoad::intercept + send_body_data + finish (status -> StatusCode when parseable, mime -> Content-Type header, borrowed body copied during the call). Registration is optional both directions — old artifacts miss the symbol (dlopen resolve leaves it null, pre-fix behavior) and old hosts never call it (RESOURCE_RESOLVER OnceLock stays empty, delegate early-returns into DoNotIntercept). http =1.5.0 added to the shim's Cargo.toml matching servo's own lockfile pin. Qt side: servobackend.cpp's servoResourceResolve answers qrc: out of QFile(":/...") with MatchExtension mime sniffing, claims missing resources as 404 and '..' paths as 403 rather than falling through to Servo's own failing fetch; everything else returns 0 untouched. Second half of the bug: even with bytes served, startpage.js binds a QWebChannel "arora" object that foreign backends cannot provide — the page rendered an empty skeleton. tabwidget.cpp injects engineTabAroraScript() on qrc: commits (EngineTab loadFinished): a window.arora shim whose translate() serves the same JavaScriptAroraObject translation domains, searchUrl() reuses the live context engine's template, darkChrome tracks the real palette, and update() is re-invoked — pages that already defined arora are untouched. Other internal pages audited: all qrc content (startpage variants, notfound/certerror/dirlist) rides the one resolver; certerror/dirlist are additionally read host-side via QFile so they never needed engine support; about/help are widgets, reader mode injects into the live page (no shell document). Verified: --servo-smoke PASS headless incl new legs — startpage-webengine, swap-startpage-commit (Chromium->Servo swap re-commits the same qrc url), swap-startpage-title ("Welcome to Arora!" reachable only if the startpage.js subresource fetch resolved AND the injected shim drove update()), qrc-commit+qrc-title for startpage-servo.html, startpage-tor.html and notfound.html — plus all pre-existing ENG12/14/15/16 legs still green; make check — all 4 gates PASS (warnings/supplychain/engine-audit/rustcoverage) + 80 programs 0 failed offscreen LIBGL_ALWAYS_SOFTWARE=1.

### ENG20

Servo engine swap aborts the whole app — user report: 'changing to servo it crashed again. fix it.' REPRODUCED on master 2026-10-12: normal window, "Reload in Servo" -> instant process abort. Captured stderr: `thread '<unnamed>' panicked at ~/.cargo/git/checkouts/servo-*/components/paint/painter.rs:416:14: assertion left == right failed — left: 1282, right: 0` followed by `panic in a function that cannot unwind` -> abort, thrown across the `se_spin` extern boundary (spikes/servo/servo-embed/src/lib.rs ~371) called from a posted QObject event. TWO defects, fix both:

(a) FFI BOUNDARY — ENG12 hardened only se_init_inner; EVERY other `#[no_mangle] pub extern "C" fn se_*` in servo-embed/src/lib.rs still lets panics cross the FFI (se_spin ~371, se_load ~390, se_resize ~407, se_mouse_move ~419, se_mouse_button ~431, se_wheel ~445, se_key ~462, and everything below — audit the whole export list incl. se_set_resource_resolver callbacks invoked from Rust threads). Wrap each entry in std::panic::catch_unwind(AssertUnwindSafe(...)) mapping panic -> error status; add a `poisoned: AtomicBool` to SeInstance set on any catch so subsequent exports short-circuit to error/no-op — a panicking servo instance degrades to a dead tab, NEVER kills the process. Keep the default panic hook's stderr print for diagnosis. NOTE the pinned libservo_embed.so predates ENG12's upstream change — the artifact must be REBUILT for this to ship (cargo build in spikes/servo/servo-embed, --offline if the servo git checkout is already vendored); a Qt-side belt-and-suspenders guard is still welcome but se_* catching is the shipped fix this time since the panic is inside spin_event_loop.

(b) ROOT CAUSE — the painter assert `left == right` (1282 vs 0) is a size/surface mismatch at first paint: almost certainly se_resize is never called (or called with 0) before the first se_spin paints, so servo's painter sees a real content size (1282px content) against a 0-stride/height surface. Trace servobackend.cpp's sizing path at EngineTab creation — when does se_resize fire relative to the first spin/frame? Reproduce under RUST_BACKTRACE=1 with the harness (spikes/servo/harness or a --servo-smoke leg); fix the ordering so the first paint sees a coherent nonzero size — defer spin/present until a real resize lands, clamp se_resize to >=1x1, or create the webview with the widget's actual size — whichever the trace proves.

Qt side: the poisoned instance must surface as an honest tab state — status message / crashed-pane placeholder (reuse ENG12's 'not available' surfacing or a new 'crashed' state), NO silent se_spin retry loop on a poisoned instance.

Verify: (1) panic-injection leg — force a panic through one se_* export (temporary or test-only export) and prove the app survives with an error return; (2) 'Reload in Servo' on a real window no longer aborts — either the page renders (if (b)'s ordering fix lands) or the tab shows the honest failure while the browser stays up; (3) --servo-smoke PASS or honest SKIP; make check PASS.

Servo tab renders nothing — user report: 'when i change to servo engine, basically nothing renders the page. just empty space.' Distinct from ENG20's abort — the swap now (or post-ENG20) produces a live but EMPTY tab: no page pixels, no crash. The real question this task answers: does a frame EVER reach the Qt side? Resolution (this run): the trace answered (b) — frames DO reach the Qt side; the failure was upstream at presentation time. Instrumented notify_new_frame_ready (temporary probe, since removed): read_to_image produced only clear-color frames under the pinned artifact because libservo_embed.so (built 10-12) predated ENG20's se_resize fix — routing through WebView::resize() runs Painter::resize_rendering_context (surfman resize + set_rect + set_document_view txn + RepaintReason::Resize + fresh root display list); the old artifact resized the RenderingContext directly, leaving webrender compositing a stale 640x480 document view — blank pixels, and on the debug artifact a GL error at Painter::render's debug_assert -> caught panic -> poisoned instance -> every later call a no-op -> permanently empty tab. ENG20's containment+fix source was already on master (accidentally committed with staged WIP in 6d38e69 — ENG22's stash-restore is a no-op; the stash still holds only the superseded ui-lane files). Rebuilt the artifact (cargo build, ~7s incremental — dep tree cached, lld): servo tabs now paint real pages — shim probe showed 274k-511k/517k non-white px on qrc startpages through the real --servo-smoke path, and the standalone harness reads #cc3333/38-colors on a colored fixture deterministically after a 2s post-Complete settle-pump (content frames trail LoadStatus::Complete by a beat — a race, not missing frames; PaintProxy::send wakes the embedder on every NewWebRenderFrameReady so the app's pump keeps painting). Permanent guard: new --servo-smoke leg 'servo-renders-pixels' grabs page->view() and asserts >100 distinct sampled pixels — the exact reported symptom.

### ENG23

Servo tab panics at painter.rs:416 assert_no_gl_error on the real desktop — user repro on master HEAD build (/tmp/arora-head/arora, fresh libservo_embed.so incl ENG21+ENG22): tab swap to Servo shows the new honest disabled-tab UI ('The Servo engine crashed — this tab is disabled') — ENG20's containment WORKS — but launch log line 206: `thread '<unnamed>' panicked at components/paint/painter.rs:416:14: assertion left == right failed` (GL_INVALID_OPERATION) then 'instance is poisoned'. So the GL error survives on a real Wayland session even though headless tests (LIBGL_ALWAYS_SOFTWARE=1) and --servo-smoke pass. Spec: (a) REPRODUCE in a real display session (not offscreen — the failure is display-dependent; xvfb/wayland env may both be needed to bisect); (b) ROOT CAUSE — servo's painter GL path vs what surface/context servo-embed hands it: SoftwareRenderingContext with a surfman swap chain vs a real GL context on the desktop; check whether GL-accelerated compositing is attempted where only software readback is supported, whether the shared GL context attributes mismatch, or the widget's surface (QOpenGLWidget? native?) isn't current when paint runs — ENG21's resize routing is landed, so it's past 0x0 surfaces; (c) FIX honestly — if servo-embed's model is software-render+readback, force/verify that path end-to-end rather than letting servo bind a GL context it can't use (the 'GL error' may BE a GL context created where none should exist); if GL compositing is intended, fix the context/surface contract; (d) keep containment + disabled-tab UX as the last line, not the fix. Verify: real-window servo tab renders pixels (not just headless), no panic lines on desktop session, --servo-smoke + render legs still pass, make check PASS.

Landed (attempts 1-5, interrupted twice by tree RSS cap — resumed from working tree):

- Root cause: `SoftwareRenderingContext` is a readback context (every frame goes through `read_to_image` into a QImage, never presented) but surfman's `Connection::new()` still picks the session display's hardware adapter on a real desktop (wayland/x11 → iris). Sticky driver-side errors (llvmpipe/iris raise GL_INVALID_OPERATION on surfman swap-chain resize paths, outside any paint) sit on that context until `Painter::render`'s `debug_assert_eq!(get_error(), NO_ERROR)` at painter.rs:416 trips — display-dependent, which is why headless stayed clean.
- Fix (spec option c — software render+readback is the shim's whole contract): `ScopedSoftwareGlEnv` in se_init_inner sets `LIBGL_ALWAYS_SOFTWARE=1` before Mesa's eglInitialize, *poisons* `WAYLAND_DISPLAY` (wl_display_connect(nullptr) silently falls back to the `wayland-0` socket when the var is merely removed — observed under Xvfb: wayland ctx created, make_current failed, surfman Drop-panicked "Contexts must be destroyed explicitly"), removes `DISPLAY`/`EGL_PLATFORM` so surfman lands on `mesa_surfaceless`/llvmpipe; restores env on drop with a one-shot display-restore retry for non-Mesa stacks. `try_rendering_context` wraps each attempt in catch_unwind so surfman's mid-init drop-panic degrades to the retry instead of aborting se_init.
- Foreign-context hygiene: `release_current_gl_context()` (RTLD_NOLOAD eglMakeCurrent/glXMakeCurrent, no-op without a GL stack) unbinds (a) any host GLX/EGL context current on the init thread — Qt/RHI under xcb holds one and Mesa's eglMakeCurrent then fails (was killing se_init under Xvfb), and (b) our surfaceless EGL context on the way out of every export (ffi_guard epilogue) + se_destroy — leaving it current broke the host's next QRhi make-current ('QRhiGles2: Failed to make context current' bursts, window stops compositing).
- Sticky-error drain: `drain_gl_error` (makes the painter's context current — exports release it — then get_error) around both sides of paint in `notify_new_frame_ready`, after `se_resize`'s surfman swap-chain ops (the actual 0x502 producer — caught live under Xvfb: pre-paint drain logged `0x502`/`0x501` where the assert used to fire), and before `se_destroy`'s drop (webrender debug-checks GL inside drop).
- Verification handle: new `se_gl_renderer` FFI + `SeInstance::gl_renderer` (sampled once at se_init via glow) + `ServoView::glRenderer()` + `--servo-smoke` leg `servo-software-renderer` asserting llvmpipe/swrast/softpipe/software — verify, don't assume the env pin took. gleam/glow pinned to servo's lockfile versions. Containment + disabled-tab UX untouched (still the last line).
- Verified: `--servo-smoke` PASS all legs under ambient session env (offscreen Qt, wayland vars present) AND real X11 (xvfb-run — the panic environment): `swap-to-servo`, `servo-renders-pixels`, `resize-spin-survived` PASS, 'released a foreign GL context' at init, GL renderer = llvmpipe, no painter panic, no QRhi collateral. `make check`: all gates PASS (warnings/supplychain/engine-audit/rustcoverage), autotests green except tst_browsermainwindow — 10 pre-existing ui-lane leg FAILs (menuIcons/devToolsSubmenu/privateStartPage/readerModeContent×5/homeButton/downloadsSidebarMinWidth = documented MENU06/TOR12/DOWN04 fallout) plus a post-Totals QtWebEngine teardown SIGSEGV (RWHVQtDelegateItem→QQuickWidget→QQuickRenderControlPrivate::windowDestroyed null deref after 'WebEnginePage still not deleted' — reproduces identically with the servo artifact removed entirely: NOT ENG23).

### PRINT01

Print Preview shows a blank gray pane with '1 / 0' pages — user screenshot. Root cause: BrowserMainWindow::filePrintPreview (~2317) uses stock QPrintPreviewDialog, whose paintRequested expects SYNC painting into a QPrinter paint device — but WebEngine printing is async via printToPdf, so the preview gets zero pages. Spec: (a) replace the stock dialog path: generate the page's PDF via Engine::Page/QWebEnginePage::printToPdf (or equivalent) with the dialog's page layout, then render pages into a real preview — master has pdfview/pdfworker + hayro rasterizer (PDF01/02) — reuse it to paint pages (QPdfView if the QtPdf module is present at ~/Qt — check, else the in-tree rasterizer); keep the existing toolbar UX (zoom, page nav, print button -> feeds the same PDF to the real printer via CUPS/QPrinter path or hands the PDF to the print dialog); (b) plain Print (printRequested ~2336) already works async — keep it; preview must reflect actual pagination/margins from the generated PDF, not a re-rendered approximation; (c) covers filePrintPreview + any Ctrl+P preview entry; works for webengine tabs; engine tabs (servo) can't printToPdf — disable preview honestly there with a note; (d) private/tor tabs: print path identical, no extra logging. Verify: preview shows real rendered pages + page count, printed output matches, PDF output selectable, servo tab refuses honestly, tst leg if the dialog is drivable (or manual-verify + doc), make check PASS.

- Resolution: new PrintPreviewDialog (src/printpreviewdialog.{h,cpp}) replaces QPrintPreviewDialog — page-mode printToPdf under the dialog's QPageLayout into a per-generation scratch file, rendered by the shell PdfView (bwrap-worker hayro, in-process fallback); Page Setup regenerates, Print goes through PdfCanvas::printPages, Save as PDF copies the generated file; a tab hosting the shell document view previews/prints the staged PDF; engine tabs get an honest refusal (no printToPdf). Gotchas found: QtWebEngine serializes print jobs per page — a printToPdf issued while an orphaned job is in flight is rejected outright (the dialog retries 3x250ms); printToPdf faithfully renders adblock cosmetic hiding — the ~/.qttest store's stale `##body` custom rule blanked the fixture, so --printpreview-smoke disables AdBlockManager. Verified: --printpreview-smoke PASS offscreen (3 pages, nonblank raster, landscape regen, print handoff writes %PDF-), tst_browsermainwindow::printPaths PASS (mid-generation close, real page count, servo refusal legs).

### ENG24

Servo unrecoverable after a poison — user: 'since the crash of the servo cannot switch to Servo again, no auto restart engine.' Once se_is_poisoned trips (ENG20 containment), every later se_* call no-ops for the PROCESS lifetime — the disabled tab UX correctly refuses, but there's no way back to a working servo short of restarting Arora. Spec: (a) determine the poison's scope — is it per-instance (then destroying+recreating the embed instance recovers) or process-global in the .so (then recovery needs a real restart path: dlclose/re-dlopen if safe, or a documented 'restart servo' action that respawns the engine); (b) implement the honest recovery: a poisoned servo engine should be re-creatable — the disabled-tab UI offers 'Retry Servo' (or auto-recreate on next servo swap) that instantiates a FRESH embed instance/loads the lib anew; the tab that crashed stays disabled unless re-created — the recovery must not resurrect the poisoned object graph; (c) poisoned state must not silently clear — only cleared by a verified-clean new instance (self-check on init: create+resize+paint probe, mark ready only after a real frame or clean probe); (d) ENG23's painter fix may make crashes rare but this stays necessary — a panic path with no recovery is a dead feature; coordinate with ENG23's tree state when it lands. Verify: crash a servo tab (panic-injection leg), switch back to Chromium (works), re-enable Servo -> engine reinitializes and renders (not permanently poisoned), poisoned tab itself still refused honestly, --servo-smoke legs incl. SE_SELFTEST_PANIC recovery, make check PASS.

Landed (attempts 1-2 — attempt 1 implemented + smoke-verified, was OOM-killed mid `make check`; attempt 2 resumed from the working tree):

- Scope answer (spec a): the poison is per-SeInstance, and the ENGINE cannot be rebuilt in-process by design — libservo v0.7.0 Opts is a process-global OnceLock, and a C probe proved dlclose is a no-op on the artifact (surviving servo threads' TLS destructors pin the image; re-dlopen serves the same image with the same INITIALIZED gate). So the honest recovery is servo's own model, not a reload: the engine survives the crash, a fresh webview replaces the dead one — the same shape as Chromium respawning a crashed renderer on the surviving browser process.
- Shim (spikes/servo/servo-embed/src/lib.rs): the Servo engine moved from one-shot locals to a GUI-thread `thread_local! { RefCell<Option<ManuallyDrop<Servo>>> }` — built once on the first se_init (Opts/config_dir/event-loop waker consumed there only), cloned per later se_init. `INSTANCE_ACTIVE: Cell<bool>` replaces the INITIALIZED AtomicBool — claimed per se_init, handed back by se_destroy (freed FIRST inside se_destroy so a panicking drop can't strand the slot; the leaked box only wastes memory) and by a failed/panicking init. New optional `se_shutdown` export drops the engine handle (constellation Exit + shutdown spin) — ManuallyDrop keeps TLS teardown from running it at thread exit where servo's own TLS may already be gone (a panic in a TLS dtor aborts).
- Waker routing: the engine's event-loop waker was bound at the FIRST ServoBuilder::build and holds that userdata for the engine's lifetime, so every generation's callbacks share ONE indirection cell — `callbacks.userdata` is now `&g_liveView` and every trampoline resolves the cell to whichever view is live (viewFor); dead/stale wakes resolve null and drop.
- Qt side (servobackend): the slot is now Free→InUse→Free (a destroyed view hands it back), → Exhausted only when se_init itself fails — terminal for the session since a pre-ENG24 artifact refuses every second init identically. Fresh-instance self-check in the ctor: a resize+spin probe — if it poisons, the instance is destroyed immediately (the surviving engine is dead too → Exhausted; never half-alive). New `instanceInUse()` splits the refused states for pageUnavailableReason ("already in use — one tab at a time" vs "the engine could not be started — restart Arora"). `se_shutdown` plumbed as ServoBackend::shutdownEngine wired to aboutToQuit while the GUI thread is intact (the smoke calls it explicitly since it never exec()s — without it servo threads outlive static teardown → pthread_mutex_destroy-busy SIGSEGV at exit).
- Crash UX (spec b): the poisoned paint surface now offers "Try Servo Again" (lazy ensureCrashUi — bottom-parked QPushButton) → restartRequested → EngineTab::restartEngine, QUEUED so the button's clicked() emission finishes before its owning view is deleted. restartEngine tears the dead page+view down FIRST (the slot only frees on destroy), rebuilds page+view on a fresh SeInstance, reloads the last committed url, emits pageRecreated (TabWidget::addEngineTab re-binds the new page's actions into the WebActionMappers + updateCurrent), and on failure shows an honest could-not-restart notice — the swap-to-Chromium path still works; a crashed tab stays disabled unless retried or closed (spec c — the poison flag dies with its object, never silently clears).
- --servo-smoke legs rewritten: reswap-refused → reswap-recovers + recovery-fresh-instance + recovery-renders (>100 distinct pixels, unpoisoned) + second-page-refused (live) + re-poisoned/retry-button-present/retry-recreates/retry-reloads (a THIRD SeInstance via the button) + slot-freed + post-destroy-recreates; retry-offered + second-page-refused-live on the crashed-but-live tab.
- Verified: --servo-smoke all legs PASS + clean EXIT=0 under the ambient session env (llvmpipe, no panic lines past the injected selftest panics); make check — check-warnings/supplychain/engine-audit/rustcoverage all PASS, suite ~80 binaries green except documented pre-existing tst_browsermainwindow ui-lane legs + PRINT01's chromeSlots + its post-Totals WebEngine teardown segv (killed post-Totals after ~13min of handler spam; standalone run: same 48/11) + tabbar verticalGripReset (TABS16) + tabwidget tabTransfer flake (PASS standalone).

### SEC25

Extension subsystem hardening — user asked 'can we strengthen it?' Code review verdict: the base is sound (MV3-only, one-shot consent gate, rc_ext_manifest_check deny-by-default, structure+hash package check, no auto-update-apply, OTR/tor excluded) — land the three remaining real gaps, NO refactor needed (extensionmanager is compact+layered): (a) INTEGRITY PIN — at install, record a hash of the extension's manifest+file tree (sha256 over manifest.json + script/content files — or the existing rc_ext_verify_package hash if it covers contents); at startup/session-load compare installed dir vs stored hash and flag tampered extensions honestly ('files changed since install — review or remove'), not silently load; bundled Bitwarden (EXT07) participates too; (b) PERMISSION-DRIFT ON UPDATE — the update-apply path (UpdateResult installable -> user applies) must run the manifest check on the NEW package and re-present dangerous/host-permission changes in the review dialog when they grow — original consent never covers escalated grants (added permissions/host patterns listed explicitly, 'new since installed version'); (c) BROAD-HOST WARNING — the review dialog visually escalates broad host_permissions (<all_urls>, *://*/*, http/https wildcard roots) — a distinct warning row/icon vs the plain permission list, both at install-review and update-drift-review; (d) everything else stays: MV3-only, one-shot consent, tor/OTR exclusion, 'structure-only sig' honesty notes remain accurate — update them if wording changes. Verify: tamper a file in an installed extension dir -> flagged on next start; update fixture adding <all_urls> -> re-review shows the delta; review dialog screenshot/grab shows broad-host warning; legit flows unchanged; tst legs, make check PASS (memory-aware: focused tests if the cap is tight).

Landed (attempts 1-2 — attempt 1 implemented + passed the focused suite, OOM-killed mid settingsdialog rerun; attempt 2 finished the sweep-timing gap, fixed a merge fallout test hang, verified):

- (a) Integrity pin: `installFinished` records `extensionTreeHash()` — hex sha256 over `<relpath>\0<contents>\0` of every file in the installed tree, sorted — under `extensions/integrity/<storageName>/<id>` in QSettings. `verifyInstalledIntegrity()` re-hashes each installed extension once the profile wires up and, on mismatch, sets `ExtensionInfo::tampered`, emits `extensionTampered` + `errorOccurred` ('files changed since install — review or remove'), and emits `changed()` — flagged, still loaded (the engine owns load state; review/remove is the user's call). Pre-pin installs adopt their current tree as baseline. Uninstall clears pin + flag; an applied update re-pins through the same `installFinished` path; bundled installs participate identically. Status column reads 'Tampered' — an 'Enabled' row would hide the flag; details pane shows the warning text.
- Sweep timing (attempt 2): Chromium's installed-extension registry fills asynchronously and QWebEngineExtensionManager exposes no ready signal, so `scheduleIntegritySweep` retries (250ms x20) while the install dir holds entries but `extensions()` is still empty — a swept-nothing result can no longer pass a tampered tree off as clean.
- (b) Drift review: `SettingsDialog::extensionUpdateCheckFinished` reads the installed extension's manifest (via `ExtensionInfo::path` + `inspectManifest`) and passes it to `ExtensionReviewDialog::review(..., installed)` — new ctor/review param, nullptr on first install. Added permissions+host patterns list as 'New since installed version %1'; newly-added dangerous grants get a second 'New high-risk access' row and still require their per-item consent checkboxes (approve stays disabled until all ticked).
- (c) Broad-host warning: `isBroadHostPattern()` — `<all_urls>` or a bare `*` host (`*://*/*`, `http://*/*`, `https://*/*`, `file:///*`…); `*.host` subdomain wildcards stay scoped. Broad rows carry SP_MessageBoxWarning in the permission list plus a dedicated 'Broad host access requested' escalation row in the warnings list — applies to install and update reviews alike.
- Merge fallout fixed along the way: the ui-work merge left `refreshExtensions()` writing Description into column 3 over Source (5-column tree: Name/Version/Status/Source/Description) — now column 4; and `tst_settingsdialog::extensionVersionColumn` predated EXT05's consent gate on its lane — `loadExtension` without `grantConsent` emits `errorOccurred` → the dialog's modal warning hung the leg 300s under offscreen. Added the missing grant.
- Verified: clean qmake6+make, 0 warnings; tst_extensionmanager 20/20 (new legs integrityPin — install pins, tamper flags via signal + ExtensionInfo::tampered, hash determinism/sensitivity, uninstall clears; broadHostWarning — pattern matrix + icon on broad row only + escalation row; updateDriftReview — new-since delta, dangerous escalation row, consent still gated, no drift rows without baseline); tst_settingsdialog 52/52; `./arora --quit-after-load` rc=0; all offscreen LIBGL_ALWAYS_SOFTWARE=1. Focused suites per the memory-cap note (full make check OOM'd earlier runs at the 2048MB cap).

### TRUST14

Trust padlock in the URL bar + shield promoted to toolbar — user spec, verbatim mapping required: 'The shield should be moved out next to the url bar on the left, and add a small padlock where the shield is. If the site is trustable and https is ok, then green and locked padlock. If site is not untrusted, but not trusted or no info but https, then yellow locked padlock. If untrusted but https then red locked padlock. If the site has not https then always unlocked padlock, using the color as mentioned above. The shield always shows the settings of the active tab.'

Current state: SiteShieldButton lives INSIDE LocationBar (locationbar.cpp:63-64, addWidget LeftSide) showing adblock/JS-block state + SitePanel popup. Trust signals exist master-side: TlsVerifier::Status (None/Pending/Verified/Warning/Unverified/Refused — tlsverifier.h:57, rustls+webpki probe, honest Unverified-never-Warning), TrancoList (top-site list), DomainBlocklist (threat list), SiteDecisionStore, BrowserApplication::siteTrust{,Lookalike,Threatlist}Enabled() live gates (browserapplication.cpp ~1139).

Spec: (a) MOVE the shield — take SiteShieldButton out of the LocationBar's LeftSide and re-home it on the nav toolbar immediately LEFT of the location bar widget; it keeps its full behavior (badge, SitePanel popup) and MUST rebind to the active tab's WebView on every tab switch (user's explicit rule: 'shield always shows the settings of the active tab' — verify setWebView tracks currentChanged, new windows, torn tabs, private tabs); (b) PADLOCK in its place — a new small button INSIDE the URL bar where the shield sat (LeftSide), displaying: HTTPS + fully trusted (TLS Verified AND no threat-list/lookalike hit — a Tranco-listed or otherwise positively-verified host counts as trusted) => GREEN locked padlock; HTTPS + neutral (TLS Verified/Unverified/None with no negative signal, or no trust data — the 'not untrusted, not trusted / no info' tier) => YELLOW locked padlock; HTTPS + untrusted (TLS Warning verdict, threat-list hit, or lookalike hit — whichever the trust stack flags) => RED locked padlock; non-HTTPS always => UNLOCKED padlock colored by the same trust tier (green/yellow/red per the trust verdict of the site). Gate the whole surface on BrowserApplication::siteTrustEnabled() — off => padlock hides (or falls back to a plain neutral lock; implementer's call, document); (c) per-tab tracking — the padlock re-evaluates on navigation commit, tab switch, TlsVerifier::statusChanged for the current host, and trust-flag changes; Pending shows the neutral/yellow state (never red on an in-flight probe — honesty rule from the verifier); (d) padlock click opens a small info popup (or SitePanel — reuse if it already carries the TLS line) showing scheme/TLS status/trust reason; keep the unlock-click harmless (no certificate bypass affordance — info only); (e) HTTPS-but-invalid already handled by Chromium's interstitial — the padlock reflects the trust verdict, it doesn't replace cert-error pages; tor windows: TlsVerifier never probes under proxy — padlock shows the honest 'no probe' neutral state there, never a misleading green. (f) private tabs + OTR same behavior.

Verify: on a tranco-listed https site -> green locked padlock; a random https site with clean chain but no listing -> yellow locked; a forced Warning/threat-list fixture -> red locked; http:// -> unlocked colored per trust; shield sits left of the URL bar in the toolbar and its popup always reflects the ACTIVE tab across switches; siteTrustEnabled()=false hides/pads neutrally; grabs reviewed (normal+tor+private); tst legs for the padlock state matrix + shield rebind; make check PASS.

DONE (2026-10-14, 4 runner attempts — kept dying to the 3600s timeout / 2048MB RSS cap mid-`make check`; the 4th resumed, finished verification, and closed it out):

Implementation: new `SitePadlockButton` (src/locationbar/sitepadlock.{h,cpp}) occupies the LocationBar LeftSide slot the shield vacated. The whole decision table is the static `stateFor(url)` seam so tests exercise it without a window: siteTrustEnabled()=false -> PlainLocked/PlainUnlocked (plain chrome lock, still honest about the scheme — the "hides or falls back" implementer's choice documented in the header); non-http(s)/no-host -> PlainUnlocked; composite verdict red (threat list or lookalike) -> red, locked iff https; https -> LockedRed on TlsVerifier::Warning, LockedGreen only on Verified AND green verdict (Tranco rank or equivalent positive listing), else LockedYellow — so Pending/Unverified/Refused/None (incl. tor, never probed) can never wear green; http -> unlocked in the verdict's tier color. Icon is painted (no bundled padlock asset — same convention as ReaderButton): verdict colors come from SitePanel::trustLevelColor (theme-aware day/night). Re-evaluates on urlChanged/loadStarted/loadFinished, TlsVerifier::statusChanged, Tranco/DomainBlocklist updateFinished (also clears the shared trust-level cache), and themeNotifier chromeSchemeChanged (QueuedConnection — palette lands mid-flip). Click opens a QMenu-hosted QFrame: host, scheme/security line (incl. arora-cert-error/http-warning/site-block interstitial schemes), the TLS chip (verified+version / Warning class / Pending / honest Unverified, hidden when never probed), and the same SitePanel::trustSignalLines rows — info only, no cert-bypass affordance. All host-derived text is Qt::PlainText.

The rebindTrust lambda runs on QTabWidget::currentChanged AND TabWidget::tabsChanged (the latter catches same-index container refilters + engine swaps) — setWebView(currentTab()) so the badge+SitePanel always describe the ACTIVE tab; it also refresh()es the current bar's padlock (trust-flag flips apply live on next switch — the surface reads QSettings live like TRUST03's shield does; there is no settings-changed signal). ### EXT11

Extension-host DIAGNOSIS slice — read-only investigation + documentation, no product-code fix lands here (implementation is EXT12). User concern: 'if this is not working for Bitwarden it may occur that nothing will work.' Live-log facts already established: Bitwarden's MV3 background service worker fails registration ('Service worker registration failed. Status code: 15'), background.js throws on missing chrome.* surfaces (webNavigation.onCommitted, extension.getBackgroundPage, tabs.getCurrent) => popup spins forever waiting on a worker that never exists. Spec: (a) PLATFORM-WIDE CHECK — is ANY MV3 extension functional? Install a minimal test extension (tiny MV3 manifest + a content script mutating a page + an action popup) plus one more real-world MV3 extension if cheap to source; classify what works: content scripts, action popups, background SWs, storage, tabs API; (b) confirm Bitwarden's exact gap — which of its required surfaces are missing vs broken, whether a Qt-side lever exists (QWebEngineSettings attribute, Chromium --enable-features flag, install options) that enables extension SW registration — try the cheap levers, record each attempt + result; (c) rule out EXT08's popup host as the breakage (worker-side errors suggest not, but verify the popup view is on the right profile/context); (d) DELIVERABLE = a written decision: which stubs (if any) would make Bitwarden honestly functional vs fake-alive, what the honest degradation surface should say/show, what the platform-wide verdict is — document in the task notes + extension README notes. Verify: the health-check matrix is reproducible (documented steps/logs), every claim is measured, no code changes beyond diagnostics land in this slice; make check PASS.

EXT11 findings (full report: `.devin/EXT11-report.md`; harness: `./arora --mv3-health-smoke`, PASS = harness mechanics only): MV3 basics work — fixture SW runs as a real ServiceWorkerGlobalScope, CS injects, popup loads chrome-extension://, sendMessage round-trips CS↔SW↔popup, storage.local cross-context round-trip, getContexts=[BACKGROUND]. Present: runtime (id/getManifest/getURL/sendMessage/onMessage/onConnect/getContexts/getBackgroundPage/openOptionsPage), storage local+session+managed, webRequest events (observe-only), alarms, idle, offscreen, i18n, tabs object w/ update only. Missing: chrome.extension whole namespace, tabs getCurrent/query/get/create/sendMessage/onUpdated/onRemoved, webNavigation, scripting, contextMenus, sidePanel, notifications, declarativeNetRequest, permissions, action, windows, cookies, bookmarks, identity, gcm, nativeMessaging, privacy, topSites, history; in CS context even chrome.tabs absent. Bitwarden (enabled): background registers a context but is dead (ping TIMEOUT, matches status 15 = kErrorScriptEvaluateFailed — eval throws: webNavigation missing → guarded fallback to tabs.onUpdated also missing; unguarded notifications/windows/contextMenus/scripting/extension.getViews accesses all fatal); popup dies in polyfills.js on chrome.extension.getBackgroundPage + tabs.getCurrent → Angular never boots, bodyTextLength 0, infinite spinner. Disabled extension pages get ZERO chrome.* — check enabled before diagnosing. EXT08 host ruled out (plain-page probe, same shape: loads fine, APIs present when enabled). Cheap levers all exhausted: --enable-experimental-extension-apis = byte-identical matrix; no WebAttribute; no QTWEBENGINE_* env; QWebEngineExtensionManager has no surface options; missing APIs' schemas are compiled in but unregistered by Qt's embedder — engine-side gap, no app lever. Decision for EXT12: no honest narrow stub unblocks Bitwarden (gaps live inside QtWebEngineCore, app can't inject into a SW; a popup chrome.extension shim alone = fake-alive over a dead vault); implement the degraded-state surface instead — detect never-ready backgrounds (registered context + unanswered ping, or registration error), Preferences row shows 'Limited/Not supported' + missing-API reason, applied to every extension failing the check.

### EXT12

Extension IMPLEMENTATION slice — build what EXT11's diagnosis prescribes (needs EXT11's decision notes). Likely shape: (a) if honest narrow stubs unblock Bitwarden (webNavigation.onCommitted registration, getBackgroundPage, tabs.getCurrent) implement them — ONLY surfaces that keep the extension genuinely functional; (b) honest degradation for extensions that can't run: detect never-ready worker registration, popup shows 'this extension needs APIs Qt WebEngine doesn't provide' instead of infinite spinner, Preferences row carries the reason rather than naked 'Enabled'; (c) apply the same degraded-state treatment to ALL extensions failing the health check, not just Bitwarden. Verify: Bitwarden either functions or reports its real blocker with no spinner; Preferences rows show degradation reasons; tst legs where scriptable; make check PASS.

EXT12 implementation notes: stubs skipped per EXT11 (gaps live inside QtWebEngineCore — nothing app-side can inject into a service worker). What landed: `ExtensionManager::ExtensionHealth` (Unknown/Healthy/Limited/NotSupported) + `ProbeVerdict` (Pending/None/Responsive/Dead) on `ExtensionInfo`; a serialized once-per-session probe — hidden plain QWebEnginePage on the canonical non-OTR profile loads `chrome-extension://<id>/manifest.json` (extension origin, no extension code runs), JS pings the worker via `chrome.runtime.sendMessage` (6s timeout) then samples `chrome.runtime.getContexts({BACKGROUND})`, C++ polls `window.__aroraExtHealth`, popup fallback when manifest.json gets no bindings, 30s watchdog, inconclusive → Unknown never guessed-Dead. classifyHealth: Dead→NotSupported (beats missing APIs), missing APIs on a live/unmeasured worker→Limited, no-background clean→Healthy. Scan re-arms on changed() (1.5s coalescer) + 5s deferred post-installOnProfile; setExtensionEnabled clears the stale verdict; uninstall drops health state. Settings → Extensions status reads Tampered/Error/Not loaded/Disabled/Not supported/Limited/Enabled; details pane shows the health reason; action-bar tooltips carry the verdict and openPopup() intercepts NotSupported with a plain-text explanation popup (Escape closes) instead of a spinning webview. Harness `./arora --extension-health-smoke`: 3 temp fixtures (live pong worker, notifications-declaring worker, eval-throwing worker) + bundled Bitwarden enabled and probed to NotSupported (ping TIMEOUT — EXT11's exact signature). tst_extensionmanager gained healthClassification (full matrix) + backgroundHealthProbe (live→Healthy, eval-crash→NotSupported).

### READ06

Symptom on master@396dce7 (READ05-hardened pipeline): the reader does NOTHING — zero reader-related lines in the app log, so it's failing silently. The exact failure mode is unknown — FIRST establish which: (a) probe says unavailable (Rust rc_readability_probe false-negative on real pages — READ05 moved probing to serialized-DOM rustcore; if collectHtml returns empty or the probe is stricter than isProbablyReaderable, the button never lights / click no-ops) vs (b) button lights but click does nothing (toggle->enter path: isolated-world runDriver never calls back — worldId plumbing, scriptBundle eval error in ReaderWorldId, SEC24 channel/forwarder mismatch) vs (c) enter runs but overlay never paints (setActive path / m_overlay wiring). Reproduce on a real article page (e.g. a Wikipedia/news article — NOT a synthetic fixture), instrument the probe->available->toggle->extract->overlay chain, find the dead link. Suspects ranked: (1) rustcore probe false-negatives on pages isProbablyReaderable would accept (serialized DOM differences — READ03's corpus passed but real pages may differ); (2) the isolated-world scriptBundle silently fails to evaluate on the real page (CSP? world init timing? scriptBundle() returning empty?); (3) reader-forward.js not registered on pages that need it, breaking a gate the overlay path waits on. Spec: fix the actual break so click->reader overlay works again on real articles end-to-end; keep READ05's hardening intact (isolated world, C++ sanitizer, sandboxed iframe, tor path); add a failure log line where the pipeline can silently die (probe callback empty, world-eval error) so the next silent dead-reader is diagnosable. Verify: click reader button on a real article -> overlay appears with content; exit restores; the exact user repro works; reader tst legs still pass + new leg for the diagnosed path; make check PASS.

Diagnosis (this run): instrumented the whole chain (probe verdict, empty serialized DOM, m_busy wedge, extract/refusal reasons, sanitizer floor, overlay-entry refusal) and added `--reader-live <url>` — a real-page harness that waits for the post-load probe, calls the same `ReaderMode::toggle()` the toolbar button invokes, then checks overlay widget presence, `.content` in the reader surface, actual framebuffer pixels (QScreen::grabWindow root grab), and exit restoration. Real pages verified end-to-end: en.wikipedia.org article, hup.hu/node/190526 (the user's own browsing target per .devin/taskloop.log), telex.hu article — probe→toggle→extract→overlay→paint→exit all PASS, so the suspects (1)-(3) above are NOT the break on current master. Three real defects found instead, each a plausible "does nothing" shape: (a) RUST SANITIZER ATE ARTICLE TEXT — the Rust `sanitize()` denylist deleted `<form>`/`<noscript>` *with their children* (the READ03 regression the C++ ReaderSanitizer fixed by unwrapping, never ported): articles whose body sits inside a form wrapper extracted to near-empty content — the /form tst leg proved it (chars 0). Fixed: Rust `strip_elements` unwrap for form/noscript matching the C++ transparent-wrapper policy, dead-tag list aligned with the C++ drop sets. (b) MISSING THIN-CONTENT FLOOR — the Rust path never applied READ03's `<500 chars && !hasMedia` refusal, so heading-only extractions opened a nearly-empty overlay (/thin leg); fixed by re-running `ReaderSanitizer::sanitize` on the Rust fragment in `enter()` — restores the floor AND the READ05 invariant that the overlay only ever renders C++-sanitized markup (defense-in-depth double pass). (c) PERMANENT BUSY WEDGE — `collectHtml`'s JS-lift path could leave `m_busy`/`m_probing` true forever when `toHtml`'s callback never lands (dead/mid-navigation renderer); every later click died silently at `enter()`. Fixed: the 5s restore fallback now settles the callback once via a shared fired-flag, and `enter()` logs when it ignores a busy click. Also added a qInfo at every remaining silent dead-end and updated the 5 stale readerModeContent legs to assert the real Rust overlay surface (aroraReaderView `.content`, reader-view `__pwned` inertness) instead of the removed JS shadow-DOM iframe. New coverage: Rust `sanitizer_unwraps_transparent_wrappers` test + readerModeContent legs now exercising the Rust overlay path.

**QUAL02 findings (landed)**

Harness (a): check-leaks.sh phase 1 over the ASan/LSan instrumented
tree — full suite + all 18 --*-smoke flags. Before: 53 our-code leak
blocks (first run), 44 after the first fix round; after all fixes:
**0 our-code blocks** (21,618 blocks, all upstream — see generated
.devin/LEAKS.md). Valgrind memcheck on the four top smoke harnesses
(--quit-after-load, --app-smoke, --browser-smoke, --extensions-smoke):
3 our-code definite/indirect records (2,848 B) pre-fix → **0**
post-fix; remaining records all sited in Qt6WebEngineCore/Qt6Core.

Leaks/UAFs fixed:

- `tabwidget.cpp addEngineTab` — `createProfile(..., nullptr)` result
  never freed: every engine tab leaked its profile (22+22 suite
  blocks). Now parents a parentless profile to its EngineTab; shared
  backend-owned profiles (servo's single context) keep their owner.
- `servobackend.cpp probeServoBackend` — registered backend was
  `new`'d unparented while the registry doesn't own backends: object +
  members + aboutToQuit connection node leaked at exit. Now qApp-owned.
- `tabbar.cpp` — `QDrag::setMimeData` doesn't take ownership; the
  drag's QMimeData leaked on every exec(). Deleted after exec returns.
- `extensionactionbar.cpp openPopup` — deferred runJavaScript callback
  captured raw `popup`/`anchor`: rebuild() can replace the anchor and
  the popup is delete-on-close while an eval is in flight → UAF.
  Rebound through QPointer guards on all three objects.
- `notesmodel.cpp instance()` — per-profile models parented to qApp
  outlived their profile; a destroyed profile left a live stale model
  keyed by a dangling pointer, returnable on address reuse. Now
  profile-parented so the QPointer registry expires with the profile.
- `main.cpp servoLifecycleSmoke` — held the refused-page probe
  results (page + profile) across the report; both now released —
  with the correction that servo's profile is the backend's SHARED
  m_defaultProfile and must NOT be deleted (a delete would leave the
  backend handing out a dangling pointer on the next createProfile).
- test-side: `tst_engineadapter` leaked the created view;
  `tst_tabbar containerBandEvents` heap-allocated its QMimeData —
  stack-allocated now.

Audits with no findings: extension host hidden/popup views (parented,
view deleted in dtor), tor popovers/panels + sidebar panels (lazy,

closeOverlay path), PiP window, download worker FFI (frees handle on
every exit path), rustcore `se_*`/`RcBuffer`/`oseStringList` (Rust only
writes `out` on success; all call sites free), se_* called only via
ServoView::api().

### QUAL03

Quality sweep 3/5 — security audit (user: 'security issues'). Run check-sanitize.sh + check-supplychain.sh first, then focused review of the surfaces that grew recently: (a) PRIVILEGED CHANNELS — QWebChannel object exposure: nothing privileged reachable from page JS (SEC24's boundary), the EXT14 chromeBridge surface exposes ONLY extension-facing methods, hosted extension JS can't reach app objects; (b) INJECTION POINTS — every string reaching HTML/SQL/shell/JS eval (reader overlay, tor panel, extension popup host, notes); (c) reader-mode isolation post-READ06 still holds (isolated world + C++ sanitize + sandboxed iframe); (d) URL/trust paths — Tor fail-closed still true (no clearnet fallback), trust verdicts honest (TRUST14), TLS probe can't leak; (e) secrets — no keys/credentials in tree, logs don't dump sensitive values; (f) file writes — path traversal in downloads/notes/session files. Fix findings; document any accepted-risk items explicitly. Verify: gates PASS, findings fixed or documented, make check PASS.

**QUAL03 findings (landed)**

Gates: check-supplychain PASS; check-sanitize resumed the
attempt-2 instrumented tree (two prior runs were OOM-killed by the
task runner's ~2 GB cap — ASan builds now run detached).

Findings fixed:

- **Hosted-extension page navigation was unlocked** — `chromeBridge`
  (extension storage, identity, relay) is bound to the host *page*,
  so a hosted document navigating (or being iframed) to foreign
  content would hand that content the bridge.  New
  `HostedBackgroundPage` locks the main frame to the wrapper url
  (about:blank for the initial commit) and subframes to the
  extension's own `chrome-extension://<id>` origin.
- **Extension relay keys were caller-trusted** — `mid`/`chan` arrive
  inside caller-supplied strings; the shim mints `<extId>#…` but
  nothing host-side verified it, so a bridge could graft an end onto
  another extension's port channel, message/disconnect it, or resolve
  a foreign pending `sendMessage`.  `ownsRelayKey()` now enforces the
  sender's own id prefix on send/connect/accept/message/disconnect,
  delivery filters ends by extension id, and `relayRespond` requires
  the responder's extension to equal the origin's.
- **`chrome.tabs` replies leaked url/title** — `tabObject()` emitted
  both unconditionally; EXT16 already scrubbed *event* payloads on
  `tabs`/host permission but the query/get/create/update replies were
  raw.  `shapedTab()` applies `seesUrls()` (same rule) to every reply.
- **PiP config JSON was injectable** — page-derived video fields were
  interpolated into `<script>` in the trusted player document;
  `</script>` inside a string broke out of the element.  `<` is now
  escaped to \u003c before interpolation.
- **Debug builds logged rejected-cookie values** — the live
  `NETWORKCOOKIEJAR_LOGREJECTEDCOOKIES` site dumped the whole
  QNetworkCookie (value = live session credential).  Now logs
  name/domain/url only.

Sanitizer-driven fixes (the instrumented suite surfaced these; all
our-code reports in the sweep):

- `ExtensionEventHub` fired `tabs.onRemoved`/`windows.onRemoved` from
  `QObject::destroyed()` handlers that downcasted and dereferenced the
  mid-destruction object (UBSan downcast + member-access reports at
  extensioneventhub.cpp:417/437/468 and browsermainwindow.cpp:3606).
  Handlers now capture the typed pointer at wire time and never
  dereference it; tracked state keeps QObject-identity keys
  (`windowObject`, `m_windowIds`/`m_dyingWindows` rekeyed) plus
  pre-minted `tabId`/`windowId`, so teardown emits without touching a
  dying object's vtable.
- SIDE09's `qApp` focusChanged lambda could run while
  `~BrowserMainWindow` had already sliced `this` past its dynamic type
  (UBSan member-access at browsermainwindow.cpp:641) — the connection
  is kept (`m_sidebarFocusConnection`) and disconnected first in the
  dtor.
- `DownloadManager::timeString()` int()'d an `inf` when a zero-speed
  sample made the ETA divide-by-zero (UBSan float-to-int at
  downloadmanager.cpp:1749) — non-finite/negative input now returns
  the empty string.
- The sweep's remaining reports are Qt-internal and out of scope:
  a fontconfig/expat `getenv` SEGV on a Chromium worker thread and a
  `ProfileAdapter` teardown UAF inside libQt6WebEngineCore —
  same class as the pre-existing entries in SANITIZER.md.

Audited clean / accepted risks (documented):

- SEC24 channel isolation holds: `external`/`aroraAutofill`/`arora`
  live in `AroraChannelWorld`; the main world reaches them only via
  the whitelisted postMessage forwarder; `arora` is qrc:-only; tor
  pages get a bare channel.
- `external.AddSearchProvider` is page-callable by design — C++
  validates the url and asks for consent before fetching (accepted).
- Reader mode still holds: dedicated script world, C++ tokenizer
  sanitizer, sandboxed iframe without `allow-scripts`.  The iframe's
  `allow-same-origin` lets the *page* inspect the frame — same-origin
  already implies that; accepted.
- `data:` urls pass `isUrlAllowedOnUntrustedInput` deliberately
  (SEC09 typed-equivalent); `javascript:` is refused.  Extension
  `tabs.create`/`update` ride the same gate (comment corrected — it
  claimed `data:` was blocked).
- Tor fail-closed holds: requests route through NavigationPolicy with
  `torMode`; no code path ever clears the app proxy; TlsVerifier
  refuses probes under tor/proxy (SSRF guard intact); the control-
  port cookie is compared as raw 32 bytes, hex on the wire, never
  logged.
- Autofill form data is JSON-encoded into an isolated closure behind
  a per-load UUID token; SecureStore blobs are AES-GCM sealed with
  0600 perms.
- Path traversal: download filenames are sanitized, note ids map
  `/`→`_`, session/extension-storage writes use fixed names or
  sanitized ext ids under `BrowserPaths` + `QSaveFile`.
- Remaining `runJavaScript` interpolations are escaped (jsQuote /
  QJsonDocument) or numeric; `javascript:` typed/bookmarklet exec is
  behind the untrusted-input gate.
- Extension popup windows use a plain `QWebEnginePage` — no app
  channel objects leak in.
- Tor circuit panel text renders via QLabel (consensus-derived, no
  HTML sink); notes render via QTextBrowser markdown (user-authored).
- PiP `requestPopOut` is page-callable but narrow: one window per
  view, nonce-gated close, value-only bridge; Chrome-parity user-
  gesture requirement is NOT enforced (accepted — worst case is the
  page popping out its own video; no privilege gained).

One late finding surfaced while re-verifying under UBSan:
`navBarEditEvent` ran `qobject_cast<QWidget*>` on every event the
qApp filter saw — including queued/meta events delivered for
receivers already mid-destruction (invalid-vptr report, fired inside
tst_browsermainwindow::navBarEditMode).  The cast is now computed
inside the mouse cases only; input events only ever target live
widgets.

89.25); suite — every binary green standalone except the documented

navBarOrderPersistence + its post-Totals SIGSEGV-exit, torStartPage
order-flake, schemehandlers::fileSchemeErrors order-flake — both
PASS standalone); tst_extensionmanager 26/26 incl. new
relay-ownership + tabs-reply-scrub legs, tst_downloadmanager 46/46,

x3, browsermainwindow member-access x2, downloadmanager inf-int);

### QUAL04

Quality sweep 4/5 — performance audit (user: 'performance issues'). (a) BASELINE FIRST — measure before touching: cold startup to first paint, tab create/switch latency, idle + loaded memory footprint, Servo frame time (ENG25's bench numbers are the reference — confirm no regression), scroll smoothness; (b) known suspect areas — icon/theme re-resolution on paint paths (AroraIcon lookups), QSettings churn in hot paths, debounced savers' intervals, per-tab widget re-creation on switch, startup work that could defer; (c) fix clear wins only — measurable improvement or leave it; no speculative optimization without a measured baseline; (d) findings + numbers in the notes. Verify: benchmarks before/after recorded; no functional regression; make check PASS.

Findings (recorded during the audit):

Baselines (isolated HOME, offscreen, Qt 6.12):
- Cold start (--profile-startup): app-ctor 182ms, first window shown
  206ms, warmup done 220ms — PERF03's deferral work is holding.
- --perf-smoke (new legs added this task): app-ctor 184ms,
  first-window 19ms, new-tab avg 1.5ms (8 tabs), 40k-entry history
  load/filter/tree/add 0ms, 40k history-menu first open 0ms, 10k-row
  flat menu first open 53ms / reopen 0ms, 3600 chrome-icon paints 7ms
  (~2us/icon), 300 fresh-icon paints 0ms, tab-switch avg 0.6ms (60
  hops), tabstrip repaint 0.34ms (200 repaints, 9 tabs), 5k-file
  permission sweep 19ms/17ms.
- Memory: idle process tree ~232MB RSS; heavy loaded page ~522MB RSS.
- Servo scroll bench: 145px/notch, ~32ms/frame — identical to ENG25's
  reference numbers, no regression.

Suspect audit (b): every named suspect is already bounded —
- ChromeIconEngine re-tints per pixmap request, but Qt's pixmap cache
  covers the themed load; steady repaint ~2us/icon. No fix needed.
- QSettings: no hits on per-event/paint paths.
- Debounced savers, extension host timers (45s watchdog, 15s nudge),
  DownloadGraph repaints, completer sorting (Rust suggest model caps
  at 500 rows + 150ms debounce), TabBar::paintEvent re-elision
  (0.34ms/strip) — all bounded.
- Tab switch: stacked widgets + connect/disconnect only, 0.6ms — no
  per-switch widget re-creation.
- Startup: nothing eager left worth deferring (PERF03).

Fixes landed (c):
- HistoryTreeModel::data() built a fresh AroraIcon::getChrome QIcon +
  engine on every DecorationRole query (per row, per repaint) — the
  only strictly-wasteful call found. Now a static shared icon; the
  engine re-resolves the theme at paint time so icon-theme switches
  still propagate. (Alloc-level win — below bench resolution; after
  numbers are flat, honestly.)
- autotests/runTests.sh: per-binary timeout guard
  (ARORA_TEST_TIMEOUT, default 900s) — tst_browsermainwindow's
  documented post-Totals WebEngine teardown SIGSEGV loop spun forever
  and killed the previous run at the loop timeout; now a hung binary
  is killed and reported 'TIMED OUT' while the suite continues.
- autotests/extensionmanager/tst_extensionmanager.cpp: updated to the
  merged 5-arg relayPortConnect signature + asserts the delivered
  sender carries the connecting document url (rust-work merge left
  the tree unbuildable).

Merge-regression found while verifying (fixed, not perf):
- tst_ExtensionManager::backgroundHealthProbe started failing only
  post-merge: the EXT17 sibling-page shim injects into every top-level
  chrome-extension://<id>/ document — including the EXT12 health
  probe's bare manifest.json page — where it broke the native
  chrome.* bindings, so the dead-worker measurement never ran
  ("the probe page got no chrome.* bindings" → verdict None →
  Limited instead of NotSupported).  Fix: the probe page now loads
  manifest.json#arora-healthprobe and the page-shim gate skips that
  fragment, keeping pristine native bindings.  Second bug in the same
  path: enqueuePendingProbes only checked m_healthVerdicts, so ids
  queued-but-not-started by requestHealthProbe were re-queued by the
  deferred scan — the duplicate probe overwrote a measured verdict
  (seen live: Responsive → None).  Queue now dedupes against
  queued/in-flight ids.  Result: dead ext measures verdict Dead in
  ~12s (was a 186s timeout-fail), tst_extensionmanager 27/27.

Regression check: suite green except the documented pre-existing

### PRINT02

Merge Print + Print Preview into one Firefox-style entry — user: 'print and print preview should merge into each other like on firefox.' Firefox's model: ONE Print item opens the preview directly (the preview IS the print dialog — page preview + print controls in the same surface). Spec: (a) single 'Print…' menu entry keeping Ctrl+P — triggering it opens the print-preview dialog directly (PRINT01's printToPdf-based preview in printpreviewdialog — preview + page setup + the actual Print action inside); (b) the separate 'Print Preview' action/entry disappears — its slot merges into the one print path (the preview dialog's own Print button performs the final native print); (c) what was 'Print' before (straight-to-native-print) — decide whether a 'Print without preview' secondary path survives anywhere (Firefox doesn't ship one — recommend dropping it, keep only if a hidden/shortcut path already exists; document the choice); (d) the preview dialog needs whatever options the old direct-print path offered that it lacks (printer selection, copies, page range — audit the native QPrintDialog options vs the preview's controls and surface the missing ones — the point of merging is users don't lose the real print pipeline); (e) toolbar/menu icons — the print icon stays on the single entry. Verify: Ctrl+P and the menu item open the preview; printing from the preview produces a real print job; no dead 'Print Preview' entry; tst legs updated; make check PASS.

- Resolution: merged to one print surface Firefox-style — m_filePrintPreviewAction/filePrintPreview deleted, the File menu carries a single 'Print...' (document-print icon, QKeySequence::Print) that routes through printRequested -> PrintPreviewDialog; JS window.print() lands in the same funnel. The straight-to-printer path is dropped entirely (Firefox ships none either): printRequested's native QPrintDialog/printer-guard block is gone and the CRASH02 dead-target validation moved into PdfView::printDocument, which the preview's new lead toolbar 'Print...' action drives. Options parity held — the preview's Print opens the real native QPrintDialog (printer selection, copies, page range), and PdfCanvas::printPages now honors QPrinter::PageRange (from/to clipping) and copyCount with collate ordering for the non-CUPS engines that don't multiply server-side. Dialog retitled 'Print'/'Print — <doc>'; engine tabs still refuse honestly under the 'Print' title, engine/web tabs hosting the shell document view preview their staged PDF via setDocumentFile. Gotchas: driving printDocument() from inside the preview's modal exec() starves any dismisser timer in the doubly-nested loop — the tst leg runs the print drive on a non-modal preview instead; a stack PrintPreviewDialog parented to the window is deleted by closeWindow (free(): invalid pointer) — tests must construct it unparented. Verified: tst_browsermainwindow 68/68 (printPaths covers preview-open pages>0, real QPrintDialog + dead-target warning, printPages %PDF- artifact + ranged output, mid-generation close, engine refusal; fileMenuOrder asserts one print entry; chromeSlots), --printpreview-smoke all legs PASS, make check fully green offscreen (4 gates PASS, every suite 0 failed).

### SESS02

Silent-exit / windowless-start cluster — observed live twice today: (a) launched after a killed session restored ZERO windows — process stayed alive windowless (only a second launch via the single-instance socket materialized one); (b) a later instance 'vanished' with no crash trace — consistent with a clean last-window-close exit but indistinguishable from a crash to the user. Spec: (1) STARTUP — if session restore yields no windows (empty/corrupt/partial session), the app must open a normal window — never run windowless; find where restore decides 'zero windows = keep running' and add the fallback; (2) EXIT INTENT — audit every path that ends the process without the user's explicit quit: lastWindowClosed behavior vs explicit Quit, PREF04's new confirmExit coverage (does it catch close-window-quit too?), downloadManager allowQuit path — the goal: no path exits silently in a way a user would read as a crash; if quitOnLastWindowClosed closing the app surprises users coming from single-window workflows, document the chosen semantics; (3) CRASH VISIBILITY — the crashreporter caught SIGABRT earlier but today's death left zero trace — verify the handler covers the realistic kill set (SEGV/ABRT/ILL/FPE) and that an un-CAUGHT death is at least logged ('arora exited, rc=X' breadcrumb on next start or in the log). Verify: kill the app mid-session -> relaunch -> a window always appears; each exit path traced; tst/smoke legs for empty-session restore; make check PASS.

- Resolution: STARTUP — new `BrowserApplication::ensureSessionWindow()` is the floor: no live BrowserMainWindow -> `newMainWindow()` + `startPageUrl()` load on the current tab, and any window whose strip came up empty gets `newTabPage()`. `postLaunch()` calls it for real sessions (`!isStandalone() || isTorMode()`), so an empty/corrupt session blob, a declined crash-guard prompt, or a window lost before postLaunch all still produce one browsing window. EXIT INTENT — `newMainWindow()` connects `destroyed` -> `windowDestroyed()` (skipped under AUTOTESTS): deferred one slot past the QPointer clear, it reaps only when the last browser window died while a stray VISIBLE top-level (dialog outliving its parent) kept the event loop running windowless — then `QCoreApplication::exit(0)` (not `::exit`) lets exec() unwind so main() still records the clean rc. `quitBrowser()` now calls `markSessionCleanExit(0)` before its hard `::exit(0)` — menu-Quit previously left the CRASH01 sentinel armed and scored as a crash next start. Sentinel scope fixed too: `markSessionStart`/`markSessionCleanExit(rc)` moved out of the blanket scope-guard to the real-session entry points — standalone smokes never arm it (a smoke death can't masquerade as a user crash) and the tor window process takes its own marks at its exec() site. CRASH VISIBILITY — `browser/lastExit` QSettings breadcrumb: `markSessionCleanExit(rc)` writes 'clean rc=N', new `markSessionTerminated(sig)` writes 'terminated by signal N (NAME)' (wired through `terminateRequested`'s signal arg), and `markSessionStart()` finding the sentinel armed writes 'unclean (crash or external kill)' + warns, else echoes 'arora: last exit: <record>' — every death now leaves a trace distinguishable from a clean close. Verify: new `--session-floor-smoke` (30 checks: zero-window floor, empty-manifest + corrupt-blob restore -> fallback window, zero-tab window regrows a tab, breadcrumb clean/signal/unclean/flush legs) PASS x3 deterministic; `--sigterm-smoke` gained the breadcrumb leg and passes all 3 signals; `--session-smoke`/`--restore-smoke` PASS. Side fixes forced by the new coverage: smokes that leave live WebEngine/Servo objects now `std::quick_exit` past Chromium's wedged static teardown (engine/container/pip/palette/tabstrip smokes); clientcert-smoke's pre-exec `application.exit(1)` no-op returned 1 properly; container-smoke pins the single-row strip (CONT06 made two-level default) + drains queued postLaunch so the start page can't hijack fixture tabs; commandpalette collectTabs uses tabDisplayTitle so TABS12 renames are reachable; check-warnings diag regex requires a line number so make's own 'warning:' lines stop counting; tst_tabwidget::tabTransfer picks the window actually holding the torn view; tormanager init-order + pdfworker [[maybe_unused]] warnings. make check: check-warnings/supplychain/engine-audit/rustcoverage all PASS, every suite binary 0 failed except one in-suite tst_browsermainwindow::torStartPage title-timing flake (68/68 standalone, rc=0).

### ENG30

Servo start page unreachable — user: 'start page can not be changed to servo, the servo start page is never viewable.' Root cause located (two defects): (a) NEW-TAB PATH — TabWidget::newTab() calls addEngineTab(backend, true) when the default engine pick is servo and returns; addEngineTab (tabwidget.cpp:1186-1355) wires everything but NEVER issues a load — the fresh servo tab sits blank forever. The branded pick exists (startPageUrl('servo') -> qrc:/startpage-servo.html, browserapplication.cpp:1260) and the qrc: resolver works (se_set_resource_resolver, smoke legs PASS) — the load is simply never issued. Fix: after a successful addEngineTab in newTab(), load BrowserApplication::startPageUrl(backend->id(), privateContext-of-tab) — mirror loadBrandedStartPage semantics (standard-mode blank-tab rule: does a servo new tab get the branded page unconditionally, or only under the same conditions? — match whatever tor/private do: loadBrandedStartPage fires for tor/private/servo picks, so a servo-default new tab SHOULD show the servo start page — follow that precedent). (b) SWAP PATH — reloadTabInEngine carries the SOURCE tab's committed url: swapping a chromium start page lands qrc:/startpage.html (the CHROMIUM-branded page) in the servo tab — renders via the resolver but wrong branding, and swapping the servo tab BACK keeps the servo variant in chromium. Fix: when the carried url is ANY branded start page (qrc:/startpage*.html or the about:home alias), re-resolve through startPageUrl(targetEngineId, privateContext) at swap time so the destination always gets ITS branding. (c) verify the Home button in a servo tab (goHome -> about:home -> startPageUrl already picks the engine variant — confirm end-to-end it lands). Verify: new-tab with default engine=servo shows the servo start page (grab); chromium-start-page -> swap -> servo-branded page; servo-start-page -> swap back -> chromium-branded page; --servo-smoke gains a 'newtab-shows-startpage' leg; make check PASS.

- Resolution: all three defects fixed in tabwidget.cpp. New helper isBrandedStartPageUrl() treats qrc:/startpage.html + any qrc:/startpage-*.html + the about:home alias as 'the start page' rather than a literal resource. (a) newTab() loads startPageUrl(backend->id(), false) on the fresh engine tab under the same rule loadBrandedStartPage uses (a pick equal to the plain page means no branding — the tab stays bare). (b) reloadTabInEngine re-resolves a carried branded url through startPageUrl(targetEngineId, false) before loading it on the replacement — swap chromium->servo now lands qrc:/startpage-servo.html and back lands qrc:/startpage.html. (c) loadUrl's CurrentTab dispatch onto a foreign engine tab re-picks branded urls for THAT backend — Home/typed about:home on a servo tab lands the servo variant even when the app default is chromium (spec assumed defaultEngineId already covered it; it only does when default == the tab's engine). One stale leg re-pointed: ENG18's swap-startpage-commit asserted the carried chromium qrc url verbatim — now expects the re-branded servo variant. Verified: clean build 0 warnings; --servo-smoke all legs PASS incl new newtab-is-servo/newtab-shows-startpage/home-keeps-servo-branding/swap-brands-* (home leg deliberately flips default back to webengine mid-leg so only destination-side re-pick can satisfy it); --quit-after-load rc=0; tst_tabwidget 31/31, tst_engineswitch 26/26, tst_omnibox 87/87 offscreen; 4 check gates PASS.

### DEVT10

BiDi context selector can't address the first tab — user: 'the first tab is not selectable by bidi dev tool.' Two candidate causes, check BOTH: (a) NON-CDP TABS — m_contextCombo fills from browsingContext.getTree, which is CDP Target.getTargets filtered to type=='page' (bidi.rs translate_tree ~1266) — Servo/engine tabs and widget tabs (Preferences, Downloads) have no CDP target so they can NEVER be selected; the user was running a Servo tab as tab 0 when reporting. The panel currently gives zero explanation — a missing tab reads as a bug. Fix: enumerate Arora's real tab strip alongside the CDP list (the window knows its tabs), and show non-debuggable tabs in the selector as disabled entries with a reason ('Servo tab — no devtools backend' / 'not a web page') rather than silently omitting them — same honesty pattern as the engine indicator's locked-with-tooltip. (b) INDEX-0 SELECTION PATH — if the first tab IS a normal WebEngine tab and still won't select: audit the getTree re-population + currentIndexChanged path (bidipanel.cpp ~1284-1303 — keep/restore, blockSignals, setCurrentIndex(0)) and with_session attach for the first target — a session attach that no-ops on index 0 or a targetId that collides with the browser target is a plausible off-by-one. Reproduce in a window where tab 0 is WebEngine vs Servo vs widget — all three must behave correctly/honestly. Fix may live in rustcore/bidi.rs (target enumeration) or bidipanel.cpp (combo) — whichever side owns it. Verify: a window with [servo, webengine, widget] tabs shows all three in the selector — the webengine one works, the others are visibly-disabled-with-reason; index-0 selection on a webengine first tab evals correctly; tst_devtools legs added; make check / cargo test PASS.

### DEVT11

BiDi Network vs Chromium DevTools — user screenshot side-by-side: 'make BiDi the same if you can.' Chrome's Network tab reads as one tool; ours reads as a raw table with chips. Close the gap in slices (bidipanel.cpp, DEVT06/07/09 base): (a) TOOLBAR — Chrome's row: record/stop toggle, clear-log button, filter field, search-in-response field, 'Preserve log' + 'Disable cache' checkboxes, throttling selector — map each: record/stop -> pause network event capture (or omit honestly), clear -> our existing clear, Preserve log exists, Disable cache -> Network.setCacheDisabled via the bridge if CDP exposes it (verify on QtWebEngine — if unimplemented, show the control disabled-with-tooltip rather than dead); throttling — likely unsupported on QtWebEngine CDP, omit or disabled-honest, do NOT fake it; (b) WATERFALL — Chrome-style per-resource-type colored bars (Doc=blue, CSS=green, JS=yellow-ish, Img=purple, Font, XHR=light blue — match Chrome's swatch palette approximately, palette-driven via THEM04's helpers not hardcoded), ms tick ruler along the top of the waterfall column, per-segment breakdown on hover if phases available (stalled/dns/download — only what CDP timing actually provides, honest absence otherwise); (c) COLUMNS — Chrome order Name/Status/Type/Initiator/Size/Time/Waterfall (we match already — verify), status cell shows code, size shows transfer+resource where known ('(disk cache)'/'(memory cache)' markers like Chrome); (d) ROW STATES — failed=red text (exists), pending translucent (exists), from-cache grayed-ish; (e) the summary footer already matches Chrome's (requests/transferred/finish/DCL/Load) — keep. What stays different honestly: no Performance/Memory/Application/Security tabs in scope — this task is the NETWORK tab only. Verify: grab ours vs Chrome on the same site at same width — toolbar parity minus honestly-unavailable controls, waterfall colors + ruler present, failed/cached rows read identically; tst_devtools legs updated; make check PASS.

- Resolution: the toolbar now mirrors Chrome's row — the record/stop capture toggle (red dot while recording, grey while paused; the gate sits in onEvent so bridge events still arrive but nothing logs — table, footer and waterfall freeze exactly like Chrome's stop) and the clear button lead, the filter field stretches, Preserve log / Disable cache trail, and the two slots the debug channel cannot fill are present but honestly disabled with reason tooltips: the throttling selector (the bridge has no BiDi translation for Network.emulateNetworkConditions) and the response-body search button (no getResponseBody equivalent ever reaches the panel — DEVT06 scope call). Waterfall bars take per-resource-type swatches via the new palette-driven AroraStyle::networkTypeSwatch (Chrome's set approximated: document blue, stylesheet green, script amber, font magenta, image purple, media teal, manifest grey, websocket violet, wasm blue-grey, xhr/fetch light blue, everything else neutral; dark palettes lift +18% toward white) — failed rows keep the red, pending keeps the translucency. The Waterfall header section is now a live ms ruler: a BidiNetworkHeader subclass paints the same nice-step 1/2/5 tick ladder (~90px label spacing) that drives the faint gridlines behind every bar, so a header tick always lands on a cell rule. The raw CDP ResourceTiming block is stored per row and the waterfall hover breaks it into Chrome's phase lines — proxy negotiation / DNS / initial connection / SSL / request sent / waiting (TTFB) / content download — each emitted only when the timing object carried it; Queueing/Stalled is honestly absent because the monotonic waterfall clock cannot reconstruct the wall-clock issue time. Size tooltip now reads 'X transferred over network, resource size Y'; cache-served rows grey out via the Disabled-Text foreground like Chrome's muted '(disk cache)' rows. Test seams: BidiPanel::testWaterfallTicks() exposes the ladder without scraping header pixels; bidiWaterfallLegibility's isBarPixel recognizes the whole type-swatch set. Verified: tst_devtools 12/12 incl new bidiNetworkParity leg (toolbar order, disabled-honest controls + reason tooltips, record gating, phase-breakdown tooltip math, size tooltip, cached-row greying, tick-ladder invariants, per-type pixel asserts) offscreen --disable-gpu; check-warnings / supplychain / engine-audit / rustcoverage all PASS; runTests suite clean; header+cell grabs visually confirm ruler labels and type-colored bars.

The bridge (rustcore bidi.rs) now normalizes CDP console levels onto BiDi's four buckets, forwards the raw console method name, a {url,line} source taken from the stack top (or Log.entryAdded's url/lineNumber), and the structured RemoteObject args with session-scoped objectIds stripped (previews truncated to 64 props, args to 32). Panel side: error rows wash red + critical icon, warnings amber + warning icon, verbose/debug dims to Disabled-Text, info/log stay plain — no '[level]' prefix survives. Toolbar = clear button, live text filter (matches message and source column), checkable Errors/Warnings/Info/Verbose dropdown reporting All/Default/No/Custom levels like Chrome, and a console-scoped Preserve log that gates wiping on navigationStarted independently of the network one. Arg previews render expandable 'name: value' children; when the engine sends no preview the row stays flat — no fake expansion. Eval sits on a '>' prompt row; Up recalls history with the in-progress draft stashed, Down past the newest restores it; thrown eval results render as red error rows. Events with a foreign context are dropped (Chrome frame scoping). Bug found while wiring: m_evalLine->installEventFilter(this) runs before the Network tab exists, so m_networkView is now nullptr-initialized for the eventFilter guard.
