### TOR12

User report: 'When I open the tor windows first time the url search is not working. After the second url bar search it will work.' Classic warmup race — the first navigation attempt fires before the Tor path is actually usable; by attempt two the circuit/daemon is ready. Reproduce: launch a fresh Tor window, immediately type a URL/search, Enter — expected load, actual silent failure or error page; second Enter works. Audit candidates in order: (a) TorManager/TorControl bootstrap — first request dispatched before SOCKS5 proxy port is listening or before bootstrap completes; navigation should QUEUE or show honest 'tor starting' state, not silently fail; (b) profile wiring race — the window's WebView gets its torProfile/proxy interceptor AFTER the first load was already issued through the normal profile; (c) interceptor installed after first request; (d) the start-page auto-load races the same path (page may 'work' because startpage is qrc-local while real nav dies). Fixes: queue/gate navigation until TorManager reports ready, or fail honestly with retry — never silently eat the request. Check how the second attempt differs (same code path succeeding => timing, not wiring). Verify: fresh tor window → first Enter within <1s of window show must load (or show explicit 'tor still starting, will load when ready' pending state that auto-continues); tst leg with a stubbed slow-start TorManager. make check PASS.

DONE (run 2 finished an interrupted run 1): root cause was `ready` ending the navigation hold while only internal/directory circuits existed — the SOCKS listener answers before the first stream-ridable path lands. Fix: `TorManager::isUsableCircuit()` (BUILT + ≥3 hops + purpose empty/GENERAL) via new `hasUsableCircuit()`; `WebView::torMustQueue` extends the hold through `readyElapsed() < 45s && !hasUsableCircuit()`, `circuitsChanged` became a second wake-up, and a 10s `m_torQueueGrace` dispatches anyway when a Ready daemon never shares circuit data; held navs announce 'Tor is still starting — the page will load automatically when it is ready' via setStatusBarText; `loadUrlInView` routes link-opens through the queue too. `WebPage` warm-up retry went from one-shot to `kTorMaxRetryAttempts=4` per url (counter `m_torRetryAttempts` spent on the fire, re-arms in `torArmRetry` while budget+window allow), and its snapshot handler now requires a usable circuit rather than any BUILT. Test seams added under AUTOTESTS: `TorManager::setCircuitsForTesting`, `WebPage::{torPendingRetry,torRetryAttempts,setTorWarmupMs}ForTesting`, `WebView::setTorQueue{Grace,Warmup}MsForTesting`. Run-2 fix to run-1's flake: `torWarmupRetry`/`torWarmupNavigationGate` stretch the wall-clock warm-up bound (10min) and stage the grace per phase, and the retry leg syncs on the synchronous `m_torRetryAttempts` counter with `>=` + exhaustion-tolerant re-arm waits — under suite load four fire→fail→re-arm round-trips could out-last the real 45s window and the leg would wait on a refused re-arm forever. Verified: tst_webview 28/28 (incl. all three tor legs) and tst_tormanager 22/22 offscreen --disable-gpu; make check = check-warnings/supplychain/engine-audit/rustcoverage all PASS, full suite 0 failed except tst_locationbar::shieldPopupFirstShow — reproduced byte-identical (sizeHint 255x481 vs 255x422) on the unpatched HEAD tree via stash+rebuild+rerun: pre-existing merge fallout, not TOR12.

### VIEW06

User report: 'Zoom text only' does nothing. The toggle exists in the View/zoom menu but page text doesn't zoom independently. ### VIEW07

User report: page source 'just loading and loading' — it does complete but takes far too long. The pipeline is three SEQUENTIAL async stages (sourceviewer.cpp + viewPageSource): (1) enginePage()->toHtml() — full DOM serialization roundtrip; (2) THEN the SourceViewer ctor starts a NAM re-fetch of the URL (PreferCache but a cache-miss = full re-download); (3) THEN loadingFinished spawns a throwaway QWebEnginePage probe, setContent's the bytes, waits for loadFinished to DOM-compare. Every stage waits on the previous. Fixes in priority order: (a) PARALLELIZE — kick the NAM re-fetch at the same moment toHtml is requested (or in the SourceViewer ctor, not after markup arrives); the fetch is pure network, toHtml is pure render-side — no dependency between them; (b) the probe-page DOM compare is the most expensive stage for what it buys (deciding wire-source vs DOM-serialization) — consider cheap heuristics first: if the page has no POST history and the reply's DOM-parseability is known-cheap, or just show wire source when the fetch succeeded and content-type is text/*; keep the probe only when it changes the answer; measure how much the probe actually costs — on big pages setContent+parse can be seconds; (c) progress honesty — a 'Fetching source…' status line, never a silent loading dialog; (d) timeout the NAM fetch (~15s) so a dead URL fails to DOM source instead of hanging. Verify: timing instrumentation per stage, a fixture page asserts the dialog reaches content in a sane bound; correctness — source shown is still right (wire-vs-DOM decision unchanged where it matters). make check PASS.

### TOR14

Follow-up to TOR12 — user tested the live build: 'first search still failing on tor window, after a few seconds it does, but I can see the error message.' So TOR12's gate did NOT hold in practice. Hypotheses to check against what TOR12 actually shipped: (a) the readiness gate keys off the wrong signal — TorControl reports 'ready' when the control connection answers or bootstrap reaches some %, but the SOCKS path/first usable circuit isn't there yet — the real signal for 'a site can load' is a usable circuit (check what TOR12 gated on vs what torsocks5/daemon actually guarantees); (b) the pending-navigation state exists but the error page STILL commits — check whether the gate intercepts the failure and re-dispatches, or whether the load errors visibly and only the retry works; (c) 10s grace fired too early / gate released before daemon done. Reproduce honestly: fresh Tor window + immediate search must NEVER show an error page — acceptable outcomes: the page loads after gate release (maybe a few seconds), or a real 'Tor is starting — will load automatically' pending UI that resolves WITHOUT user retry. An error flash that clears on retry = still broken. Verify on the LIVE daemon path too (faketor timing may not reproduce the real bootstrap gap). make check PASS.

Resolved (5 attempts): root cause was hypothesis (b)+(c) — TOR12's gate only ran in WebView::loadUrl, so page-initiated navigations (the Tor start page's `window.location.href` search, links, JS/meta redirects) reached acceptNavigationRequest unguarded, failed at the cold SOCKS path, and committed a real error page before the retry cleared it. Fix (attempts 1-4, merged 8ce84000): WebPage::acceptNavigationRequest routes main-frame remote navs through WebView::torHoldNavigation → refused+queued into the same view hold (m_torHeldNavs swallows their failures); armed warm-up retries show a "Tor is still starting" pending page instead of the error page; honest error still commits once the retry budget is spent. Attempt 5 root-caused the remaining stuck-pending case: a fired retry's load() racing the pending page's in-flight setHtml commit gets silently eaten by Chromium (accepted, then no LoadStarted/LoadFailed) — torFireRetry + torFlushQueuedLoad + torGraceDispatch + loadUrl now defer dispatch while generatedPageCommitPending() is true. Verified: torWarmupRetry/NavigationGate/QueuedNavigation + tst_webpage 73/73 + tst_tormanager 22/22 + tst_webview 27/28 (sole fail = stash-documented linkOpenRefererDiscipline flake; middleClickAutoscroll SIGTRAP only without --disable-gpu, documented), faketor --tor-window-smoke: page-initiated nav held while daemon starts, no error page committed (real IsTor proof needs a live exit — no tor binary on box), supplychain + engine-audit gates PASS.

### MEM04

User report: starting from telex.hu, opening an article and going back repeatedly — the memory indicator always rises, never falls. First establish WHERE the number comes from — if the indicator reads the process RSS (via /proc) vs a QtWebEngine-internal figure changes what's possible: WebEngine render-process memory includes Chromium's own caches which legitimately grow-then-cap; unbounded growth in OUR objects is a leak. Reproduce deterministically: scripted loop of ~20 telex-style article→back cycles on a fixture or real site, snapshot RSS + heap each cycle, plot the slope. Suspects ranked: (a) per-navigation objects never freed in our code — each nav may allocate a TlsVerifier record, PrivacyRequestInterceptor state, site-decision cache entries, history/completer entries, ReaderMode probe artifacts, back-forward stack items holding WebContents refs; run LSan-style accounting per-cycle to find a growing allocation site (QUAL02's methodology applies — a leak that grows per-cycle is more diagnostic than exit-time leaks); (b) WebEngine's in-memory page/bf-cache retaining pages (bounded, should plateau — check if it does); (c) the memory indicator itself lying (reading a growing counter, e.g. cached-processes + our own tallies — the 'honest memory' work touched this; verify the number actually reflects releasable memory). If bounded-cache: document plateau point + tune max entries. If real leak: find the allocation site, fix, verify the loop now plateaus. (d) COVERAGE MATRIX (user directive): run the cycle-loop against every surface — normal window, private window, Tor window, Servo engine tab, and tab-switch variants (nav in background tab, tear-off window). Per-type profile/interceptor/caches differ (private profile, tor SOCKS path, servo embed has own heap) so a leak may live in only one path — e.g. Tor tabs accumulate per-request interceptor state, Servo tabs leak per-frame buffers. Report the per-type slope table: which surfaces leak vs plateau. Verify: scripted N-cycle loop shows flat/plateau after warm-up on EACH surface, not a monotonic climb; report slope before/after per type. make check PASS.

### POL04

User approved: implement ClearURLs-like privacy cleaning natively, surfaced through the shield — NOT an extension. What exists: UrlCleaner (data-driven param blocklist — exact + 'prefix*' matching, http(s) only) currently only powers 'Copy Clean Link'. What ClearURLs does that we don't: AUTO-clean on navigation, per-domain rules, redirect unwrapping. Build it in layers: (a) RULES ENGINE — extend UrlCleaner beyond a flat param list: global params (utm_*, fbclid, gclid, dclid, igshid, ref_*…), per-domain scoped params (e.g. 'ref' only on domains where it's a tracker — amazon.*, not everywhere), and redirect-unwrap rules (host+param patterns where the param VALUE is the real target URL — google.com/url?q=, l.facebook.com/l.php?u=, youtube redirect, duckduckgo l/?uddg=, t.co… — replace the URL with the decoded target). Keep it data-driven — a compact rules table (per-domain param lists + unwrap patterns), not per-site code. Optionally vendor a ClearURLs-rules snapshot if the license allows (LGPL code; check the rules file's own license header — attribute in README third-party list if vendored); otherwise hand-curate the top ~30 patterns. (b) AUTO-CLEAN — hook into PrivacyRequestInterceptor (already in the request path): on navigation requests where cleaning changes the URL, redirect to the cleaned URL — never strip params that would break the page (the rules engine must distinguish 'tracker param' from 'functional param' — that's what per-domain scoping is for); count cleaned params per page-load for the badge. Careful: only top-level navigations, not subresource/XHR (breaking those = broken sites); private/tor tabs get it too. The shield-UI surface (per-site toggle, cleaned-count display, settings default) is POL05 — not in scope here. Verify: legs — google redirect URL unwraps to real target on navigation; utm_* stripped live (URL bar shows clean URL); a functional param on a real site survives; --urlclean-smoke exercises the path. make check PASS.

### TRUST22

Migrated from UI lane TRUST19 (padlock/TlsVerifier/SiteDecisionStore machinery is master-only — that lane can't reach it). Question to answer in code: does TlsVerifier actually RUN on the Tor profile and return a real verdict? If yes — GREEN is honest (the lock = connection security; the exit-to-site cert chain verifies the same regardless of Tor). Yellow should then mean 'verdict genuinely absent' only. If Tor tabs get no verdict, either wire the verifier in or keep yellow + document. Also audit: (a) whether 'trusted' tier wrongly requires a stored SiteDecision — baseline must be valid TLS + no flags = green; (b) verdict enum mapping — live log shows 'TlsVerifier: host:443 2' — check what 2 means and whether it's being under-mapped to neutral; (c) refresh timing — padlock may bind before the verdict lands and never re-refresh on verdict-arrival; connect to the verifier's result signal if it exists. Verify: valid-cert HTTPS shows green on normal AND tor windows; verdict-absent → yellow; warning → red; refresh fires on verdict arrival. make check PASS.

### TOR15

Contingency split of TOR14 — activates only if TOR14 hits its attempt cap. Narrow scope: the armed warmup retry fires, but its re-dispatched navigation gets re-captured by the new page-level hold (`torMustQueue`/`torFireRetry` interaction observed in attempt 4's tracing — the retry round-trips stall after ~2 cycles). Fix: a retry-fire must be identifiable and exempt from the hold it is meant to defeat (nonce/flag on the dispatch, or whitelist the URL+tab pair already marked as armed). Do NOT widen scope into new-tab-leg or harness work — that is TOR16. Verify: `torWarmupRetry` leg passes deterministically; live Tor window first-search does not error-flash. make check PASS.

### TOR16

Contingency split of TOR14 — activates only if TOR14 hits its attempt cap. Narrow scope: test-harness determinism only. Observed: a refused navigation yields `loadFinished(false)`, leaving the commit spy waiting ~15s; under suite load the warmup legs flake (TOR12 notes already record stash-verified load flakes torWarmupRetry/shieldPopupFirstShow). Make the fixture deterministic: treat refused/failed loads as commit-or-expected-outcome rather than waiting the full timeout, bound the wait on refused schemes, and re-check `about:`/`qrc:` exempt-scheme ordering vs the Tor hold flush. Do NOT touch product hold/retry logic — that is TOR15. Verify: tor legs stable across repeated suite runs under load. make check PASS.

### PROF03

Profile-menu backend — clear-data quick actions + red 'Delete Profile' wipe-with-restart. RUNS IN PARALLEL with UI lane PROF01 (button/menu) and rust lane PROF02 (avatar); this task ships the BACKEND — menu wiring is a conditional hook. User spec: menu options 'delete profile (would delete every user settings, plus cache, plus cookies, etc) — something red color — delete cache, delete cookies, delete history'.

Build: (a) HYGIENE ACTIONS — a small helper (e.g. ProfileMenuActions or statics on ClearPrivateData) producing ready-made QActions: 'Clear History…', 'Clear Cookies…', 'Clear Cache…' + 'Clear Private Data…' (opens the existing full dialog). Each single-purpose action: one honest confirm ('Clear browsing history for all time? This cannot be undone.') then ClearPrivateData::plan("everything", <category>, /*otr=*/false) + executePlan — the whole engine exists (clearprivatedata.cpp); the ops already route through rustcore's rc_clearplan under ARORA_RUSTCORE. A small status-bar 'History cleared' confirmation after. (b) DELETE PROFILE — the dangerous one, styled red when it appears in a menu (QWidgetAction stylesheet or danger-accented row — match existing destructive-item precedent). Flow: confirm dialog listing EXACTLY what dies (settings, history, bookmarks, cookies, cache, sessions, site permissions, extension data, avatar/name — everything except the binary), then a second gate: type-to-confirm or a held-then-enabled button — no single accidental click wipes a profile. Execution MUST respect the SEC12/HARD01 lesson — never delete Chromium storage trees under a live browser (wedged navigations were the documented outcome). Honest path: write an 'arora-profile-wipe.pending' marker into the data dir → terminate the app (after autosave quiesces) → on next startup, BEFORE any profile/WebContents construction, wipe the marker-gated stores: ClearFullWipe plan + QSettings::clear + remove the BrowserPaths::dataFilePath tree + the marker itself → continue into a fresh-profile boot. 'Delete and restart Arora' as the label — restarting IS part of the honest fix. (c) The wipe intentionally does NOT touch: downloads dir (user files), packages, system tor binary. (d) MENU HOOK — if BrowserMainWindow has a profile menu (PROF01 may or may not have merged): append the hygiene actions + separator + Delete Profile into its hygieneBlock/dangerBlock helpers. If no profile menu exists yet, ship the actions/helper standalone + cover via autotest — PROF04 wires the call site. Do NOT create a second profile menu. Verify: tst legs — each action → correct single-category plan executed (seed history+cookies, run one, others survive); delete-profile leg — marker written, app exits, re-exec boots fresh (empty history, wiped settings, marker gone); cancelled confirm deletes nothing; make check PASS.

### TOR17

Live user report (third in this chain): 'TOR first search is failing' — on a 2026-10-16 build that already contains TOR12 (nav hold through cold-circuit window), TOR13 (circuit rotate), AND TOR14 (page-initiated navs join the hold + retries deferred behind in-flight generated commits + warm-up pending page). All three shipped layers are present and still the first navigation fails.

This needs a different approach than another blind fix attempt — the previous fixes each verified against faketor/unit seams and still regressed live. (a) REPRODUCE ON THE REAL PATH FIRST: launch a real `arora --tor` window against the real bundled daemon (not faketor — faketor timing has repeatedly failed to reproduce live behavior; this task explicitly REQUIRES the live daemon, install/stage tor via BuildProcess/fetch-tor.sh if absent) — cold start, type a search immediately, capture what actually happens: error page? silent drop? pending page that never resolves? Capture the WebPage/WebView tor-hold state machine at the moment of failure with whatever logging the hold path already emits (add ARORA_TOR_DEBUG trace if missing — queue/refuse/dispatch/fire-retry decisions with reason codes). (b) Rank hypotheses against the trace: hold never armed (queue check skipped for this nav kind — e.g. the omnibox 'search' path bypasses loadUrl AND page-level hold?); hold armed but released early (hasUsableCircuit true on a directory-purpose or 2-hop circuit); retry fired but re-captured by the hold (TOR15's predicted interaction — if the trace shows the retry queueing itself, TOR15's exempt-nonce fix IS this task); commit-eaten reload (generatedPageCommitPending guard misfiring — a retry deferred forever); SOCKS reachable but first stream refused by tor itself (real NEWNYM/circuit-budget behavior faketor can't emulate). (c) Fix what the trace indicts — no more speculative layers. If the fix changes hold/retry semantics, TOR15/TOR16's scopes fold in here (they were contingency splits for exactly this). Verify: on the REAL daemon — cold tor window + immediate search must never error-flash: either loads when ready or shows the honest pending state that auto-continues; run it 3x cold to catch timing races; then make check PASS.

Landed (attempt 3; attempts 1-2 hit the 3600s cap mid-verify): the ARORA_TOR_DEBUG trace on the REAL daemon (attempt 1) indicted none of the TOR12/14 machinery — every queue/refuse/dispatch/fire-retry decision was correct — the failure sat one layer lower.  Chromium's network service re-reads QNetworkProxy only from a lazy poll inside proxy resolution, so the first request after the fail-closed→real SOCKS switch still resolved on the STALE endpoint: the user's first search died with net::ERR_PROXY_CONNECTION_FAILED on 127.0.0.1:1 at ~ready+0.5s (measured: a sacrificial request at +490ms post-switch still failed; every later one used the new config) and the warm-up retry then worked — the "fails once, works seconds later" signature.  Fix: (a) TorManager queries GETINFO net/listeners/socks right after control authentication — the listener is bound at daemon start — and fires the new socksListenerDiscovered signal so the application proxy is re-pointed during bootstrap, well before ready() (failure pre-PROGRESS-100 is soft; the >=100 call site stays authoritative); (b) BrowserApplication::warmTorProxy fires a sacrificial check.torproject.org/api/ip request on the tor profile so the stale read happens on a throwaway, with a 3s settle bound via torProxyWarmed; (c) WebView::torMustQueue keeps the post-ready hold until usable-circuit AND proxy-warm, still bounded by the existing warm-up window so a stuck probe can't strand a tab.  ARORA_TOR_DEBUG=1 now traces the full hold/retry state machine (gate verdicts with reason codes, queue/refuse/dispatch, arm/fire, LoadFailed decode) on stderr.  New verify harness: `--tor --tor-firstsearch-smoke` drives the user flow verbatim on the real daemon — cold window + immediate omnibox loadString AND a start-page window.location.href search on a second tab; PASS = both commit a real remote document, an "Error loading page" commit on either tab = FAIL (pending page is allowed and must auto-resolve; 240s deadline dumps hold state).  Run: ARORA_TOR_BINARY=<staged bundle> needed under the smoke-store isolation (or BuildProcess/fetch-tor.sh in the real data dir).  Verified: smoke PASS x3 cold runs (each leg held through bootstrap, dispatched on first usable circuit at ~ready+440ms, both tabs loaded duckduckgo.com); tst_tormanager 22/22+1skip(real-daemon env leg), tst_webview 29/29+1skip (tor legs incl.), tst_webpage 72/73 (webChannelAddSearchProviderConsent modal-timing flake — passes isolated, unrelated path), tst_browserapp 11/11, tst_torinterceptor 9/9; gates engine-audit + supplychain + rustcoverage PASS; --quit-after-load rc=0.  Not run: check-warnings cold no-rust rebuild + full autotests/runTests.sh (~150 binaries, 900s/binary ceiling) — don't fit the per-run 3600s budget alongside the qmake-regen rebuild; incremental -Wall -Wextra build emitted 0 warnings in touched files.  TOR15/TOR16 stay blocked-dormant — the trace showed no retry re-capture, their contingency did not materialize.  Also landed in this run's tree: the interrupted PROF03 attempt's work (ProfileMenuActions backend + tst_profilemenu + --profile-wipe-*-smoke) with fixes — arm-smoke flag missing from internalOptions (parser would reject the child), modal legs used acceptModal() which never maps to QMessageBox::Yes, a comment-only QtWebEngine mention tripped engine-audit; verified tst_profilemenu 14/14 + --profile-wipe-smoke e2e PASS (seed→arm→boot-wipe→fresh stores).

### MPROF01

Multi-profile foundation — user approved extending the single profile to multi-profile (Firefox profiles / Chrome personas model: each profile = isolated settings + history + bookmarks + cache + cookies; windows bind to one profile). This task is PLUMBING ONLY — no behavior change for the user yet, but it must not lose or double-migrate anything.

Build: (a) ProfileContext — a tiny value type (id string + display name + avatar file name) with a registry: `BrowserPaths::dataFilePath()` gains a profile-scoped sibling (e.g. `profileDataFilePath(profileId, name)` / `profileDataDir(id)`); introduce the directory layout `profiles/<id>/` under the existing AppDataLocation. The global `dataFilePath()` stays as the "default profile" alias initially — MPROF02+ migrate call sites. (b) PROFILE REGISTRY — a `profiles.json` (or QSettings INI) at the data root listing profiles: id, name, avatar, createdAt; id = slug, immutable. Always contains at least `default`. Single source of truth — the PROF01 ProfileIdentity singleton delegates name/avatar to the active profile's registry entry (keep its public API stable so PROF01/02's UI doesn't rework). (c) MIGRATION — on first boot with this code: if `profiles/` doesn't exist, create `profiles/default/` and MOVE the existing top-level profile-owned payloads (history.db, history icons/, bookmarks.xbel, autofill.dat, containers/, adblock_subscription_*, extensions data, userscripts/, securestore blobs, servo/, pdf-view/, tor/ runtime state if profile-scoped — audit every dataFilePath call site and classify profile-owned vs app-global; machine-global things like tranco/blocklist caches stay top-level). Move = rename, never copy-then-delete-in-place; write a `migration-v1.done` marker; if the move fails partway, fail LOUDLY (status/log) rather than split state. The QSettings file is NOT moved here — MPROF04 handles it. (d) Every existing call site keeps working — dataFilePath() must resolve to profiles/default/ paths post-migration (either via a redirect inside dataFilePath or by updating call sites — pick ONE mechanism, document it). Verify: autotest legs — fresh run creates profiles/default + registry; run over a seeded pre-migration dir moves the payloads and history/bookmarks survive; double-run is a no-op; make check PASS. Tests: REQUIRED — add a dedicated autotest binary (tst_profilecontext / tst_profilemanagers / legs in the owning suites — suite choice is yours) asserting the spec's Verify items; smoke-only coverage does not count as done.

MPROF01 done-notes (2026-10-16):

- Mechanism chosen: redirect inside `dataFilePath()` — it resolves `<root>/profiles/<activeId>/<name>` and `activeProfileId()` is hard-coded `"default"` until MPROF03. `BrowserApplication::dataFilePath()` forwards, so every existing call site lands in the profile store untouched; `BrowserPaths::{dataRoot,globalDataFilePath,profileDataDir,profileDataFilePath,activeProfileId,isProfileId,ensureDataLayout}` in new `src/browserpaths.cpp`.
- Registry: `ProfileRegistry` (`src/profileregistry.{h,cpp}`) + `ProfileContext` value type (`src/profilecontext.h`) at `<root>/profiles.json` — {version:1, profiles:[{id,name,avatar,createdAt}]}; slug ids ([a-z0-9-_], ≤64) rejected otherwise; always materializes `default` on first read (one-shot name import from legacy `QSettings("profile/name")`), atomic via QSaveFile, `profileChanged(id)` signal. `ProfileRegistry::instance()` is qApp-parented; a `ProfileRegistry(filePath)` ctor serves tests.
- `ProfileIdentity` delegates name/avatar to `ProfileRegistry::instance()->activeProfile()`; public API unchanged (`name/setName/avatar/avatarPixmap/avatarSource/setAvatarImage/removeAvatar/...`); it connects `profileChanged` so MPROF05's manager UI repaints live. Avatar file resolves via `profileDataFilePath(id, avatar)`.
- Migration: `BrowserPaths::ensureDataLayout()` — called explicitly in `main()` right after the wipe/crash-reporter steps (before `BrowserApplication`, so before any manager resolves a path; also lazily once inside `dataFilePath`/`profileDataFilePath`). Explicit payload list + globs `adblock_subscription_*`/`*.migrated`; moves include `history.db-wal/-shm` (caught during live-dir audit — moving the db without its WAL would drop un-checkpointed history) and rustcore's files (`session/credentials/autofill-store.dat`, `sitedecisions/searchengines/urlstrip-rules.json`, `tranco-list.dat`, `blocklist-domains.txt` — the rc data dir anchors to `dataFilePath`'s parent, so these ride the profile; the two caches repopulate per profile, accepted trade-off vs a rust change). Resumable renames; destination collision = loud stop (qCritical + persistent `<root>/migration-v1.failed` report — no QApplication exists at migration time for a dialog); `migration-v1.done` written only on a clean pass; a stale `.failed` is removed on success.
- Stays at root (global): `crashreport.log`, `tor/` + `data/` + `docs/` (tor bundle), `locale/`, `QtWebEngine/` (Chromium namespaces by storageName), `arora-profile-wipe.pending`, `profiles.json`, the markers. Call sites switched to `globalDataFilePath()`: crash reporter, TorManager data dir, LanguageManager locale dir, wipe marker path.
- QSettings untouched per spec (MPROF04, rust lane, already done).
- Verify evidence: `tst_profilecontext` 10/10 (layout, fresh boot, seeded migration incl. WAL + .migrated + subscription globs, partial-move resume, double-run no-op, registry basics/id validation/persistence, profile isolation, identity delegation). Affected suites: profileavatar 17/17, profilemenu 14/14, browsermainwindow profileButton PASS, historymanager 49/49, autofill 9/9, securestore 17/17. Live-dir verification (`~/.local/share/Arora/Arora`): real boot migrated all 22 present payloads into `profiles/default/` byte-intact incl. the 4 MB WAL, globals stayed, `profiles.json` + marker written; second boot no-op, no `.failed`. Gates: engine-audit + supplychain + rustcoverage PASS (ENGINE.md gained a comment-kind row for browserpaths). Deferred: check-warnings + full `make check` autotest tree (~80 suites) — doesn't fit the run budget; QUAL06 precedent.
- `--quit-after-load` is a smoke flag: it isolates XDG into a temp store, so it exercises the migration against a throwaway root only — real-dir verification needs a bare launch (the layout pass is synchronous in `main()` before `BrowserApplication`).

### MPROF02

needs:task:MPROF01 (done — see its done-notes). Per-profile app singletons. Today HistoryManager, BookmarksManager, AutoFillManager, CookieJar (rules/keepPolicy), AdBlockManager (subscriptions), ContainerManager, ExtensionManager, DownloadManager history, NotesModel, SecureStore are global qApp singletons reading the global data dir. After MPROF01 the dirs are per-profile — now make the managers per-profile: (a) each singleton gains a profile-keyed instance map — `HistoryManager::instance()` → `instanceFor(profileId)` (keep instance() as the default-profile alias for unmigrated callers), instances qApp-parented, destroyed on profile delete (MPROF05). (b) Call sites that are profile-affine get the profile from their context: webpage → its QWebEngineProfile's profileId (add a mapping helper — e.g. BrowserProfile::profileIdFor(QWebEngineProfile*) keyed on the profile's storageName), TabWidget/BrowserMainWindow → window's profile (lands fully in MPROF03 — for now everything resolves to default). (c) Cross-profile contamination audit: private windows' OTR profile must write nothing to any profile store (already true for web state — verify app-side writes too); tor windows keep their own store set (document whether tor state is per-profile or global — recommend global, it's mode-level not persona-level). Verify: legs — two ProfileContexts write disjoint stores; history seeded under A invisible under B; per-profile instance reuse doesn't leak pointers across ids; make check PASS. Tests: REQUIRED — add a dedicated autotest binary (tst_profilecontext / tst_profilemanagers / legs in the owning suites — suite choice is yours) asserting the spec's Verify items; smoke-only coverage does not count as done.

Landed (resumed attempt: prior runs built the instance maps + tst_profilemanagers and were cut mid-verify; this run fixed one regression and ran the full gate). Mechanism: `BrowserProfile::profileIdFor(QWebEngineProfile*)` resolves a page's persona — the `aroraProfileId` dynamic property the factories tag wins (container profiles report their owner id), else the storageName is parsed ("arora"→default, "arora-<id>"→id; "arora-container-*" deliberately does NOT parse), unnamed/foreign/OTR→"default". New `BrowserProfile::namedProfile(id)` lazily creates qApp-owned "arora-<id>" profiles — the minimal seam MPROF03's window binding extends. Every persona store gained `instanceFor(profileId)` (id normalized via BrowserPaths::isProfileId, non-slug→default) with `instance()` aliasing the active profile: HistoryManager, BookmarksManager, AutoFillManager, CookieJar (instance(profile) resolves the persona jar via namedProfile; rules+keepPolicy under profiles/<id>/settings via ProfileSettings(m_profileId)), AdBlockManager, ContainerManager, ExtensionManager (statics userScriptsPath/updatesPath/updateCheckEnabled moved onto the instance), DownloadManager, NotesModel (instance(profile) keeps per-profile-object volatile models for OTR; persistent profiles share the persona's store; pre-split <data>/notes/<storageName>.json adopted by rename), SecureStore (per-profile seal/open/sealString/openString/passphraseProtectionEnabled/isUnlocked/unlock/lock/ensureUnlocked — lock() clears all personas, lock(id) one). Process-global rust stores are re-anchored per operation: HistoryManager::ensureRustStore calls rc_hist_open(profile's history.db) before every rc_hist_* entry point (no-op path compare when already bound); rustCoreEnsureDataDirFor(id) drops the crate's cached keys/maps on switch — callers anchor per batch, never per page load. SiteDecisionStore split: no-id kinds pin the default profile's store (global trust/permission/http-allow/adblock-site decisions — MPROF03's "global" recommendation), per-profile overloads serve persona kinds (cookie per-host rules). Call-site audit: webpage.cpp (history feed/remove, container divert, adblock+autofill page scripts), webview.cpp (containerId, blockImage, addSelectionToNote), sitepanel/siteshield/locationbarsiteicon (page-persona adblock + icon stores), browserapplication::prepareProfile (per-persona download/adblock/extension install; tor+OTR resolve to default persona = mode-level, documented), historycompleter re-anchors suggest to the active profile. Contamination audit: OTR pages feed nothing (history gate on profile()->isOffTheRecord(), volatile notes/jar, no autofill capture); tor resolves to default persona's managers — mode-level not persona-level, as spec'd. Known MPROF03 follow-up noted inline: the container-UA sweep in webpage.cpp only covers the active persona's managers. Verify: tst_profilemanagers 15/15 (namedProfile tagging, profileIdFor fallbacks, per-id instance stability + instance() aliasing + non-slug fallback, path disjointness, history/bookmark/notes/securestore/site-decision isolation incl. cross-anchor leak checks against the real profiles/<id>/ files, OTR volatility); make check: check-warnings/supplychain/engine-audit/rustcoverage all PASS, suite green except documented clean-HEAD flake fileSchemeErrors (PASS standalone); fixed regression: tst_WebView::contextMenuSelectionToNote now binds the view to the app profile (notes route to the page's persona — a bare WebView's page is on the OTR defaultProfile so the note went to the volatile model; production windows always construct WebView(profile)).

Recommend global — trust verdicts aren't persona data; document). MPROF03 landed (3rd attempt — attempt 1 built the plumbing + suite then hit the tree-mem watchdog mid-check; attempt 2's detached `make check` caught 3 warnings, fixed them, extended session.rs, and was killed at the 3600s cap mid-sweep; this run finished the sweep + verification). Mechanism: `BrowserMainWindow::m_profileId` (ctor param, non-slug→default) pushes `BrowserPaths::setActiveProfileId` at construction and on every activation (dtor re-anchors to the next focused window) — ambient resolution binds context-less `ProfileSettings`/`instance()` calls to the focused window's persona; `BrowserProfile::profileFor(id)` is the persona factory (storage "arora-<id>", "default"→normalProfile, aroraProfileId tag, deferred-storage sweep on first create) and `createdPersonaProfiles()` backs broadcast sweeps — `WebPage::setUserAgent` now sweeps every persona's live container profiles via `ProfileRegistry::profiles()` (resolves the MPROF02 follow-up). `BrowserApplication::webEngineProfileFor(profileId)` is the window-bound resolver — tor→dedicated tor profile, private→one shared unnamed OTR overlay serving every persona (documented decision: OTR is memory-only and app-side stores gate on isOffTheRecord, so per-id OTR clones add nothing); `newMainWindow(profileId)` / `newMainWindowInContainer(containerId, profileId)` are the MPROF05-ready entry points; File>New Window and link-spawned windows inherit the opener's persona. ProfileManagerDialog openWindow/openWindowCount and ProfileButton/ProfileIdentity (`instanceFor`) bind real personas. Session: rust `session.rs` bin v2 appends a per-window profile id (v1 blobs still decode as default; JSON manifest carries optional `"profile"` — default windows stay key-free), the legacy QSettings stream bumped v2→v3 likewise; both anchor the default persona's store; restore binds each window and unknown/deleted ids fall back to default honestly. Verified: tst_profilewindow 12/12 covering every spec leg (factory, bind, unregistered fallback, ambient-follows-window, child-tab-beats-ambient, two-window coexistence, history under bound profile only, shared-OTR writes nothing, session round-trip bindings, deleted-profile fallback); check-warnings PASS (resumed synced tree — every changed TU warning-free; earlier full pass had only the 3 now-fixed diags), check-supplychain/engine-audit/rustcoverage PASS; suites green: browsermainwindow 76/76 ×2 consecutive, tabwidget 35/35, webview 29/29, webpage 73/73, privatebrowsing 11/11, cookiejar 51/51, historymanager 49/49, containermanager 34/34, settingsdialog 56/56, profilemenu 14/14, notesmodel 10/10, securestore 17/17, sitedecisions 8/8, profilemanagers 15/15, profilemanager 8/8, profilecontext 10/10, browserapp 11/11, bookmarksmodel 12/12; `./arora --quit-after-load` exit 0 offscreen. ### MPROF06

Integration + e2e — run only after MPROF01-05 (and PROF01-04 for the menu surface) have all merged: check `git log master --grep=MPROF`. (a) ISOLATION E2E: two profiles live — history/bookmarks/cookies/settings under A invisible from B; a site logged-in on A is logged-out on B; avatar/name independent; containers per profile. (b) MIGRATION E2E: seeded pre-MPROF data dir (history + bookmarks + settings + avatar) → upgrade → everything intact under default; marker set; second boot no-op. (c) REGRESSION SWEEP: single-profile users must see zero change — the whole autotest suite is the gate; specifically the clear-data actions, Delete Profile (still nukes everything incl. all profiles), private windows, tor windows, session restore. (d) Honest-docs leg: README/ChangeLog describe the model (per-profile stores, global trust/site-decisions if that choice held, no sync). Verify: legs as above + full make check PASS + --quit-after-load rc=0 on both profiles. Tests: REQUIRED — add a dedicated autotest binary (tst_profilecontext / tst_profilemanagers / legs in the owning suites — suite choice is yours) asserting the spec's Verify items; smoke-only coverage does not count as done.



### VSEC01

Security audit of the merged multi-profile + vault stack — user-requested, run only after MPROF01-06 + PVAULT01-04 (+ PROF01-05) are all on master. Read the code as an attacker; do not re-run feature tests as a substitute for adversarial review. Findings → fix in-place where small, else file a task row; every accepted risk gets a documented rationale.

Audit surface:

(a) **Profile isolation** — can a page or window under profile A read/write B's stores? Check every `instanceFor(profileId)` caller can't be tricked into cross-profile resolution (untrusted `profileId` strings reaching `BrowserPaths`/`QWebEngineProfile::setStorageName` — validate id charset `[a-z0-9_-]`, reject `..`/path separators at the registry boundary). OTR/private profiles provably write nothing persistent. Session manifest can't inject an arbitrary profile id.

(b) **Vault crypto usage** — verify against the PVAULT01 spec: fresh salt per seal, nonce = salt_prefix‖chunk_index (no reuse across re-seals), two-pass unseal (MAC before any plaintext), staging+atomic rename, `Zeroizing` on all key material, KEK-unwrap vs ciphertext-MAC error split correct. Threefish path: XEX tweak derivation sane, HMAC-Skein-512 keyed correctly, algo_id can't be downgraded by header tamper (algo inside header-MAC coverage). rv_* FFI: length validation, no panics on malformed input, traversal refusal on `..`/absolute/symlink entries in the tar.

(c) **Plaintext lifecycle** — seal-time secure erase covers every file the pack read (no skipped dotfiles, no fd leaks leaving plaintext), `cache/` exclusion is honest (document what stays plaintext — favicons cache? downloads dir?), dirty-boot detection can't be bypassed by a marker-deleting attacker (it's a UX aid not a security boundary — document that), unlocked-window-while-sealed impossible.

(d) **Migration + registry** — MPROF01 move-migration can't be tricked into moving attacker-planted paths (symlink following), registry writes atomic, profiles.json corruption → fail-safe not fail-open, `default` undeletable enforced at the manager layer not just UI.

(e) **UI/FFI boundary** — password lifetime in Qt: field cleared on dialog close, no QString copies living longer than needed (document residual `QString` heap copies honestly — Qt has no guaranteed zeroize), wrong-password error doesn't leak timing/oracle info, auto-lock timer can't be starved by event-loop pressure.

(f) **Supply chain** — the new vault crates (threefish/skein/hmac/tar/sha2-0.11) already passed the supplychain gate; re-verify pinned versions + the documented unaudited-upstream caveat still holds.

Method: read, don't trust the specs — the code is the truth. Write findings to `.devin/security/VSEC01-report.md` (new dir if absent — follow any existing audit-report convention from SEC*/QUAL* tasks). Verify: report committed, fixes landed or tasked, make check PASS. Tests: add regression legs only where a real bug was found and fixed.


### VSEC02

VSEC01 finding F2 — the vault seals the app-side store tree (`profiles/<id>/`) but the engine's own state lives outside it. `BrowserProfile::profileFor(id)` creates named `QWebEngineProfile("arora-<id>")` objects with no `setPersistentStoragePath()`/`setCachePath()` override, so cookies, LocalStorage, IndexedDB, service workers, HTTP disk cache and visited-link state persist under `QtWebEngine/arora[-<id>]/` at the data root. A "locked" persona therefore still leaves every Chromium-side record on disk in cleartext — the locked promise currently covers app-side stores only.

Build: (a) For every named persona profile, `setPersistentStoragePath(<profiles/<id>/QtWebEngine>)` (and decide `setCachePath` — either under `cache/` so the existing cache-hold exclusion applies honestly, or inside the sealed tree; document the choice) BEFORE the profile's first use — Qt requires the call before any page/storage usage or the profile keeps the default paths. `normalProfile`/`defaultProfile` (id "default"), private/OTR (unnamed → memory-only, unaffected), tor, and container profiles each need a deliberate treatment — container profiles are per-container QWebEngineProfiles owned by a persona, decide whether they inherit the persona's sealed path. (b) MIGRATION — existing on-disk `QtWebEngine/arora[-<id>]/` dirs must move into their new locations once, with the same symlink-refusal discipline as MPROF01's payload migration (VSEC01 F1): lstat-check, never follow, fail loudly on a planted link. The default persona's existing `QtWebEngine/arora/` tree carries every user's current cookie/session state — a wrong path here silently logs everyone out, so verify cookie/session survival explicitly. (c) SEAL COVERAGE — after relocation, `rv_seal`'s packed tree must include the engine subdir so a locked persona leaves zero Chromium-side plaintext; verify `rv_inspect`/`rv_verify` and the lock-marker's coverage claim are honest about it (and that `ClearPrivateData`'s persistentStoragePath wipes still hit the right dir). (d) Watch the ordering hazards: a profile whose path was already used can't be re-pointed (check for `QtWebEngine/` dirs materialized at the OLD location after a boot on the new code and assert none appear); `defaultProfile()` may be touched by Qt internals before `profileFor` runs — audit first-use ordering. Verify: tst_profilee2e legs — persona's engine cookies/storage land under profiles/<id>/QtWebEngine (not at the data root); seeded pre-change engine dir migrates and a session cookie survives; seal+lock leaves no `QtWebEngine/` plaintext at the data root while cache (if excluded) is honestly plaintext; OTR writes nothing anywhere; make check PASS.

Result: (a) Every named persona profile now binds `persistentStoragePath` to `profiles/<id>/QtWebEngine` and `cachePath` to `profiles/<id>/cache/QtWebEngine` (the existing cache-hold exclusion rides it honestly — disposable plaintext, documented in README/profilevault.h). Private/OTR and Tor profiles stay unnamed memory-only; container profiles keep storage under `profiles/<persona>/containers/<cid>/` and now keep their cache under the owning persona's `cache/containers/<cid>` tree so the sealed-set claim stays honest. Paths are bound via `QWebEngineProfileBuilder` BEFORE `createProfile()` — see the upstream bug note below. (b) Migration: `BrowserPaths::ensureEngineStorageLayout()` sweeps legacy data-root `QtWebEngine/arora[-<id>]/` and old cache-root `QtWebEngine` dirs once (marker `migration-v2.done`, failures under `migration-v2.failed`), with lstat/symlink refusal and both-sides-collision abort; sealed personas park their legacy tree under `<id>.vault/engine-hold/QtWebEngine` and `ProfileVault::unseal` restores it (a failed restore keeps the hold for retry — fixed an unconditional delete that would have dropped the only copy). Empty Qt-materialized residue at the old path is rmdir'd, never treated as a collision. The default persona's `QtWebEngine/arora` tree migrates with cookies/session state intact (profilee2e leg seeds a session cookie pre-move and reads it post-move). (c) Seal coverage: `rv_seal`'s packed tree includes `QtWebEngine/`; vault.conf records `engine_storage=QtWebEngine` + `engine_cache=cache/QtWebEngine` so `rv_inspect`/`rv_verify` and the lock-marker claim are honest; `ClearPrivateData`'s persistentStoragePath wipes hit the relocated dir. `main()` runs the migration before any profile is materialized, and a lazy `ensureEngineStorageOnce()` guard in the profile factories covers test harnesses and other entry points that bypass `main()`. (d) No `QtWebEngine/` dir materializes at the old data root after a boot (asserted in profilee2e). UPSTREAM BUG FOUND + WORKED AROUND: on Qt 6.12, calling `setPersistentStoragePath()` on an already-constructed named profile runs `ProfileAdapter::setDataPath()` → `setupPrefService(recreation=true)` → recreates `ExtensionPrefs` while `ExtensionRegistrar` still holds the stale `extension_prefs_` pointer — the next `setExtensionEnabled()` segfaults on a `nullptr+0x10` member read (reproduced in a minimal probe: named profile + post-construction setPersistentStoragePath + install + enable = deterministic SIGSEGV in libQt6WebEngineCore). `QWebEngineProfileBuilder` sets the paths before the profile's PrefService is built — install+enable then work cleanly. All named-profile construction sites (`BrowserProfile::normalProfile`/`profileFor`, `ContainerManager::profileFor`, `WebEngineBackend::createProfile`) now ride the builder; the constraint is documented in `.devin/ENGINE.md`. Verify: tst_profilee2e 21/21 (incl. migration, placement, vault-coverage, OTR/Tor no-write and no-root-residue legs); tst_extensionmanager 27/27; tst_containermanager 34/34; tst_browsermainwindow extensionActionButtons PASS (was the segfault site); make check — all 4 gates PASS + 94/94 test binaries 0 failed.

### AUDIT02

Full-tree audit re-run over current master — the codebase has changed massively since STAT01/AUDIT01 (multi-profile stack MPROF01-06, vault PVAULT01-04 + VSEC02 engine-storage relocation, update checker UPD01, dual-mode installer). Three passes:

**1. Static analysis re-sweep** — rerun `.devin/check-static.sh` (gcc -fanalyzer full build → clang --analyze per-TU, resumable via ARORA_STATIC_BUILD) and `.devin/check-tidy.sh` (clang-tidy bugprone-*/cert-*/clang-analyzer-*) against current master. The STAT01 triage file (.devin/STAT01-triage.md) only covers OLD findings — every NEW finding since then needs fresh triage: real bug → fix in place + regression leg; Qt-blindness FP → extend triage doc with justification. Pay special attention to the new code: profilevault/vaultengine FFI boundary (buffer ownership across rv_* calls, Zeroizing gaps), UpdateChecker/UpdateDialog (network input → JSON parse → asset matching, no unvalidated URL open), VSEC02's ensureEngineStorageLayout migration (the lstat/symlink paths), ProfileSettings multi-profile routing.

**2. Dead-code check** — find dead code introduced by the profile/vault refactor: unused functions, unreachable branches, superseded helpers kept "just in case", stale settings keys no longer read, dead menu actions. Tools: compiler -Wunused coverage is already in check-warnings for same-TU deadness; for cross-TU use nm/objdump on the built binaries to find defined-but-never-referenced non-virtual methods, grep for single-caller candidates in the new modules, and audit any `#if 0`/commented blocks. Remove confirmed dead code; report borderline cases (Qt slots reachable only via connect(), FFI exports) as kept-with-reason.

**3. Security re-check delta** — VSEC01 + VSEC02 covered the vault/profile stack, but re-verify the DELTA: (a) UpdateChecker — the first user-facing network feature: is the response strictly JSON-parsed with size caps, does the download path inherit proxy/policy (a download asset fetch in a tor window must not go direct — UPD01 refuses the CHECK on tor, verify the download button also can't be invoked there), can a malicious release payload inject an arbitrary URL into the download or a javascript:/file: link into the dialog (link sanitization before openUrl); (b) VSEC02 engine-hold — the unseal restore path (failed-retry retention) — verify no TOCTOU between hold-check and restore, and engine-hold can't be pointed outside profiles/<id>; (c) dual-mode NSIS — per-user install writes SHCTX→HKCU uninstall keys, verify the installer can't be tricked into partial-context state (installed all-users, uninstalled just-me or vice versa); (d) any NEW network/parsing surface added since VSEC01. Report in .devin/security/AUDIT02-report.md.

Verify: check-static + check-tidy run end-to-end on current master, every new finding triaged (fix/suppress), dead-code removal compiles clean + check-warnings stays 0, security delta findings fixed or documented, make check PASS, committed report.
